- **Why**: FOR-43 asks for the same check on every interface; a view was the one place that read levels alone. An action-level override cannot be expressed through a level, so the page needs the answer per action, computed where the overrides are.
- **Rejected**: one `can()` that takes a level or an action name (ambiguous); client-side evaluation of overrides (the page would need the manifest and the matrix); a separate table for views (a second place to edit and audit).
- **Not done**: `serveView` is not yet called by the gateway (views are served by the tool's Worker, FOR-56): when it is, it must pass `registry.accessFor(tool, person.roles)`; an override left behind by a view a later version removes is ignored, as for actions.
- **Applied in**: FOR-43; `packages/manifest/src/access.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/views/serve.ts`, `playground/serve.ts`, `packages/registry`, `packages/sdk/src/react`.

## D-047 — The console saves the matrix in one request, applied with its audit lines in a single D1 batch

- **Date**: 03/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: `PUT /console-api/api/tools/<tool>/access-matrix` takes `{ changes: [{ role, action, allowed: true | false | null }] }` (1 to 500 cells; the single-cell body of D-040 is replaced) and answers with the matrix. `registry.setRoleAccessMany` checks every cell against the manifest first, then writes the cells and one audit line per cell in one `d1.batch`: a bad cell changes nothing. A cell named twice counts once, the last. The console sends the whole draft in one request.
- **Why**: a request that fails part-way left some cells applied (D-040's "not done"); D1 batches are transactions, so the audit lines and the writes cannot disagree.
- **Rejected**: keeping the single-cell body next to the batch one (two shapes to keep right, and nothing else calls it).
- **Applied in**: FOR-43; `apps/gateway/src/console-api/access-matrix.ts`, `packages/registry` (`setRoleAccessMany`), `apps/console/src/access-matrix.tsx`.

## D-046 — A delegated connector gets the caller's own access token from the gateway, or the consent link as a 428

- **Date**: 03/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: on each call to a tool that declares a `delegated` connector, the gateway looks up the caller's consent for the tool, the environment the call is served from (preview, pilot or production) and the connector type. If there is one it hands the tool Worker the access token in the header `x-forgeron-connector-tokens` (a JSON map by connector name), after refreshing it with the sealed refresh token when it expires within a minute (the pattern of per-person secrets, D-036: headers only the gateway can set, dropped from the incoming request). The refresh token is never in a header, a listing or a log. If there is none (never given, revoked by an admin or the person, or the provider refuses the refresh token), the header `x-forgeron-connector-consent` carries the consent link by connector name (`/console-api/api/tools/<tool>/environments/<env>/consent/<name>`). A provider that cannot be reached is a `502 UPSTREAM` before the tool runs: asking the person to consent again would not help.
- **Runtime**: `Platform.connectorSessions` (`tokens(user)`, `consentUrl(name)`) gives a first-use check for the delegated connectors an action's tool declares: a missing consent is a `SECRET_REQUIRED` failure (HTTP 428, MCP URL elicitation `-32042` for protocol 2025-11-25, which already carries `inputUrl`) whose `details` has `consent: true`, `inputUrl` and the `missing` connectors. The compiled Worker builds the sessions from the two headers. No new error code: the person's next step is the same (open a link, then call again).
- **Audit**: one `access` audit line per token handed over (`connector-token:<type>`, actor the person, detail the environment and connector name), one per admin revocation (`connector-consent:<name>:revoke`), and one per change of the sign-in provider or of a connector's OAuth client (`identity-provider:set|delete`, `connector-client:<type>:set|delete`, with `secretChanged`, never a secret). Audit failures never block the call or the change.
- **Why**: the same shape as per-person secrets keeps one mechanism for what a person gives the platform, and keeps the refresh token (the durable credential) out of the tool Worker; the access token is short-lived and is what the connector needs to call the upstream.
- **Know**: the access token is in the tool Worker's memory during the call, so the tool's own code can read it; the connector package's host list (`createConnectorClient`) is what stops it from reaching other hosts, and a tool that reads `ctx` through a connector client does not see it. Every call to a tool with a delegated connector costs one registry read and, when the token is stale, one refresh call to the provider.
- **Not done / not verified**: nothing ran against a real provider. (The compiler import of connector packages, the 5xx handling of the token endpoint and the audit of grants and withdrawals, listed here at first, are D-048.)
- **Applied in**: FOR-79; `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/connector-tokens.ts`, `dispatch/user-secrets.ts`, `console-api/identity.ts`, `console-api/connections.ts`; `packages/runtime/src/engine.ts`; `packages/compiler/src/build.ts`.

## D-048 — The build bundles the connector packages a tool declares; only an explicit OAuth refusal revokes a consent; grants are audited

- **Date**: 03/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: (1) `forgeron build` resolves `@forgeron/connector-<type>` from the tool's folder for each connector in `resources.connectors` and bundles its **default export** (the `defineConnector(...)` definition; every connector package has one) into the Worker. The Worker gives the runtime `platform.connectorClient`, which is the SDK's `createConnectorClient(definition, declaration, session)`: `ctx.connectors.<name>` is built per call from the session the gateway headers make (D-046). A declared connector whose package is not installed is a build error naming the package. (2) `createConsent` revokes a consent only when the token endpoint answers 4xx with `invalid_grant`, `invalid_client` or `unauthorized_client` (RFC 6749 section 5.2). A 5xx, a dropped connection, an answer that is not OAuth JSON, a 429, or another error (`invalid_request`, `invalid_scope`) throws `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` (a network error is thrown as it is) and keeps the consent. The gateway answers `502 UPSTREAM` with `retryable: true` and `Retry-After: 30` before the tool runs, and `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` on the consent callback is a `502` too. (3) A grant (`connector-consent:<type>:grant`, after the provider's code is swapped and the tokens kept) and a withdrawal by the person (`connector-consent:<type>:withdraw`) write an `access` audit line each (actor the person, detail the environment, and for a withdrawal the connector name); never a token. Audit failures never block the person.
- **Why**: a provider outage that answers instead of dropping the connection must not cost people their consent, which they would have to give again one by one; and the audit log must say who gave or withdrew access, as it does for who used it. A default export gives the compiler one name to import whatever the package's own export is called.
- **Know**: only `delegated` connectors get a client in the Worker today: the runtime builds `ctx.connectors.<name>` from the caller's token, and nothing gives a tool the `application` token yet. The declaration is checked against the definition (type, mode, scopes) when the client is built, so a bad scope is a `FORBIDDEN` on first use, not yet a build error. A tool's package manager must install the connector packages (they are `@forgeron/*` workspace packages today).
- **Not done / not verified**: `application` mode; checking the declaration at build; an audit line when the platform itself revokes a consent (the provider refuses the refresh token); nothing ran against a real provider.
- **Applied in**: FOR-125; `packages/compiler/src/build.ts`, `packages/registry/src/consent.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/consent/consent.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/connector-tokens.ts`.

## D-049 — The gateway signs people in by a code sent to their email, beside OIDC; members, invitations and email domains are managed in the console

- **Date**: 03/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Flow.** `GET /auth/login` is the sign-in page. `POST /auth/email/request {email}` answers one page whether or not the address may sign in, and sets `__Host-fg_email` (sealed, `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict`, 10 minutes) carrying the id of the request. `POST /auth/email/verify {code}` weighs the code, then opens the same `__Host-fg_session` as the OIDC callback and redirects to `/` or to the CLI's own `/auth/cli?…` page (`next`, the only path allowed, checked at the request and again at the verification). A new session is minted at each sign-in; no incoming cookie is reused (no fixation). `forgeron login` is unchanged: `/auth/cli` sends an unauthenticated browser to `/auth/login?next=…`.
  2. **The code.** 8 digits from `crypto.getRandomValues` (rejection sampling), shown as `1234 5678`, valid 10 minutes, single use. The platform D1 keeps `HMAC-SHA256(AUTH_SECRET, "code:<id>:<code>")` only (`email_codes`, migration 0016), compared in constant time. Five tries per code, counted by the same statement that reads the row (`UPDATE … RETURNING`), so parallel guesses cannot exceed five; the code is spent by a second conditional `UPDATE`, so it opens one door once. The code is bound to the browser that asked by the cookie: no cookie, or another request's cookie, is refused.
  3. **No link.** The mail carries the code to type, not a link: a mail scanner that opens every link would consume or confirm it. (A link with a POST confirmation page was the alternative; a typed code needs no second page and cannot be prefetched.)
  4. **Anti-enumeration.** The answer, its headers and its cookie are the same for an address that may sign in and one that may not; a row is stored either way; the mail is sent after the answer (`ctx.waitUntil`, `createGateway`'s `defer`) so latency does not differ; a failed send is logged without the address and not shown. Nothing is mailed to an address that is neither a member, invited, `--admin-email`, nor of an allowed domain. A wrong code for an address that cannot sign in fails like a wrong code for one that can. The limits count every request alike (5 per address and 20 per source address per hour, the source being `cf-connecting-ip` hashed with the key), so a `429` says nothing about the account.
  5. **Who may sign in** (`admission`, decided again at verification): an existing member (found by email, case-insensitively); else an unexpired invitation (roles as invited); else `ADMIN_EMAIL` (role `admin`: the first administrator); else an address whose domain is an allowed domain exactly (no subdomain, no suffix) with the default role. Joining writes the member (subject `email:<address>`, or the provider's subject for OIDC), its audit line and the invitation's deletion in one batch.
  6. **Members, invitations, domains** (`GET /api/members`, `PATCH|DELETE /api/members/<id>`, `POST /api/invitations`, `DELETE /api/invitations/<email>`, `PUT /api/membership-settings`, console API, admins only, JSON only). Every change is an `access` audit line written in the same batch as the change (`member:invite|revoke-invitation|roles|remove|join|domains`). The last administrator cannot be demoted or removed. `admin` cannot be the default role (anyone with a domain email would be an administrator). Allowed domains are those an admin saved, else `--member-email-domains`; the same policy (domains, default role, invitations) now applies to OIDC sign-ins too. An invitation lasts 14 days and is announced by a mail (best effort) that carries no code.
  7. **One member per person.** An OIDC sign-in whose email already belongs to a member (who came by email first, or the reverse) is that member; before this, a second member was created.
  8. **Mail** is the `MailSender` interface (`apps/gateway/src/mail`): Cloudflare Email Sending through a `send_email` binding `SEND_EMAIL` and `MAIL_FROM` (`env.SEND_EMAIL.send({from, to, subject, text})`), an in-memory sender for tests. Source: Cloudflare documentation, Email Service, "Email Sending Workers API", "Send bindings" and "REST API" (pages dated 15 and 30 October 2026, read 3 November 2026): Email Sending is in public beta (since 16 April 2026), needs Workers Paid and a domain onboarded to Email Service on Cloudflare DNS; the Workers binding needs no token, the REST API (`POST /accounts/{id}/email/sending/send`) needs an API token with send permission. The binding was chosen: no new secret.
  9. **Install.** `platform install --sign-in email[,oidc] --mail-from <address>` sets `SIGN_IN_METHODS`, `MAIL_FROM` and the `send_email` binding `SEND_EMAIL`; `--admin-email` and `--member-email-domains` are as before; the OIDC flags are required only when `oidc` is in `--sign-in` (their absence is the old behaviour). The flag is `--sign-in` and not `--auth` because `--auth` already chooses the Cloudflare credential (`token|wrangler`, D-026). Without `SIGN_IN_METHODS` a gateway behaves as before (OIDC alone); the email routes answer 404 unless `email` is named and the sender is configured. When both are on, `/auth/login` shows the OIDC button ("Continue with your company account") first and the email form second; with OIDC alone it still redirects to the provider; `?method=oidc` always starts the provider.
- **Why**: a small team or a first self-host install has no identity provider, and the first administrator must be able to sign in to configure one. The code is hashed so a read of the D1 gives no way in; the answer is uniform so the form cannot be used to list who works there.
- **Rejected**: a link in the mail (scanner prefetch); a password; creating an account for any address (spam, and nothing stops a stranger from owning a member row); revealing "no such account" or "too many requests for this address only when it exists"; making the first person to sign in the administrator (D-027).
- **Know**: a person can be locked out of asking for a code for an hour by someone who asks five times for their address (the limit is per address; the code itself is unaffected). The sign-in secrets are the mailbox: whoever reads the mailbox of an administrator is that administrator. A removed member who is in an allowed domain joins again at their next sign-in; remove the domain to keep them out. `ADMIN_EMAIL` re-creates the administrator at sign-in if their member row was removed (D-027). Removing a member stops their sessions and tokens at the next call (every call reads the member), not a revocation list. Timing: the lookups and the stored row are the same for every address and only the send differs, which is deferred; a residual difference (scheduling the send) was not measured.
- **Not verified**: no real Cloudflare Email Sending domain, no real mailbox, no real browser. The shape `send_email` of the Workers API binding metadata (`{ type: "send_email", name }`) and that `inherit` carries it across a re-run were not checked against a live account; `from` is a bare address (the documentation shows a bare address; a named sender was not tried). Delivery, spam placement and DKIM of the sender domain are the owner's to check.
- **Applied in**: FOR-126; `apps/gateway/src/identity/email-auth.ts`, `mail/mail.ts`, `console-api/members.ts`, `gateway.ts`; `packages/registry` (`0016_email_sign_in`, `email-codes.ts`, members methods); `apps/console/src/members.tsx`; `apps/cli/src/commands/gateway-identity.ts`; `docs/self-host.md`.

## D-050 — SAML sign-in is verified by a small XML-DSig verifier on Web Crypto, trusting only the element a pinned signature covers

- **Date**: 03/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · replaces D-041 point 5
- **Decision**:
  1. A workspace can sign in through SAML 2.0, service-provider initiated. `GET /auth/login` records a request id and redirects to the identity provider's SSO URL with a deflated `AuthnRequest` (HTTP-Redirect binding, unsigned) and a `RelayState`; the identity provider posts the response to `POST /auth/saml/acs` (HTTP-POST binding); `GET /auth/saml/metadata` serves our metadata (entity id = that URL) for the identity provider's administrator. The session is the same `__Host-fg_session` cookie (`HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `Lax`) as OIDC, opened with a 303. IdP-initiated sign-in is **refused**: a response must answer a request this gateway made.
  2. The provider is a row of `saml_providers` (migration 0016): the IdP entity id, the SSO URL (https), one to five pinned PEM certificates (more than one lets the provider rotate), `emailAttribute`, `groupsAttribute`, `nameAttribute` (an attribute `Name` or `FriendlyName`) and `groupRoles`. It is edited by an admin through the same `PUT|GET|DELETE /console-api/api/identity-provider` with `kind: "saml"`, audited as `identity-provider:set` (the audit line carries the entity id and the number of certificates, never a certificate). Each certificate is parsed when saved. One provider per workspace: saving a SAML provider removes an OIDC one and the other way round; a saved SAML provider wins over `OIDC_*` bindings. Without `emailAttribute` the email is the NameID, which must contain `@`; the member subject is `saml:<NameID>` (never equal to an OIDC subject); a transient NameID is refused. Groups and `groupRoles` work exactly as for OIDC (D-041 point 3).
  3. **No library.** Verifying XML-DSig needs exclusive canonicalization, and every vetted implementation found (xml-crypto, xmldsigjs, samlify, node-saml) needs `node:*` or a DOM, which the neutral bundle forbids (D-028). The verifier is written here, deliberately narrow, in four files of `apps/gateway/src/identity`: `saml-xml.ts` (reader and exclusive C14N), `saml-cert.ts` (public key of a pinned certificate), `saml-signature.ts` (the signature), `saml.ts` (request, response, assertion). The narrowness is what makes it small enough to audit.
  4. **The reader** refuses a DOCTYPE and any entity other than the five predefined ones and character references, a processing instruction inside an element, an undeclared prefix, a duplicate attribute, more than 64 levels, 20 000 elements or 1 MB. No entity is ever expanded, so there is no XXE or entity bomb. Comments are dropped and the text around them is one string, the same one the digest was computed over (no comment-splitting trick on the NameID).
  5. **The signature**: the key is a pinned certificate (`KeyInfo` in the message is never read; the certificate's own signature, issuer and validity are not checked: trust is the pinning, and an IdP often signs past its stated expiry; RSA keys under 2048 bits are refused). Allowed: exclusive C14N without comments (an `InclusiveNamespaces` PrefixList is honoured), RSA-SHA256 or ECDSA P-256 with SHA-256 (the raw `r||s` that XML-DSig and Web Crypto share), SHA-256 digests, exactly one `Reference` whose `URI` is `#<ID of the element the Signature is a child of>`, transforms exactly enveloped-signature then exclusive C14N. Anything else (SHA-1, HMAC, inclusive C14N, XPath, other transforms) is refused, which closes algorithm confusion.
  6. **Signature wrapping (XSW)**: the response must have exactly one `Assertion` child and no `EncryptedAssertion`; only a `Signature` that is a direct child of the Response or of that Assertion counts, and every one present must verify; at least one is required; no `ID` (`ID`, `Id`, `id`) may appear twice in the document; and the assertion that is read is the node object the signature was verified over, never one found by id. A signed assertion moved into `Extensions`, a signature copied into a forged assertion, a forged assertion with a reused id, and a second assertion beside a signed Response are all refused (tested). When only the Assertion is signed, the unsigned Response attributes are never relied on.
  7. **The assertion**, read from the covered element only: `Version` 2.0; `Issuer` equals the pinned entity id (and the Response's if the Response is signed); at least one bearer `SubjectConfirmationData` with `Recipient` equal to our ACS URL, `InResponseTo` equal to our request id and an unexpired `NotOnOrAfter`; `Conditions` with `NotBefore`/`NotOnOrAfter` and every `AudienceRestriction` naming our entity id (an unknown condition is refused); an `AuthnStatement`; a Response that is signed must also have `Destination` equal to the ACS URL and a matching `InResponseTo`; `Status` must be success. Clock skew tolerated: 120 seconds.
  8. **Replay and binding**: the request id lives in `saml_requests` (kind `request`, 10 minutes) and in the sealed state cookie with the `RelayState`; the response must carry both. The request is used with one `DELETE … RETURNING` (two simultaneous answers: one wins), the assertion id is recorded with one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT … RETURNING` until its `NotOnOrAfter` plus the skew (a replayed assertion is refused). Both are consumed only after the signature and the assertion are valid, so a forged POST cannot burn a person's request. Expired rows are purged when a sign-in starts.
  9. The state cookie of a SAML sign-in is `__Host-fg_saml` with `SameSite=None; Secure; HttpOnly`: the identity provider answers with a cross-site POST, which a `Lax` cookie does not travel with. The session cookie stays `Lax`.
- **Why**: Okta, Entra ID and ADFS tenants of customers are often SAML-only by policy; OIDC cannot serve them. A hand-written verifier is only acceptable if it is narrow, strict about structure (the wrapping defence is structural, not a lookup by id) and proven against signatures made independently of its own canonicalizer.
- **Rejected**: a dependency (needs `node:*` or a DOM, or is far larger than the surface used); trusting `KeyInfo` or fetching the key from metadata at sign-in (trust must come from the admin); accepting any one of several signatures without checking the others; looking the signed element up by id; IdP-initiated sign-in (no request to bind the response to, so no replay window short of the assertion's validity); signed or encrypted AuthnRequests/assertions (not needed for a bearer assertion over TLS, can be added); SHA-1 and RSA-SHA1 (some old IdPs only offer them: they are refused); metadata import by URL (the admin pastes the certificates).
- **Know**: the tests sign real assertions with Web Crypto, RSA and EC, with documents written directly in canonical form, so the digest and the signature do not depend on the gateway's own canonicalizer; the canonicalizer is also tested alone. The tests are the evidence the verifier is correct; they do not replace a review by someone who writes SAML for a living. A person's NameID that changes at the provider (transient, or a rename) is a new member. `emailAttribute` is the name the IdP sends (Entra: `http://schemas.xmlsoap.org/ws/2005/05/identity/claims/emailaddress`; Okta and others: whatever the admin set). The groups of an Entra SAML assertion are group ids unless configured otherwise.
- **Not done / not verified**: no real SAML identity provider (Entra ID, Okta, ADFS, Google, Keycloak) was tried: the attribute names, signing algorithms, whether the IdP signs the Response, the Assertion or both, and whether it accepts an unsigned AuthnRequest and the HTTP-Redirect binding with a fresh `RelayState` come from the specification and from documentation, not from a live tenant; an ECDSA-signing IdP is rare and was proven against the simulation only; no single logout; no signed or encrypted requests; no metadata refresh or import; the console has no screen for it; the clock skew and the 10-minute request lifetime are starting values.
- **Applied in**: FOR-124; `apps/gateway/src/identity/saml*.ts`, `auth.ts`, `presets.ts`, `console-api/identity.ts`, `simulated-saml-idp.ts`; `packages/registry` (`0016_saml`, `identity-config.ts`, `saml-requests.ts`).

## D-051 — A console mail mode writes the sign-in codes to the Worker's logs for test installs; opt-in, never on the cloud

- **Date**: 03/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: `platform install --sign-in email --mail console` stores `MAIL_MODE=console`; no `send_email` binding and no `MAIL_FROM` are created or required (`--mail-from` with it is refused). The gateway then uses `consoleMailSender`: one `console.log` line of JSON per code (`{"event":"sign-in code","address":…,"code":…}`) and one per invitation (`{"event":"invitation","address":…,"roles":[…]}`), read with `wrangler tail` or the dashboard logs. Everything else of D-049 stands: the line is written only for an address that is a member, invited, `ADMIN_EMAIL` or of an allowed domain (a stranger gets the same HTTP answer and no line), the code is still stored hashed, single use and rate limited, and `ADMIN_EMAIL` signs in with the logged code. The default stays real mail. The sign-in pages carry one line saying it is test mode.
- **Guard**: the gateway refuses the mode, with an error in the logs and email sign-in off, when it serves the cloud (`DISPATCHER` bound, or `BASE_DOMAIN` is `forgeron.app` or under it); the install refuses `--mail console` for such a domain. Self-host is allowed.
- **Why**: a first install on a zone without Email Sending, or without the Workers Paid plan, still needs the administrator to get in.
- **Know**: anyone who can read the Worker's logs (account members, log sinks) can sign in as any allowed address, administrators included; the code is the only secret logged. Logs may be retained by a sink after the code has expired.
- **Not verified**: no real `wrangler tail` or dashboard log was read.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/mail/mail.ts`, `gateway.ts`; `apps/cli/src/commands/gateway-identity.ts`, `platform.ts`; `docs/self-host.md`.

## D-052 — The gateway uploads the console assets again with each new version of itself, from the platform D1

- **Date**: 03/11/2026 · **Status**: Active (corrects D-032)
- **Decision**: (1) `platform install` stores the console files (path, hash, size, type, base64 content, in chunks of 20,000 characters) in the platform D1 (`gateway_assets`, migration 0018, `packages/registry/src/gateway-assets.ts`), in the same step as `gateway_code`. (2) `createGatewayBindings` takes an `assets()` hook instead of `keepAssets`; with it, each version upload first runs `uploadAssets` (assets upload session with the manifest, buckets only for the files Cloudflare lacks, completion token) and carries `assets: { jwt, config }` and an `assets` binding `ASSETS` (declared, not inherited); `keep_assets` is never sent. `uploadAssets` and the routing `config` (`GATEWAY_ASSETS_CONFIG`) live in `packages/hosting/src/self-host/assets.ts`, shared by the CLI and the gateway; `readAssets` stays in the CLI. (3) The gateway wires the hook only when it has an `ASSETS` binding; with nothing stored it fails with "run `forgeron platform install` again". The assets are read only when a tool is bound. (4) `FakeCloudflare` refuses `keep_assets` with Cloudflare's own message and refuses a version upload without `assets.jwt` for a Worker that has assets.
- **Why**: the real API answered 400 to `keep_assets` on the versions endpoint, so no tool could be deployed to a gateway serving the console. Cloudflare may drop files nobody references, so the manifest alone is not enough: the contents are kept.
- **Rejected**: the manifest alone (an empty bucket list is only answered while Cloudflare still holds the files); fetching the files from the console build at deploy time (the gateway has no access to it); a Worker-side asset binding fetch (assets of the running version cannot be listed).
- **Not verified**: a real install; the size of a real console in D1 (rows are chunked, but the number of statements in the single batch of `platform install` is not tried against D1's limits); the `assets` binding declared by name in a version upload by the gateway.
- **Know**: a gateway installed before this change has no stored assets until `platform install --console <dir>` is run again.
- **Applied in**: FOR-127.

## D-053 — Every HTML page of the gateway shares one shell; `forgeron login` asks before it authorizes; an invitation opens through an opaque link

- **Date**: 03/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **One shell** (`apps/gateway/src/pages`). `renderPage` builds a whole document: the anvil and "forgeron", the workspace, one card (eyebrow, one `h1`, body), a footer. The stylesheet is generated from `assets/design-system/tokens.json` when the module loads (the source of `packages/ui/tokens/tokens.css`), so there is no second copy to keep in step. Forge (dark) is the default, Atelier (light) comes from `prefers-color-scheme`. The brand fonts are named first in the stacks and fall back to the system ones: no `@font-face`, no request. Embedding them was weighed and refused: the four woff2 files of `assets/design-system/fonts` are 165 to 184 KB each (about 0.9 MB, 1.2 MB as base64) and would ride on every page, and the CSP would need `font-src` (`data:` or `'self'` with a route to serve them), which D-049's CSP does not have. The logo is outlined paths, so the identity does not depend on a font; the titles fall back to Inter where installed, else the system sans. Zero rounding, `line` borders, 14px text, 32px controls, a 2px `focus` ring offset by 2px. At 600px and below the controls are 40px (a thumb), the action row stacks with the primary action first, and the page margins shrink. Color only for a state, always with a word (`Error`, `Warning`, `Done`).
  2. **The CSP is the one of D-049, unchanged** (`default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'`), now sent by every page. The pages have no script and no image: the logo is an inline SVG.
  3. **Errors.** `errorPage(status)` says what happened, then what to do. A request that accepts `text/html` (a navigation) gets the page for 400, 401, 403, 404, 405, 429, 500, 502; any other client keeps its JSON. The Worker's entry (`index.ts`) turns an uncaught failure into the 500 page or JSON, logging the error's name only. The runtime's `/secrets` page and the connector consent landing (`/console-api/api/consent/callback`) use the same shell: the runtime takes a renderer (`handleSecretsInput(…, render)`) and falls back to a bare document when it has none.
  4. **Sign-in.** The email step, the code step (one input, `inputmode="numeric"`, `autocomplete="one-time-code"`, `maxlength="12"` so `1234 5678` and `1234-5678` paste), a resend form, and the pages for `?reason=expired` and `?reason=signed-out` (logout now lands there). The code step tells a wrong code ("not correct") from one that is expired, spent or out of tries ("expired or no longer valid"): a row exists for every address, so neither says anything about the account, and the number of tries left is never shown. The typed address is echoed back to the person who typed it (the sealed cookie carries it for the resend); the pages are otherwise identical for any address.
  5. **Invitation link.** The invitation mail carries `https://<workspace>.<base>/auth/invite/<id>`. The id is the first 24 bytes of `HMAC-SHA256(AUTH_SECRET, "invitation:<workspace>:<email>:<createdAt>")`, base64url: it cannot be guessed, it says nothing of the address, and it changes when the person is invited again (the old link stops working). The gateway finds the invitation by computing the id of each pending invitation of the workspace (a short list), so there is no schema change. The landing says who invited the person (the member's name, or their email), to which workspace and with which role, and offers the code form for that address. A wrong, expired, withdrawn or replaced link is the same 404 page. The id opens the landing and nothing else: no cookie, no session; signing in still takes the code mailed to the address. This does not reopen the "link in the mail" that D-049 rejected: that was a link that signs in.
  6. **`/auth/cli` asks first.** `GET /auth/cli` (signed in, loopback `redirect_uri` checked as before) shows "Authorize the forgeron CLI on 127.0.0.1:<port>?" with the person, the workspace and the token's lifetime, and two buttons that `POST /auth/cli` with the CLI's request and `decision=allow|deny`. The POST applies the rule of D-049 (`Origin` equal to the page's, or `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` with no or a null origin), needs the session, and checks the request again. Anything but `allow` is a cancel: the CLI is sent `?error=access_denied&state=…` (it already says "The workspace refused the sign-in"). A signed-out browser is still sent to sign in first, and the sign-in page says it is to authorize the CLI.
  7. **The answer is a page, not a redirect.** The CSP's `form-action 'self'` is applied by Chrome to the redirect that follows a form post, so a 302 to `127.0.0.1` would be blocked, and loosening the directive for one port is what D-049's CSP rules out. The POST answers 200 with a page that says what happened, a `<meta http-equiv="refresh">` to the loopback address and a "Continue to the CLI" link as a fallback. The one-time code travels in that address as before (60 seconds, PKCE-bound, single use).
- **Why**: the first real install showed unstyled pages; people could not tell which page was which, and `forgeron login` handed a token to whatever opened the link in a signed-in browser without asking (a page or a message that sent someone to `/auth/cli?redirect_uri=http://127.0.0.1:…` completed at once). One shell makes every page follow `docs/design-system.md`, and the confirmation puts a click between the link and the code.
- **Rejected**: a link that signs in; an invitation id stored in a new column (a migration for a list that is read once per landing); an external stylesheet or web fonts (a request, and the CSP forbids them); `form-action 'self' http://127.0.0.1:*` on the CLI page; a 303 to the loopback address after the POST; showing the number of tries left.
- **Know**: `?reason=expired` is served, but nothing sends a browser there yet: the console has to link to it when a request answers 401. A person who opens the invitation link while signed in as another member still gets the landing. The mail's link is plain text in a text mail: a mail client may not make it clickable, and it carries no secret.
- **Not verified**: rendered in the Claude desktop app's browser pane only (dark, light and phone width, as static files); not in Safari, Firefox or a screen reader; the meta refresh to the loopback address was proven against the gateway's markup, not in a browser against a real `forgeron login`; the brand fonts show only where they are installed.
- **Applied in**: FOR-128; `apps/gateway/src/pages/` (`logo.ts` from `assets/logos`), `identity/email-auth.ts`, `identity/cli.ts`, `identity/invitation.ts`, `identity/origin.ts`, `identity/auth.ts`, `consent/consent.ts`, `dispatch/user-secrets.ts`, `index.ts`; `packages/runtime/src/secrets.ts`; `docs/self-host.md`.

## D-054 — The console is an application: a sidebar shell, path routing with the History API, and screens for the first run, members, settings and the audit log

- **Date**: 03/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. `apps/console` draws a shell (`@forgeron/ui` `AppShell`, `Sidebar`, `Nav`): the workspace and the signed-in person with sign-out in the `surface` sidebar, content on `bg`, a skip link, and at 800px or less the sidebar collapses behind a menu button. Forge (dark) is the default; Atelier (light) is `data-theme="light"` set from `prefers-color-scheme` and kept in step with it. There is no manual toggle.
  2. **Routing is the History API and one table** (`router.ts`): `/`, `/tools`, `/tools/<name>`, `/members`, `/approvals`, `/audit`, `/settings`. No router dependency: seven paths do not justify one. The gateway answers any path that is not a file with the console (`not_found_handling: single-page-application`), so a deep link or a reload lands in the app. A plain click on a link routes in the page and moves focus to the content; a modified click is the browser's. The tool pages (FOR-130) replace `ToolPage` and take the `/tools/<name>` route.
  3. **Admin-only screens are hidden from a member**, not greyed: Members, Approvals, Audit log and Settings are in the sidebar of an admin only; a member who types the address gets "This page is for admins". The platform still refuses every call (403); the screen says so first and does not ask.
  4. **Three small JSON routes** were added to the console API because no screen could be drawn without them: `GET /api/workspace` (the person, the endpoint, and counts: members, pending invitations, tools, tools with a version on an environment, whether any action was ever called; for admins also which sign-in methods are on and the mail mode), `GET /api/approvals` with `POST /api/approvals/<id>` (the console called `/api/tools/<tool>/approvals`, which never existed; deciding is admin-only and audited by the registry, once), and `GET /api/audit` (the latest lines of the tools, newest first, `detail` not sent, the actor as an email when it is a member).
  5. **The first-run checklist is computed, not stored**: signed in (always true once the page loads), team invited (a second member or a pending invitation), first tool deployed (a version on any environment), first call made (an `action` line in the audit log of any tool). It is true after a reload and for every member. Each step that is not done shows its exact CLI commands in a copy block.
  6. **Settings** edits the one single sign-on provider (OIDC, Entra ID or Google; or SAML 2.0): saving one replaces the other, and the form says so. A client secret goes in and never comes back (empty keeps the saved one); a save carries the saved `groupRoles` along, which the form does not edit, so it does not drop them. Email sign-in and the mail mode (D-049, D-051) are the install's: shown with a warning for `console`, changed by installing again.
  7. **A local harness** (`bun run --cwd apps/console harness`, the `console-harness` preview) runs the console on an in-memory gateway (`src/mock/install.ts`, `VITE_MOCK=1`; `?mock=fresh|member|signedout|error|slow`). It is dead code in a build: `import.meta.env.VITE_MOCK` is replaced by `undefined` and the module is not emitted.
  8. **Same product as the landing page**: the console reuses `@forgeron/ui` as `apps/site` does (the same `Logo` anvil, tokens, 48px grid on `bg`), and `CopyBlock` and `Input` follow the reference `CodeBlock` and `Input` of `assets/design-system/components` (title bar with a bordered Copy button, `$` prompt in `ink-muted`, field on `bg`).
- **Why**: the first real install showed a console with no navigation and no way to know what to do first. The checklist answers that from data the platform already holds.
- **Rejected**: `react-router` (a dependency for seven paths); hash routing (the gateway already falls back to the console, and paths are what people share); a stored "onboarding done" flag (it can disagree with the workspace); a theme toggle (the system's scheme is the person's choice, and a stored one is state to explain).
- **Know**: the audit log lists the lines tied to a tool (actions, deployments, approvals, access); changes of members, invitations and the sign-in provider have no tool and are not in it yet. `ToolsList` still shows versions as badges; `EnvTag` shapes come with the tool pages.
- **Not verified**: no real workspace was used: every screen was seen against the harness only. The copy blocks hold commands as `docs/sdk.md` and the CLI state them; `forgeron deploy --env preview --branch <name>` keeps `<name>` for the person to replace.
- **Applied in**: FOR-129; `apps/console/src`, `packages/ui/src` (`shell`, `feedback`, `form`, `dialog`, `copy-block`, `tabs`), `apps/gateway/src/console-api/workspace.ts`, `approvals.ts`, `audit.ts`.

## D-055 — The pilot environment is removed: Forgeron has `local`, `preview` and `production`; a version goes from a preview to production

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Replaced in part by D-125 (a version no longer goes from a preview to production: a permanent channel takes the releases of the branch it follows; `promote` is removed)
- **Decision**: there is no `pilot` environment, no pilot group and no groups API. The environments are `local`, `preview` (one per branch) and `production`. A version is promoted from a preview to production with `promote --to production` (no rebuild, D-033) and put back with `rollback`. Replaces D-021 (and its compatibility check between pilot and production migrations, which was never implemented), D-034 (the pilot group: `group_members`, `pilot_group`, `GET /api/groups`, `PUT /api/groups/<name>`, the dispatcher's `inPilot`) and the pilot parts of D-033 (`deploy --group`, `promote --group`, `--to pilot`, `--env pilot`, the `group` of `POST /deploy` and `POST /promote`). Kept: `GET /api/members` and the members API (D-049), the SSO group claims and `groupRoles` (D-019, D-050), approvals, the changelog and impact rule (FOR-64) and the migration rules, now for production: a changelog entry whose impact is at least the detected one is needed to deploy or promote to production, a destructive migration needs `--allow-destructive`, migrations apply before traffic is switched and a failing one keeps the old version (`sdk.md` §8). A version is `1.5.0+sha` on production, `1.5.0-preview.<branch>+sha` on a preview. A new registry migration, `0019_remove_pilot`, upgrades an installed platform: it deletes the pilot environments with their deployments, secrets and connections, drops `group_members` and `environments.pilot_group`, and rebuilds `environments`, `secrets` and `connections` so that their `CHECK` allows `preview` and `production` only (`deployments` is copied aside during the rebuild, because dropping `environments` cascades into it). It is not flagged destructive: that flag guards a tool's data, `platform install` applies platform migrations without it, and the rows deleted belong to a retired feature. It has no `down`: the pilot data is gone.
- **Why**: the owner found it over-engineered after reviewing the environments: a third environment on the address and database of production, a group of people to maintain, and a compatibility check to write, when a preview already tests a version and promotion plus rollback put it live.
- **Rejected**: keeping the pilot hidden behind a flag (the code and its tests stay); a separate pilot database (D-021 already rejected it); a canary by percentage of callers (a different feature, nobody asked).
- **Know**: the pilot Worker of an install (`<workspace>--<tool>--pilot`) is not deleted by anything: `platform install` reads the environments from the registry after migrating, so it no longer carries the `TOOL_<WORKSPACE>__<TOOL>__PILOT` service binding over, and the Worker stays on the Cloudflare account, unreachable, until someone deletes it. A tool's own `TOOL_*_PILOT` binding on an old gateway version is dropped by the install for the same reason. A version that was only on the pilot is still in `versions` and can be promoted from there. A pilot secret is deleted: set it again on production if it was only there.
- **Not verified**: the migration ran against an in-memory D1 (`remove-pilot.test.ts`) with data in every table it touches, not against a real D1; D1's foreign-key enforcement during the table rebuild is the part to watch.
- **Applied in**: FOR-132; `packages/registry` (`0019_remove_pilot`, `deploy.ts`, `registry.ts`), `packages/hosting`, `packages/runtime`, `packages/sdk`, `apps/gateway` (`dispatch`, `deploy`, `console-api`), `apps/cli`, `apps/console`, `packages/ui` and `assets/design-system` (`EnvTag`), `sdk.md` §7, §8, §15, §16, §20, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `white-paper.md`, `self-host.md`, `design-system.md`.

## D-056 — The console has one route table and a feature folder per domain: a page is added without editing the shell

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: every screen of the console is an entry of the route table, `apps/console/src/shell/routes.ts`: a path with `:name` parameters, a title, who may see it (`everyone`, `member`, `admin`), a lazy page, and optionally a sidebar item (group, label, shortcut) or a place in the settings area. A feature adds its entries by default-exporting a `RouteDef[]` from `apps/console/src/features/<domain>/routes.ts`, which the table collects with `import.meta.glob`; a page that a person may not use is not in the menu, and its address answers "This page is for admins." The shell hands every page the same `PageProps` (`ws`, `api`, `now`, `go`, `params`, `refreshPending`) and draws it inside `AppLayout` under a `Suspense`. The tabs of the tool page are a registry, `features/tools/tabs.ts` (`registerToolTab({ id, title, adminOnly, load })`), that a feature fills from its own `routes.ts`, so the tool page never imports the features. A feature never imports another feature's internals. Replaces the closed list of `RouteId` in `router.ts` (D-054), which needed an edit of the router and of the shell for each screen.
- **Why**: five sessions add screens in parallel; with a closed `switch` in the shell each one would edit the same lines, and a screen's permission was checked in the page, after the menu had already shown it.
- **Rejected**: a routing library (one table and the History API do the job, D-054); registering routes by calling a function at import time (the order of imports would decide the order of the menu, a glob plus the table order does not); a tab registry the shell fills (the shell would import every feature).
- **Applied in**: FOR-134; `apps/console/src/shell`, `apps/console/src/features`, `apps/console/src/router.ts`, `docs/console-ux.md`.

## D-059 — The console reads a tool's database read only: statements built by the server, personal data masked by a `/* personal */` tag or a column name

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: the gateway answers `GET /console-api/api/tools/<tool>/db/...` (environments, tables with row counts, schema, one table's definition, rows, CSV export) for admins and developers, for one environment the registry knows (`env=production`, or `env=preview&branch=<name>`). It reaches the tool's D1 through the hosting layer: `Hosting.openDatabase(environment)` finds the database by its name (`<worker>--db`) and reads it through the Cloudflare REST API with the gateway's own token, which never goes to the browser (self-host real, cloud simulated like the rest of it). There is no endpoint that takes SQL. Every statement is built by the server from validated parameters: a table or column is only ever one SQLite reported (`sqlite_master`, `pragma_table_info`, `pragma_foreign_key_list`, `pragma_index_list`), quoted; a filter is `{ column, op, value }` with an operator that fits the column's type and a value that is only ever bound; paging is 100 rows at most, an offset past 10 000 is refused, a filter list holds 10 at most; a read gives up after 10 s (504); an export stops at 10 000 rows (`x-forgeron-truncated`). A last guard (`assertSelect`) refuses anything that is not one `SELECT` without a second statement or a writing keyword, and the connection handed to the explorer only has that one method. `_forgeron_migrations` is hidden unless `platform=1`; `sqlite_*` and `_cf_*` never show. Every access writes an audit line before data is sent (kind `access`, subject `db:list`, `db:schema`, `db:table:<t>`, `db:rows:<t>`, `db:export:<t>`; detail: environment, branch, page, the filter's columns and operators, never its values); if the line cannot be written nothing is sent. A refused attempt is written too (`denied`). A failure of the host answers 502 without what it said.
- **Personal data**: a column is masked when the tool tagged it with `.personal()` (`@forgeron/sdk/db`), which writes `/* personal */` after the column in its `CREATE TABLE`; the gateway reads the tag from `sqlite_master.sql`, so it works on any environment without the manifest carrying it. A column whose name says it (email, phone, first or last name, address, birth date, IBAN, IP, passport, password, token, secret…) is masked too, as a default for tools that did not tag it; the reason (`tagged` or `name`) goes to the console. Masking is server side: a masked column is not selected at all, its cells are `{ masked: reason }`, it is left out of the CSV, and it cannot be sorted or filtered on (that would leak it one comparison at a time). An admin may pass `reveal=1`; it is audited (`revealed`). A developer cannot.
- **Why**: the only way to look at a database was `forgeron db shell` locally. Real runs showed a simulation misses what the real API refuses, so the surface is kept small enough to be proven by tests: no free SQL, nothing to escape, one pattern of statement.
- **Rejected**: a `SELECT` console (a free statement needs a parser we do not have; it may come later, behind this same guard); a token for the browser (never); carrying the tags in the manifest (the compiler, the manifest and the registry change for what the database can already say); a naming convention only (a column called `notes` that holds names would not be masked).
- **Know**: a column tagged after its table exists needs a migration: the generator sees `personal` as a column change and rebuilds the table, as for any other column change. A tool created before this has no tag: only the names mask. A masked value never reaches the page but is read by D1 when `reveal=1`. The statement timeout stops waiting, it does not cancel the statement on Cloudflare's side. The explorer shows the database as it is, in production too: nothing is copied.
- **Found on a real install**: D1 refuses any read of its own `_cf_*` tables, `pragma_*` on one included (`not authorized`); the three metadata statements joined every `sqlite_master` table to a `pragma_*` and failed on `_cf_KV`, so the tab answered 502. Each statement now leaves `sqlite_*` and `_cf_*` out in its own `WHERE`, and the gateway logs the host's status, message and Cloudflare error code (never the path, ids or a token). The test database is SQLite with an authorizer that refuses what D1 refuses. The refusal itself is read from the failing install's symptoms and D1's behaviour with `_cf_KV`, not from its error text, which was not seen.
- **`platform=1` answered 502 on a real install (FOR-159)**: not reproduced (the strict D1 test database answers 200). `platform=1` changes one thing, the tables counted, and the counts were one `UNION ALL` over every table; they are now one statement per table, and the log names the table whose count failed. That a compound statement is what the host refused is a suspicion, not a finding: the next log line settles it.
- **Not verified**: nothing ran against a real D1. `pragma_table_info`, `pragma_foreign_key_list`, `pragma_index_list` and `pragma_index_info` joined to `sqlite_master` are standard SQLite and work on the in-memory D1 used in tests; whether D1's REST endpoint lets them through, and its limits on a statement's length, are to check on the first real install. Row counts use one `UNION ALL` of `COUNT(*)` (200 tables at most).
- **Applied in**: FOR-136; `apps/gateway/src/console-api/database`, `packages/hosting` (`openDatabase`), `packages/sdk/src/db` (`personal()`), `apps/console/src/features/database`, `packages/ui` (`DataGrid`, `ConstraintChip`, `Pager`, `Drawer`), `docs/sdk.md` §8.

## D-057 — The architecture diagram is a pure layout of the manifest drawn as a static SVG, and it draws only what the manifest says

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: `layoutArchitecture(manifest)` (`packages/ui/src/architecture/layout.ts`) is a pure, deterministic function from a manifest to nodes, edges and coordinates in a fixed 840-unit-wide box. `ArchitectureDiagram` draws it as an inline SVG with a `viewBox`, so it scales to its container, and no layout library. The only edges are interface to action group, from each action's `interfaces`; the manifest says nothing more, so the resources, roles, events, tasks and used tools are nodes without edges. Every node is a focusable element (`tabindex=0`) whose `aria-label` is a full sentence; a text list says the same, and replaces the drawing when its container is narrower than 640px (container query), where the drawing would be too small to read. `GET /api/tools/<tool>/manifest` (developers and admins) gives the console the manifest of the version production serves, else the latest.
- **Why**: the manifest is the one source of truth, so the diagram cannot disagree with the tool. A line between an action and a resource that the manifest does not state would be a guess a reader takes for a fact; the ticket's own rule was to never invent one.
- **Rejected**: a layout library (ELK, dagre): the shape is fixed (three columns and a band), a library adds weight and non-determinism for no gain; drawing action to resource edges from names (`deals.*` to a `deals` table): the manifest carries neither tables nor which action reads what; scaling the drawing down on a phone: unreadable.
- **Know**: the database node is one box, the manifest declares no tables. If the manifest later records which resources an action uses (for example a `uses` list on an action), the layout gets those edges and nothing else changes. The fixtures of the four templates (`packages/ui/src/architecture/fixtures.ts`) are the built manifests with action input schemas cut down to `{ type: "object" }`: rebuild them if the templates' actions change.
- **Not verified**: the tab on the real tool page (FOR-130 is not merged: the tab is registered in the registry of FOR-134, nothing draws it yet); the route against a real D1.
- **Applied in**: FOR-135; `packages/ui` (`architecture/`), `apps/console` (`features/architecture/`), `apps/gateway` (`console-api/manifest.ts`), `apps/site` (`/design-system/architecture`).

## D-058 — The console shell: one workspace switcher, the person's menu at the bottom, and a settings area with its own sidebar

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: the sidebar has no product name and no second workspace label. Top: one workspace switcher (the logo mark, the workspace name, a chevron) whose menu lists the workspaces the person belongs to, then, for an admin, "Workspace settings" and "Invite people" (which opens the members page with the invite dialog). Then a search button (`/`), the navigation in two groups (Home, Tools; then Members, Approvals, Audit log for admins) and Settings at the bottom. Footer: the person's menu (avatar and name; Profile, theme System, Forge or Atelier, Documentation, Sign out). Shortcuts: `g` then the route's letter, `/` for search; neither works while typing in a field or while a menu or dialog is open. At phone width the sidebar is a drawer. Settings is an area with its own sidebar ("Back to app", then Account, Workspace, Administration, from the routes that carry `settings`): Account for everyone, the other two for admins. A settings page is titled groups of cards of rows (label and description left, control right, a chevron on a row that opens a sub-page) with a Danger zone last. The theme choice (system, Forge, Atelier) is the person's and is kept in this browser (`localStorage`, key `forgeron-theme`); `system` follows `prefers-color-scheme`. New `@forgeron/ui` components: `Menu`, `Avatar`, `SettingsGroup`, `SettingsRow`, a heading and icons on `Nav`.
- **Why**: the owner found the old sidebar dense and meaningless; people know the switcher, account menu and settings area of Linear and Vercel, and settings were one page among others.
- **Rejected**: a command palette with actions (the search only goes to pages for now); a theme stored on the platform (nothing on the server needs it); one settings page with tabs (it does not hold Account and Workspace settings apart).
- **Know**: the platform has no API for the person's sessions, connected accounts across tools, personal secrets or seat limits, nor for listing several workspaces; those pages say so instead of showing invented data, and the switcher lists the current workspace only. The Documentation link points at the site's address (`https://forgeron.app`), not at a documentation page. Deleting a workspace is not offered by the console: the Danger zone says where it is done.
- **Applied in**: FOR-134; `apps/console/src/shell`, `apps/console/src/features/settings`, `packages/ui/src` (`menu`, `avatar`, `settings`, `shell`).

## D-060 — A deployment keeps its commit; the tool page reads branches, versions and activity from three console routes; promote and rollback take a reason

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **A deployment records the commit it was built from.** Registry migration `0020_deployment_commit` adds three nullable columns to `deployments` (`commit_sha`, `commit_message`, `commit_author`); an installed platform at 0019 upgrades without touching its rows (they have no commit). `POST /deploy` takes an optional `commit: { sha, message?, author? }` (hex sha of 7 to 64 characters, the message cut to its first line and 200 characters, the author to 100); anything else is a 400. `forgeron deploy` sends it: the sha CI gives (`GITHUB_SHA`) or the checkout's `git log -1`, with the subject and author when they are the checkout's. It is best effort: no git, no commit, the deployment goes on.
  2. **Three routes serve the tool page**, for an admin or a developer (a member without either gets 403; an anonymous caller 401): `GET /api/tools/<tool>/branches` (production and one row per preview: version, commit, who, when, address, migrations applied and the last one, the latest five deployments; `rollbackTo` on production), `GET /api/tools/<tool>/versions` (newest first: where it is served, its changelog, the version published before it, and the manifest changes compared with that one with `diffManifests`, so the changelog "compared with the previous version" of FOR-50 is the author's words plus what the manifest says) and `GET /api/tools/<tool>/activity` (the tool's audit lines, `detail` not sent). `GET /api/tools` gains `summary` (the manifest's description: the production version's, else the latest) and `lastDeployment`. A rollback or a promotion has no commit of its own: its row shows the commit of the deployment that published that version.
  3. **Promote and Roll back call the platform's own routes** with the person's session (`POST /promote`, `POST /rollback`), not `/console-api`: the rules (changelog, approvals, migrations) stay in one place. Both take an optional `reason` (a text of 500 characters at most), written in the audit line's `detail`. The dialog shows the from and to versions, the changelog entries and the impact of the version that goes live (promote) or leaves (roll back), the reason, and says that migrations are not rolled back; a refusal of the platform (a missing changelog entry, an approval awaiting) is shown in the dialog.
  4. **Close preview from the console**: `DELETE /deploy` also accepts an administrator's session, with the tool in the body (CI keeps using the tool's deployment token, whose tool is never the body's). The console asks for the branch name to be typed.
  5. **Status**: a deployment that fails leaves no row, so the API answers `ready` for everything it lists; the screens draw `building` and `failed` (and filter on them) for when the platform records a state.
  6. **The tools list and the tool page are the `features/tools` feature**: the list (table or grid, remembered in `localStorage`, search by name and summary), the page (header and the tabs of the registry) and its tabs Branches, Versions and Activity, plus Access and Secrets moved there from the old `tools-page.tsx`. A tool has no icon in its manifest: a square with its initials stands in. The tabs are `adminOnly` because the shell hands pages `ws.person.admin` only; developers can read the routes (CLI, MCP) but do not see the tabs until the shell exposes roles.
- **Why**: the list showed a version per environment and nothing about who or what: with one preview per branch (D-055) an admin needs to see each branch, its commit and its author, and to promote or roll back knowing what changes.
- **Rejected**: a `reason` kept apart from the audit line (a second table for one text); recording failed builds to have a real `failed` state (a different feature: the deployment is synchronous and says why it failed); having the console build its own promote routes under `/console-api` (a second copy of the changelog and approval rules).
- **Know**: the address of a version of a preview is the environment's current one; `Roll back` on production puts back the version it served before the current one, so two rollbacks in a row swap two versions (D-055's `previousVersion`). The impact shown for a promotion is the declared one of the version, else the one found by comparing manifests; the platform checks it again.
- **Not verified**: the screens were seen against the in-memory harness only, not a real workspace; migration 0020 ran on an in-memory D1 at 0019 with rows (`remove-pilot.test.ts`), not on the real installed D1 (add three columns, no table rebuild).
- **Applied in**: FOR-130; `packages/registry` (`0020_deployment_commit`, `registry.ts`, `deploy.ts`), `apps/gateway` (`console-api/tool-pages.ts`, `tools.ts`, `deploy/deploy.ts`, `deploy/promote.ts`), `apps/cli` (`commands/deploy.ts`), `apps/console/src/features/tools`.

## D-061 — A tool's README and images are collected at build, stored with the version, and served to every member through a catalog API

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Build**: `forgeron build` reads `README.md` of the tool folder, the images it references with a relative path (markdown `![](…)` and `<img src="…">`, outside code blocks; an external address is left alone) and the config's `icon`. Limits: 1 MB per file (128 KB for the icon), 5 MB in all; types `png`, `jpg`, `webp`, `gif`, `svg`, checked against the file's first bytes (not `svg`); no path that leaves the tool folder by `..`, an absolute path or a link (`realpath` inside the folder); an SVG with `<script>`, `foreignObject`, `iframe`, `embed`, `object`, `animate`, `set`, an `on…` attribute, `javascript:`, a DOCTYPE or entity, `@import`, or any `href` or `url()` that is not `#…` is refused, never rewritten. Each refusal is a build error naming the file. `docs.json` (build output) holds the content, the manifest holds `docs: { readme?, assets: [{ path, type, size, hash }] }` and `icon` (a path among the assets), never the content. The README is part of the fingerprint like any source file.
  2. **Config**: one optional key, `icon`. `title` and `description` already exist: the catalog's `title` is the title (else the name) and its `summary` is the `description`. A separate `summary` key would hold the same sentence twice. Public API change: `docs/sdk.md` sections 3 and 14 and `ToolConfig` in `@forgeron/sdk`.
  3. **Storage**: registry migration `0021_tool_docs` creates `docs_blobs (tool_id, hash, part, content)` and `build_docs (build_id, path, hash, type, size)`; it only adds tables, so an installed platform (at 0019) upgrades with `platform install` and nothing else changes. The content is chunked at 20,000 characters like the gateway's code (D-032), stored once per tool and hash (a version that keeps an image does not copy it), in batches of 100 statements: the content first, then the list of the build's files, so a build that has a list has all its content. The README is stored as text and an image as base64. `deployBuild` stores them with the build; `promote` and `rollback` need nothing, since they move a version that already has its files. `platform install` is unchanged.
  4. **The platform does not trust the build**: `POST /deploy` and `deployBuild` check the documentation again (`docsProblem`): path, type from the extension, size, hash and the SVG rules above, and that the manifest lists exactly what was sent (400 `INVALID`, `INVALID_DOCS` in the registry; nothing is recorded).
  5. **Catalog API** (`docs/console-ux.md`, "Catalog API"; types in `@forgeron/manifest` `catalog.ts`): `GET /console-api/api/catalog`, `/api/catalog/<tool>` and `/api/catalog/<tool>/assets/<path>`, for any signed-in member (401 otherwise). A tool with at least one version is listed, whoever the person is; `access` is `granted` when the person's roles, with the admin's matrix applied (`mayAccess`, the rule of the runtime), may call at least one action, else `requestable`, and `roles` names the roles that would let them. The version shown is the one production serves, else the latest published (a tool whose production waits for an approval is still in the catalog). The workspace's address in `connect` comes from the request.
  6. **Images are served by the console API, not under `/t/<tool>/_docs`**: `/t/<tool>` belongs to the tool (reserved paths, previews, dispatch) and the console API already has the session, so only a signed-in member reads an image. Each asset's `url` ends with `?v=<sha256>`; with that hash the answer is `cache-control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable`, with any other it is `private, no-store` (the current file, never cached as immutable). Always `content-type` from a five-entry allow list, `x-content-type-options: nosniff`, `cross-origin-resource-policy: same-origin` and `content-security-policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` (SVG: `default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox`). The path is decoded once and must be a plain relative path of the bundle (no `..`, `.`, empty segment, backslash, NUL); the README is not reachable as a file.
  7. **The README is returned as a JSON string of markdown**, never HTML. The client must render markdown without raw HTML and show only images whose address is one of `assets[].url` (`docs/console-ux.md`).
  8. The four templates have a README for a member (what it does, how to use it, permissions) with the developer notes below it.
- **Why**: a person who is not an administrator has to find what a tool does and how to get it before they can ask for it, and the tool's repository already says it. Collecting at build means the card is the version that runs, and checking in the platform means a hand-made request cannot put a script in front of members.
- **Rejected**: reading the README from GitHub at runtime (a token, a rate limit, and a card that can disagree with the version); a `summary` key beside `description`; serving images from `/t/<tool>/_docs/` (the tool's namespace, and anonymous at the edge unless the gateway re-checks); sanitising an SVG by rewriting it (a refusal says what to fix; a rewrite hides it); one row per version for each image (copies of the same file).
- **Know**: old blobs are never deleted (a tool's deleted version keeps them until the tool goes: `ON DELETE CASCADE`); an external image in a README is not collected and the console will not show it; the markdown renderer is the console's job (FOR-138).
- **Not verified**: the migration ran against an in-memory D1 only, not a real one; an SVG is checked by patterns, not by an XML parser, and was not tried in browsers; a deployment of a build with the 5 MB maximum was not tried against the real D1 or Worker request limits; `forgeron deploy` with docs was not run against a gateway (the CLI change is a pass-through of `docs.json`, tested through the route).
- **Applied in**: FOR-137; `packages/manifest` (`docs.ts`, `catalog.ts`, `schema.ts`), `packages/compiler` (`docs.ts`, `build.ts`, `manifest.ts`), `packages/sdk` (`config.ts`), `packages/hosting` (`BuildDocs`), `packages/registry` (`0021_tool_docs`, `docs-check.ts`, `catalog.ts`, `registry.ts`, `deploy.ts`), `apps/gateway` (`console-api/catalog.ts`, `deploy/deploy.ts`), `apps/cli` (`deploy`), `templates/*/README.md`, `docs/sdk.md`, `docs/console-ux.md`.

## D-062 — The member space renders a README to React elements; links and images are allow-listed

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **One Home for everyone** (`/`): "My tools" (the tools the person may use) and "Catalog" (the ones they could ask for, with the roles to ask an admin for). An admin sees the same lists under a strip of the workspace's numbers and, until it is done, the first-run checklist. A tool the person may not use is in the catalog, never missing and never greyed out.
  2. **The tool card is the Overview tab** of the tool page (`registerOverviewTab()`, visible to everyone): header with the primary action (Open when the tool has a web interface, Connect otherwise), the tabs About (the README) and How to install (per interface, copy blocks), and a details column (version, last release with its changelog, interfaces, roles, what the person will be asked for, links). A person without access sees what to ask for and no primary button.
  3. **A README is rendered, never executed.** `react-markdown` 10.1.0 with `remark-gfm` 4.0.1 (pinned) builds React elements: no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, no `rehype-raw`, so raw HTML in a README is shown as text and never parsed. Every address goes through an allow-list in `packages/ui/src/markdown.tsx`. Links keep `http`, `https`, `mailto` and in-page anchors (rewritten to the `md-` ids of the headings, so a heading named "content" cannot clobber the console's own ids); a relative link, `javascript:`, `data:`, or a scheme hidden by control characters keeps its text and loses the link. External links get `target="_blank"` and `rel="noopener noreferrer"` and are marked. An image is drawn only when its relative path is one of the card's `assets[].path` (the address drawn is that asset's `url`, served by the console API; a path that climbs out, an absolute path, another origin or a `data:` address is refused), lazily, with `referrerpolicy="no-referrer"`, a maximum width and a required alt text: without one, or when refused, a labelled placeholder is shown.
  4. **Presentational pieces live in `packages/ui`**: `Markdown`, `ToolCard`, `ToolIcon`, `DetailList` (with `CopyBlock` and `EmptyState`), each tested. `apps/console/preview.html` is their preview: a fixed fake README with images, a table, code, a task list and a hostile sample that must stay inert.
  5. **The catalog API is D-061's**: the console reads `GET /console-api/api/catalog` and `/api/catalog/<tool>` through `features/portal/catalog.ts`, typed with `@forgeron/manifest`'s `CatalogTool` and `ToolCard` and read defensively (no zod in the browser), and the harness answers them from `features/portal/mock.ts`.
- **Why**: a person who is not an admin signs in to the same console and needs to find what they may use, what they could ask for, and how to use it. A README is written by the owner of a tool and shown to every member: safety has to come from what the renderer is built from, not from filtering its output.
- **Rejected**: `marked` or `markdown-it` with the output set through `innerHTML` and a sanitiser (a second library to get right, and an HTML string one mistake away from a script); `rehype-raw` with `rehype-sanitize` (it would allow some HTML, which no README needs); a per-tool icon from an icon set (there is none yet, `docs/design-system.md`): the card uses a monogram; images from any HTTPS origin (a tracking pixel in every member's browser).
- **Know**: a relative link in a README (to another file of the repository) has nothing to open and is shown as text. Raw HTML written in a README appears as visible text instead of disappearing. The Open button goes to `/t/<tool>/`, an address assumed from how tools are served. The contract has no links field, so the details column has no links yet; a changelog entry is free-form in the contract, so only entries that are a string or hold a line of text are shown.
- **Not verified**: no real workspace was used: everything was seen on the harness in the in-app browser (dark and light, 375 px and desktop, keyboard only), not in Safari or Firefox, not with a screen reader. The console was not run against the real catalog API, only against the harness mock typed with the contract.
- **Applied in**: FOR-138; `packages/ui/src` (`markdown`, `tool-card`), `apps/console/src/features/portal`, `apps/console/preview.html`.

## D-063 — The registry has one door and the gateway has a kernel: folder cycles are forbidden by rules, each proved by a self-test case

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **`registry-door-is-index`**: code outside `packages/registry` imports `@forgeron/registry` only (its `src/index.ts`), never a file under `src/`. It holds today (the package `exports` already hides the files); the rule keeps it so when `registry.ts` is split into modules (FOR-156).
  2. **`apps/gateway/src/kernel/`** holds what several gateway folders share and that belongs to none: `Person` (`person.ts`) and `workspaceOf` (`workspace.ts`, with the reserved names). It imports nothing else of the gateway (`gateway-kernel-is-a-leaf`).
  3. **The two folder cycles are removed and forbidden**: `identity <-> dispatch` (`workspaceOf` in `auth.ts` and `email-auth.ts`, `Person` in `dispatch/user-secrets.ts`) and `identity <-> pages` (`Person` in `pages/cli.ts`). `identity` keeps rendering `pages`; `dispatch` and `pages` never import `identity` (`identity-is-below-dispatch-and-pages`, test files aside), and `identity` never imports `dispatch` (`identity-does-not-import-dispatch`). Each rule has a case in `scripts/deps-selftest.ts`.
  4. `dispatch.ts` still exports `workspaceOf` (a re-export of the kernel's), so `gateway.ts` and `dispatch.test.ts` do not change. No behaviour change.
- **Why**: `docs/spikes/module-model.md` (FOR-144) found that `no-cycles` is green at file level while two folder cycles exist; the kernel is the smallest move that removes both, and a rule is what keeps them from coming back.
- **Rejected**: putting `Person` and `workspaceOf` in `packages/registry` (the ticket says "registry kernel"): `workspaceOf` parses a host and `Person` is the runtime's user, both gateway matters, and the registry would then export HTTP concerns to the gateway it serves; the registry's own kernel (ids, clock, `secretBox`, `auditStatement`) comes with FOR-156. Folding the folder rules into `no-cycles` with `--collapse`: dependency-cruiser finds cycles before it collapses, so a folder cycle stays invisible (tried on the old and the new tree: both report nothing).
- **Know**: the rule that `identity` is below `dispatch` and `pages` is a direction chosen because `identity` renders pages and not the reverse; a future folder that both need goes in the kernel.
- **Not verified**: folder cycles other than the two named (the check above cannot find them), the rest of `apps/gateway` beyond the touched tests and the typecheck.
- **Applied in**: FOR-155; `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`, `scripts/deps-selftest.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/kernel`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` ("Dependency rules").

## D-064 — The platform API moves to Hono one route at a time: route objects in `@forgeron/platform-contract`, a Hono shell in the gateway that falls through to the legacy chain

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Carrier**: Hono 4.13.16 with `@hono/zod-openapi` 1.6.3 (zod 4.6.7), as chosen by the spike (`docs/spikes/hono-contract.md`, FOR-139). No GraphQL for the platform API. The hand-written `/auth/*` pages stay as they are.
  2. **The contract is a package**: `packages/platform-contract` holds route objects as plain data (`defineRoute`, a `RouteSpec` type written in the package, so no HTTP library is imported), `ErrorSchema` (`{ error, message? }`, the body of every refusal) and `createClient(fetch, base)`, a typed client derived from the route objects alone (fill the path, the query and a JSON body; the answer is a union told apart by `status`). The console and the CLI use it with their own `fetch`; they never import the gateway, so `apps-do-not-import-each-other` holds, and `hc` is not used. Rule `platform-contract-imports-schemas-only` (case in `scripts/deps-selftest.ts`): the package imports no package of the repository but `manifest`. Its only npm dependency is `zod`; dependency-cruiser leaves `node_modules` out of its graph, so knip enforces that (an import `package.json` does not list fails).
  3. **The shell**: `createGateway` builds one `OpenAPIHono` (`apps/gateway/src/platform/app.ts`) and, for a `/console-api` request, calls it before the hand-written chain with `{ identity }` as its `env`. A path Hono does not own answers 404 with the header `x-forgeron-unmatched`, and the gateway goes on with the chain; with no route nothing changes. Everything that could change an answer for a path Hono does not own is attached to a route (`platformRoute(spec, "member" | "admin")` adds the guards), never to the app: a guard on `*` would refuse a legacy path before the chain saw it. The person is not even looked up for such a path.
  4. **Guards, in the order of the legacy handlers**: 401 `UNAUTHENTICATED` without a person, 403 `FORBIDDEN` "This is for admins." for a member on an admin route, then, on POST, PUT and PATCH, 415 `INVALID` "Send JSON." unless the content type starts with `application/json` (the cross-site guard of the console API besides the session cookie, unchanged; no Origin check is added), then the route's schema: 400 `INVALID` "Invalid input.". `onError` maps the `HTTPException` Hono throws (malformed JSON, wrong media type) to 400 or 415 in that shape; any other error is rethrown, so the Worker's own handler logs and answers 500 as before. Every answer of the app carries `cache-control: no-store`.
  5. **A shared matrix**: `describeRouteMatrix` (`apps/gateway/src/platform/route-matrix.ts`) states the 401, 403, 415 and 400 answers once; each route's test calls it with the route's address, access and invalid bodies.
  6. **Build**: `scripts/build.ts` adds `resolve.mainFields: ["module", "main"]`, because `@asteasolutions/zod-to-openapi` (under `@hono/zod-openapi`) has `module` and `main` but no `exports`, which `platform: "neutral"` does not read: Rolldown left the import external and workerd could not load it. `bundle.test.ts` (workerd) is what catches that again.
  7. **Order**: this is steps 1 to 3 of the spike's migration order. The catalog routes (FOR-151), the read and write routes, the console and CLI clients and the removal of the chain follow, one pull request each.
- **Why**: the 12 hand-written handlers each repeat the path regex, the JSON helper, the guards, the parser and the error mapping; a route object checked against its handler at compile time, a client that fails to compile when the contract changes and an OpenAPI 3.1 document from the same objects replace that. Moving one handler at a time keeps every step shippable and `bun run check` green.
- **Rejected**: Hono RPC (`hc`), which needs the server's app type in the console; `hono-openapi`, which does not check the handler against the contract; a global identity or JSON guard on `*` (it would answer legacy paths); typing the contract with `RouteConfig` from `@hono/zod-openapi` (the package would import an HTTP library, which a client would bundle); GraphQL.
