- **Know**: the gateway bundle grows by about 608 KB raw / 114 KB gzip (below); the module is stored in the platform D1 in 20,000-character chunks, so it takes about twice the rows and is reloaded on each binding change. `@hono/zod-openapi` extends zod's prototype for the whole Worker (`extendZodWithOpenApi`), `@forgeron/manifest` schemas included; no side effect was found by the existing tests, but none was looked for beyond them.
- **Not verified**: the bundle was loaded in workerd through Miniflare only, never against a real Cloudflare account or the real D1 (the module size through `platform install` and `loadGatewayCode`, cold start); the shell was tested with a route that exists in tests only, since no real route is served by Hono yet; the console's own bundle with the contract client was not built (the client is imported by nothing yet).
- **Applied in**: FOR-150; `packages/platform-contract`, `apps/gateway/src/platform`, `apps/gateway/src/gateway.ts`, `apps/gateway/scripts/build.ts`, `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`, `scripts/deps-selftest.ts`.

## D-065 — The registry is split into modules behind the same `createRegistry`; the migration order stays global and frozen

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **`packages/registry/src/modules/<domain>/`** for `catalog` (tools, builds, versions, environments, deployments, README and images), `secrets` (tool and per-person secrets), `connections`, `members` (members, invitations, domains, seats), `identity-store` (one-time codes, token revocation), `approvals`, `access` (the roles × actions matrix) and `audit` (the read side and `recordAudit`). Each has `store.ts` (the SQL), `types.ts` where it has types, `migrations.ts` where it owns tables, and `public.ts`, the only file another part of the registry may import. `registry.ts` composes them behind the unchanged `createRegistry(d1, options)`; `index.ts` and its 29 importers do not change. The method names are the same 69 (checked against the old object).
  2. **`kernel/`** holds what every module shares and knows no module (`kernel-is-generic`): `createKernel` (the D1 handle, `now`, `newId`, `all`, `first`, `write`, `secretBox`, `auditStatement`), `Row`, `text`, `json`, `RegistryError`, the audit types, `st`. **`auditStatement` stays a statement builder in the kernel**: a module puts it in the same `d1.batch` as the write it records, so the line cannot be written without the change, or the change without the line. `audit-atomicity.test.ts` shows it for activation, role access, members and approvals with a trigger that makes the audit insert fail.
  3. **A module asks another through a narrow port**: `secrets`, `connections`, `approvals` and `access` take a `CatalogPort` (`requireTool` and the few catalog methods they call), a `Pick` of the catalog's public API.
  4. **Migrations**: each module exports its own list and keeps the global number in the name; `migrations.ts` composes the lists sorted by name and throws on a duplicate name. A migration that spans several modules (`0001`, `0016`, `0019`) belongs to the module that owns the tables it creates or removes. The migrations of stores that are not modules yet (`0010` gateway code, `0015` identity config, `0017` SAML, `0018` gateway assets) are in `root-migrations.ts`. `migrations.golden.json` is the list as installed (0001 to 0021), generated from the unsplit file and byte-identical to it; `migrations.test.ts` compares the composed list with it (names, fingerprints, statements, order), applies it on a D1, and checks that a D1 installed with the frozen list finds nothing pending, missing, edited or late. A new migration is appended to the frozen copy; an existing entry is never changed.
  5. **Rules, each with a case in `scripts/deps-selftest.ts`**: `module-uses-public-api` (a module imports another's `public.ts` only), `registry-root-uses-module-public-api` (nor does the rest of the package), `kernel-is-generic`.
- **Why**: `registry.ts` was one 1,750-line closure that most work touched. Splitting by domain makes a change local, the rules keep the doors shut, and the frozen list keeps the installed D1 (at 0021) from reporting MISSING or EDITED.
- **Rejected**: Awilix or any container (D-063 and the spike: the modules are few and SQL-bound); a domain / persistence split inside each module (the logic is SQL statements); one package per module; per-module numbering (the installed D1 orders by global name); making `auditStatement` a call to the audit module (the line would leave the batch); a differently shaped API (the 54 importers would change).
- **Know**: an intra-module call still uses `this` as before, so a method called detached from the registry object fails as it always did. The registry is a spread of the modules' objects: two modules defining the same method name would silently override one another; the unchanged list of 69 names rules it out today, nothing forbids it tomorrow. `approvals` has no migration list: its table is created by `0001`, owned by `catalog`. `unapproved` moved from the access region to `approvals`, where its table is.
- **Not verified**: the migrations were applied on the in-memory D1 only, not on a copy of the installed D1 and not on Cloudflare; the audit atomicity is shown for four modules (catalog `activate`, access, members, approvals), not for each of the 10 audit statements; nothing was deployed.
- **Applied in**: FOR-156; `packages/registry/src/{kernel,modules,registry.ts,migrations.ts,root-migrations.ts,migrations.golden.json}`, `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`, `scripts/deps-selftest.ts`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `packages/registry/README.md`.

## D-066 — The catalog is the first contract served by Hono: the OpenAPI document is for signed-in members, the console calls it through a client that bundles no schema

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Catalog routes** (FOR-151, step 4 of the spike's migration order): `GET /api/catalog`, `/api/catalog/<tool>` and `/api/catalog/<tool>/assets/<path>` are route objects in `packages/platform-contract` (`catalog.ts`) that use the manifest's schemas (`CatalogResponseSchema`, `ToolCardSchema`); the gateway serves them from `apps/gateway/src/platform/catalog.ts` and `console-api/catalog.ts` is deleted. Their answers, headers and tests are those of D-061; `gateway-catalog.test.ts` is unchanged and runs through the gateway. The contract now declares the `500` of a `RegistryError` (`{ error: <registry code>, message }`) that the old handler already gave.
  2. **The image route is a plain Hono route** (registered for the document with `openAPIRegistry.registerPath`): `app.openapi` turns `{path}` into a one-segment parameter and an image path has slashes. Its handler is not checked against the contract at compile time; it keeps the old headers (type from an allow list of five, `nosniff`, the CSP, `cross-origin-resource-policy`, `etag`, the cache keyed by `?v=<hash>`) and `HEAD`. The shell's `no-store` is now only a default: it leaves a `cache-control` a route set.
  3. **Other methods are `405`** on the three catalog paths, before anyone is asked who they are, as before (`methodNotAllowed`, registered after the routes). A tool name that is not `[a-z0-9-]+` on the card route is now a `404` with a message from the route, where it used to reach the end of the chain (also `404`).
  4. **`GET /console-api/api/openapi.json`** serves the OpenAPI 3.1 document of what the contract serves (`servers: /console-api`), built once per Worker instance, `no-store`, **for any signed-in member** (anonymous `401`): the same rule as the catalog it describes. It describes the API a member's own console calls, so an anonymous visitor has nothing to gain from it and a public document would only tell a scanner which routes exist. Whether to make it public (for third-party tooling) is left to the owner; making it so is one line in `platform.ts`.
  5. **The console client bundles no schema**: the contract's `./client` entry (`createClient`, `addressOf`) imports no schema and no package of ours. A browser imports the route as a type only (`import type { listCatalogRoute }`) and holds an `Address` (`addressOf<typeof listCatalogRoute>({ method: "get", path: "/api/catalog" })`), which fails to compile when the method or path is not the route's. A route object is its own `Address`, so the gateway and the CLI pass routes. `ResponseSpec` accepts other media types (images); the client reads JSON only.
  6. **The console reads the catalog through `typedClient(fetchJson)`** (`api.ts`): the contract's client over a screen's `fetchJson`, GET only, so a refusal still throws `ApiError` and the tests and the harness keep handing in their own `fetchJson`. The hand-written reading of the answers is gone; the allow-list of image addresses (`ASSET_URL`) and the flattening of a changelog entry to a line stay. The answers are typed, not validated at run time.
- **Measured** (one build each, 04/11/2026): gateway bundle 1,128,800 → 1,133,767 bytes raw, 244,153 → 245,781 gzip (no `node:*`, nothing external); console production bundle (`vite build`, JS and CSS, gzip with Node's default level): 581,973 → 582,149 bytes raw, 180,897 → 181,965 gzip (one more file). Importing the route objects in the console instead of the address cost +94,689 raw / +27,257 gzip in the entry chunk (zod and the manifest schemas), which is why the client entry is separate.
- **Rejected**: the route objects in the browser (the 27 KB above); a public document (see 4); a hand-written `Address` that is not checked against the route.
- **Not verified**: the console was not run against a real gateway (only its tests with the typed client over `fetchJson`, and the production build); the image route and the document were not run in a browser or against a real D1; the document was checked for its paths, methods, statuses and media types, not against an OpenAPI validator.
- **Applied in**: FOR-151; `packages/platform-contract` (`catalog.ts`, `route.ts`, `client.ts`), `apps/gateway/src/platform` (`catalog.ts`, `platform.ts`, `app.ts`), `apps/console/src/api.ts` and `features/portal/catalog.ts`, `docs/console-ux.md`.

## D-067 — The tool routes (list, branches, versions, activity, manifest) are served by Hono; a developer access level with a per-route 403 message

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **First of the read routes** (FOR-152, step 5 of the spike's migration order): `GET /api/tools` (any signed-in member) and `GET /api/tools/<tool>/{branches,versions,activity,manifest}` (admins and developers) are route objects in `packages/platform-contract` (`tools.ts`) and served from `apps/gateway/src/platform/tools.ts`. `console-api/manifest.ts` and the handlers of `tools.ts` and `tool-pages.ts` are deleted; what they computed stays (`listTools`, shared with the admin MCP, and `branches`, `versionsOf`, `activityOf`). The answers are the same JSON; the handlers are checked against the declared answers at compile time.
  2. **Access `developer`** in the shell (`platformRoute(spec, "developer", forbidden?)`): 403 for anyone who is neither an admin nor a developer. The message of the 403 is a per-route option because the old handlers worded it differently (`This is for admins and developers.` for the tool page, `The manifest is for developers and admins.` for the manifest); `describeRouteMatrix` takes it as `forbidden`, and checks that an admin who is not a developer gets in.
  3. **Unchanged on purpose**: the order (401, 403, then 404 for an unknown tool, so a plain member is never told whether a tool exists), `?limit=` on the activity route read leniently (anything but a positive integer is 50, at most 200; the contract types it as a string for that reason, no 400), the 405 on any other method (before anyone is asked who they are), the manifest's `404 NO_MANIFEST`.
  4. **The tests are the old ones**, now calling the app through `platformCaller` (`platform/test-app.ts`): `tools.test.ts`, `tool-pages.test.ts` and `manifest.test.ts` keep their cases; `platform/tools.test.ts` adds the shared matrix for the five routes.
- **Why**: one handler per pull request keeps each step reviewable (FOR-152); the tool page and the manifest are the first routes with a role other than admin, so the access level had to exist before the next ones.
- **Not verified**: the console was not run against this build (it reads these routes through its own `fetchJson`; moving it to the typed client is a later step); the new contract schemas for these answers were written from the builders' types, and `manifest` is typed as a free record (the manifest schema has refinements the OpenAPI generator may not accept): the document was not checked with an OpenAPI validator.
- **Applied in**: FOR-152 (tools); `packages/platform-contract/src/tools.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/platform` (`tools.ts`, `app.ts`, `route-matrix.ts`, `test-app.ts`), `apps/gateway/src/console-api` (`tools.ts`, `tool-pages.ts`).

## D-068 — The workspace, audit log and access matrix routes are served by Hono; the access matrix write now requires a JSON content type

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Second read-route PR** (FOR-152): `GET /api/workspace` (any signed-in member), `GET /api/audit` (admins) and `GET`/`PUT /api/tools/<tool>/access-matrix` (admins) are route objects in `packages/platform-contract` (`workspace.ts`, `access-matrix.ts`) served from `apps/gateway/src/platform` (`workspace.ts`, `access-matrix.ts`). The handlers `workspace.ts`, `audit.ts` and `access-matrix.ts` of `console-api/` are deleted. Same JSON, same 405 on other methods before anyone is asked who they are, same lenient `?limit=` on the audit log (50 by default, at most 200). The access matrix is the first write route: its `PUT` is the first to go through the shell's JSON-only guard and the schema of the body.
  2. **The person of the shell carries `email` and `name`** (the workspace route draws them), and the shell's `env` carries `baseDomain` and `signIn()` (which methods are on): the gateway's `identity` closure and the `signIn` it built for the old handler are handed over as they were. The workspace route now builds `person` as `{ id, email, name, roles, admin }` explicitly, where the old one spread the caller it was given.
  3. **Behaviour that is not the same as before, on purpose or by the framework**:
     - the access matrix `PUT` answers `415` to a body that is not sent as `application/json`: the old handler read the body whatever its content type, which is what the JSON-only rule of D-064 exists to prevent for a write on the console API (a cross-site form can send `text/plain`). The console always sends JSON;
     - for an invalid body sent to a tool that does not exist, the answer is `400` where the old handler said `404` (the body is checked before the tool is looked up); both are for admins only;
     - the `400` for a body that is not JSON at all now reads `The body is not JSON.` for every route of the shell (it was `Invalid input.`), and the schema message of the access matrix is the old one.
  4. **Tests**: `workspace.test.ts` keeps its cases for the workspace and the audit log and now calls the app (`platformCaller` takes `signIn` and `baseDomain`); `gateway-access.test.ts` (through the gateway) is unchanged; `platform/console.test.ts` adds the shared 401/403/415/400 matrix for the four routes, with the access matrix's own 403 and 400 messages (`describeRouteMatrix` takes `invalidMessage`).
- **Not verified**: the console was not run against this build (it reads these routes through `fetchJson`); the document was not checked with an OpenAPI validator; nothing ran against a real D1.
- **Applied in**: FOR-152 (workspace, audit, access matrix); `packages/platform-contract/src` (`workspace.ts`, `access-matrix.ts`), `apps/gateway/src/platform` (`workspace.ts`, `access-matrix.ts`, `app.ts`, `console.test.ts`), `apps/gateway/src/gateway.ts`.

## D-069 — The connections and secrets status routes are served by Hono

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Third read-route PR** (FOR-152): `GET /api/tools/<tool>/environments/<env>/connections` and `DELETE …/connections/<user>/<connector>` (admins) and `GET /api/tools/<tool>/secrets-status` (admins and developers) are route objects in `packages/platform-contract` (`connections.ts`, `secrets-status.ts`) served from `apps/gateway/src/platform/connections.ts`; `console-api/connections.ts` and `secrets-status.ts` are deleted. The answers carry no token and no secret value, as before: the schemas are `strictObject`, and `ConnectionInfo` and `SecretStatus` have no such field.
  2. **Same behaviour**: the 403 messages (`Connections are for admins.`, `Secrets status is for developers and admins.`), the order (401, 403, 404 for an unknown tool), the `204` of a revocation and its audit line (`connector-consent:<connector>:revoke`, written by the registry; a failure to write it does not undo the revocation), `404` when that person has no such consent, `400 The person is not valid.` for a person that is not a valid URL encoding (the id is decoded here, once, from the address as sent, because Hono passes a malformed one on undecoded).
  3. **Not the same**: an environment other than `preview` or `production` is a `404` from the route to an admin (it used to fall through to the end of the chain, to anyone); any other method on a connections path is `405` before anyone is asked who they are (it was `405` after the checks); the revocation's `audit` option of the old handler (never passed by the gateway) is gone.
  4. **Tests**: `consent.test.ts` (the connections cases) and `secrets-status.test.ts` keep their cases and call the app; `platform/console.test.ts` adds the shared matrix for the three routes (the `DELETE` has no body, so no 415).
- **Not verified**: the console was not run against this build; no real D1; the document was not checked with an OpenAPI validator.
- **Applied in**: FOR-152 (connections, secrets status); `packages/platform-contract/src` (`connections.ts`, `secrets-status.ts`), `apps/gateway/src/platform/connections.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/gateway.ts`.

## D-070 — The database explorer routes are mounted on Hono, in the contract and the document, and still answered by the explorer itself

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Last of the read routes** (FOR-152): the six routes of the explorer (`GET /api/tools/<tool>/db/{environments,tables,schema}`, `…/tables/<table>`, `…/rows`, `…/export`, D-059) are route objects in `packages/platform-contract` (`database.ts`), registered in the OpenAPI document and mounted on the Hono app (`apps/gateway/src/platform/database.ts`) for every method. The chain's `handleDatabase` call is gone; `handleDatabase` stays the implementation.
  2. **Not ported to typed handlers, on purpose**: the explorer's order of checks is part of what it promises: it looks the tool up first (404), writes a `denied` audit line **before** it answers 403 (a refusal is on the record), gives `401` and `403` a `message` the console shows, and checks the environment, the masking (`reveal` is admin only), the query (400 with its own codes), the timeout (504) and the host's failures (502, the reason only in the log) in that order. The shell's guards (401, then 403, then 404, no audit line) would change all of that, so these routes take no shell guard and the handler decides. Their `200` schemas are documentation (`looseObject` for the table definitions, which the schema reader keeps extending): the handler is not checked against them at compile time, unlike the routes of D-066 to D-069.
  3. **The CSV works through Hono**: the export is a plain `Response` (`text/csv; charset=utf-8`, `content-disposition: attachment`, `cache-control: no-store`, `x-forgeron-truncated` when it cut); the shell's `no-store` default only fills a header a route left out. The CSV is built in memory in chunks of 1,000 rows up to the export cap, not streamed, as before.
  4. **Same behaviour**: every case of the explorer's test (38) passes through the app; a path the routes do not own (`/db/nope`) still falls through (404); anything but `GET` is the explorer's own 405 ("The database is read only.") before anyone is asked who they are. The shell's `env` carries `hosting(workspace)` and an optional `databaseTimeoutMs` (the tests shorten it, as they did the handler's `timeoutMs`).
- **Not verified**: no real D1 and no Cloudflare database (the tests use SQLite behind the explorer's own read-only wrapper); the console was not run against this build; the document was not checked with an OpenAPI validator; a CSV at the export cap was not run through the Worker's real limits.
- **Applied in**: FOR-152 (database explorer); `packages/platform-contract/src/database.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/platform` (`database.ts`, `app.ts`, `test-app.ts`), `apps/gateway/src/gateway.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/console-api/database/database.test.ts`.

## D-071 — The members, invitations and membership settings routes are served by Hono, the first write routes from the contract

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **First write-route PR** (FOR-153, step 6 of the spike's migration order; the ticket's "members and invitations" and "membership settings and allowed domains" are one handler and one set of tests, so they move together): `GET /api/members`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /api/members/<id>`, `POST /api/invitations`, `DELETE /api/invitations/<email>`, `GET` and `PUT /api/membership-settings` (all for admins, D-049) are route objects in `packages/platform-contract` (`members.ts`) served from `apps/gateway/src/platform/members.ts`; `console-api/members.ts` is deleted.
  2. **Same behaviour, checked through the whole gateway**: `gateway-email.test.ts` and `gateway-pages.test.ts` (members, invitations, settings, the invitation mail and its link) pass unchanged. The registry still writes the audit line in the same batch as the change (`setMemberRoles`, `removeMember`, `inviteMember`, `revokeInvitation`, `setMembershipSettings` take the actor): the handlers only call them. The registry's refusals map as before (`NOT_FOUND` 404, `INVALID` 400, any other 409). Names are trimmed, an email is trimmed and lower-cased, an invitation lasts 14 days, `notified` says whether the mail went (the gateway hands `notify` over as before), and the 400 messages are the old ones, given per route (`` `roles` is a list of role names. ``, `` `email` and `roles` (a list of role names) are required. ``, `` `domains` is a list of domains; `defaultRole` a role name or null. ``).
  3. **The JSON-only rule is the shell's**: a `PATCH`, `POST` or `PUT` that is not `application/json` is `415 Send JSON.`, after the 401 and 403, as in the old handler; `DELETE` takes no body and no check. The shell's `env` now carries `installDomains`, `now()` and `notify`.
  4. **Not the same**: any other method on these paths is `405` before anyone is asked who they are (it was `405` after the 401 and 403); the schemas validate the shape of the body before the registry is asked (the old checks did too, by hand), and a body that is JSON but not an object is the same `400`.
  5. **Tests**: `platform/members.test.ts` adds the shared 401/403/415/400 matrix for the seven routes (with their messages and invalid bodies), and the cases the matrix does not cover (settings from the install, trimming, 404s, the invitation's shape).
- **Not verified**: the invitation mail was not sent by a real mail service (the gateway tests use the console mail sender); the console was not run against this build; no real D1; the document was not checked with an OpenAPI validator.
- **Applied in**: FOR-153 (members, invitations, membership settings); `packages/platform-contract/src/members.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/platform` (`members.ts`, `app.ts`, `test-app.ts`, `members.test.ts`), `apps/gateway/src/gateway.ts`.

## D-072 — The identity provider, connector client and tool secret routes are served by Hono; the personal secrets page stays where it is

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Second write-route PR** (FOR-153): `GET`, `PUT` and `DELETE /api/identity-provider`, `GET /api/connector-clients`, `PUT` and `DELETE /api/connector-clients/<type>` (D-041, D-050) and `GET /api/tools/<tool>/environments/<env>/secrets` and `PUT …/secrets/<NAME>` (admins) are route objects in `packages/platform-contract` (`identity.ts`, `secrets.ts`) served from `apps/gateway/src/platform` (`identity.ts`, `secrets.ts`); `console-api/identity.ts` and `secrets.ts` are deleted. The shell's `env` now carries the identity `config` and `canKeepSecrets`.
  2. **Same behaviour**: a secret value or a client secret goes in and never comes out; the `501 NOT_CONFIGURED` without a secret key; the 400 messages (the parsers' own, `` `value` is a non-empty string (16 KB max). ``, the connector client's); `201` for a new connector client and `200` for an update; `204` for a deletion; the audit lines of an identity or connector change (`identity-provider:set`, `…:delete`, `connector-client:<type>:set|delete`, written after the change, best effort and never with a secret in them, as before; the registry audits the rest in the change's own batch). `gateway-sso.test.ts`, `gateway-saml.test.ts` and `gateway-cli-login.test.ts` (sign-in through the console API) pass unchanged; `console-api/secrets.test.ts` keeps its cases and calls the app; `platform/settings.test.ts` adds the shared matrix for the nine routes.
  3. **The two provider bodies are checked by the existing parsers, not by a zod schema**: `parseIdentityProviderInput` and `parseSamlProviderInput` (and the connector client's rules) answer with a message that names the field, which the console shows as it is, and they decide between OIDC and SAML by `kind`. The contract takes those bodies as any JSON and describes them in words; they are not typed in the client. Typing them is a later step if the console moves to the typed client for these screens.
  4. **Not the same as before**: a `PUT` of a secret that is not `application/json` is now `415` (the old handler read the body whatever its content type; the CLI and the console always send JSON); a body that is not JSON at all on the two provider routes is `400 The body is not JSON.` (it was the parser's message for an empty body); an environment other than `preview` or `production`, a secret name that is not `[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*` and a connector type that is not `[a-z][a-z0-9-]*` are `404` from the route (they fell through to the end of the chain); any other method is `405` before anyone is asked who they are.
  5. **The personal secrets page is not moved**: `/t/<tool>/secrets` (D-036) is not a console API route. It is a page and a form of the runtime (`handleSecretsInput` of `@forgeron/runtime`, rendered with the gateway's page shell) at the workspace origin, outside `/console-api`, reached by a session or by the token of `forgeron login` (audience `<workspace>/platform`) with the audience rules of D-015, and it is part of how a tool's call is dispatched. It belongs with the hand-written HTML pages that the spike keeps out of this migration. The ticket's "secrets (tool and personal)" is therefore half done here: the tool's secrets. Moving the personal ones, if wanted, is a separate decision about the pages.
- **Not verified**: no real identity provider or sealed secret on a real D1 (the gateway tests use a simulated provider and a throwaway key); the console and the CLI were not run against this build; the document was not checked with an OpenAPI validator.
- **Applied in**: FOR-153 (identity provider, secrets); `packages/platform-contract/src` (`identity.ts`, `secrets.ts`, `route.ts`), `apps/gateway/src/platform` (`identity.ts`, `secrets.ts`, `app.ts`, `settings.test.ts`), `apps/gateway/src/gateway.ts`.

## D-073 — The approvals and revocation routes are served by Hono; a write that a browser sends from another origin is refused on the whole console API

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Last write-route PR** (FOR-153): `GET /api/approvals`, `POST /api/approvals/<id>` (the registry audits a decision and refuses a second one), `POST /api/people/<id>/tokens/revoke`, `GET /api/tools/<tool>/user-secrets` and `DELETE …/user-secrets/<person>[/<NAME>]` (D-035, D-036; a value never comes out), all for admins, are route objects in `packages/platform-contract` (`approvals.ts`, `revocations.ts`) served from `apps/gateway/src/platform/approvals.ts`; `console-api/approvals.ts` and `revocations.ts` are deleted. After this, the legacy chain holds only the consent routes. Same JSON and status codes: `403` messages (`This is for admins.`, `Revocation is for admins.`), the old `400` message for a decision, `404` for an approval of another workspace, `409` for a second decision. A revocation is a `POST` with no body, so it takes no JSON check: `platformRoute` now adds the JSON-only guard only to a route that declares a body.
  2. **Origin check, decided**: a write (`POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`) on a route of the shell is refused with `403 FORBIDDEN` "Cross-origin writes are refused." when the browser says it comes from elsewhere: `Sec-Fetch-Site` is `cross-site` or `same-site` (a sibling workspace such as `globex.forgeron.app` is same-site and cross-origin), or, with no `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`, `Origin` is present and is not the workspace's own origin (`null` included). A client that sends neither header (the CLI, a script with a token) is not asked: it has no ambient cookie to abuse, so a Bearer token keeps working from anywhere. The check runs after the 401 and 403 (nobody signed in is a `401`, not a statement about origins) and before the JSON check; it is on every write route through `platformRoute`, and `GET` is untouched.
  3. **Why**: the console API was guarded by two things: a cross-site form cannot send `application/json`, and the session cookie. The JSON rule holds only as long as no route accepts another content type and no CORS header is ever added; an Origin check holds even then, and it costs nothing for the console (same origin) and for the CLI (no header). It is a second lock behind the JSON rule, not a replacement: the JSON rule stays.
  4. **Not the same as before**: any other method on these paths is `405` before anyone is asked who they are; a write from a browser at another origin or site is `403` even for an admin with a valid cookie.
  5. **Tests**: `describeRouteMatrix` now checks the Origin rule on every write route of the shell (refused: other origin, `null`, a sibling workspace, `cross-site`, `same-site`, with `401` first for nobody; let through: the page's own origin, `same-origin`, no header); `platform.test.ts` tests `crossOriginWrite` on its own; `platform/approvals.test.ts` adds the matrix for the six routes (`noBody` for the revocation `POST`); `console-api/workspace.test.ts` keeps the approvals cases and calls the app.
  6. **Left out, on purpose**: token issue and revocation (`/auth/token`, `/auth/token/revoke`), the CLI sign-in (`/auth/cli`) and deploy tokens are not console API routes (they sit under `/auth` and at the workspace origin) and are FOR-154's: the spike's step 7 mounts the CLI's platform routes on the same app. Nothing in `console-api/` is called "rotation".
- **Not verified**: the Origin check was not run against a real browser (the rule is tested on requests built with the headers a browser sends, which a page cannot set); the console was not run against this build; no real D1; the document was not checked with an OpenAPI validator.
- **Applied in**: FOR-153 (approvals, revocations, Origin check); `packages/platform-contract/src` (`approvals.ts`, `revocations.ts`), `apps/gateway/src/platform` (`approvals.ts`, `app.ts`, `route-matrix.ts`, `platform.test.ts`, `approvals.test.ts`), `apps/gateway/src/gateway.ts`.

## D-074 — The console calls the console API through the contract's typed client; its addresses are a schema-free table in the contract

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Step 7 of the spike's migration order, console part** (FOR-154): every call of the console to `/console-api` (the reads of `workspace`, `audit`, `members`, `access-matrix`, `approvals`, `secrets-status`, `manifest`, `identity-provider`, the tools, the catalog and the database explorer, and the writes of the members, invitations, domains, access matrix, approvals and identity provider) goes through `typedClient` / `typedSender` of `apps/console/src/api.ts`, which build the contract's `createClient` over the transport a screen is given. The path, the method, the parameters, the query and the body of a call are checked against the route at compile time (a wrong body or a renamed route fails to compile in the console); the answer is typed from the route.
  2. **The transport stays**: `Api.fetchJson` and `Api.sendJson` are still what screens, tests and the local harness hand in, so `ApiError` and `explain` are untouched (a refusal still throws), and the screens' view models still read their answers defensively (no schema runs in the browser). The typed client sits over them, it does not replace them: 145 console tests pass unchanged. The platform's own routes outside `/console-api` (`/promote`, `/rollback`, `/deploy`, called through `sendPlatform`) are the next step.
  3. **`addresses`** (`packages/platform-contract/src/addresses.ts`, exported by `@forgeron/platform-contract/client`): one entry per route, `addressOf<typeof route>({ method, path })`, so the console imports the routes as types only and no schema reaches the browser bundle (D-066). A test checks that every route of the contract has its entry with the route's own method and path, that the table has no extra entry, and that `client.ts`, `route.ts` and `addresses.ts` import nothing but types and `addressOf`. A new route is added to its module, exported from `index.ts` and given its entry in `addresses.ts`: the test fails until it is.
  4. **No import across apps**: the console imports only the contract package; `apps-do-not-import-each-other` holds (`bun run deps`).
- **Measured**: console production bundle (`vite build`, JS and CSS, gzip with Node's default level, one build each): 582,149 → 585,826 bytes raw, 181,965 → 182,853 gzip (+3,677 / +888: the address table and the client). No zod, no schema.
- **Not verified**: the console was not run against a real gateway or in a browser (tests and the production build only); the typed answers are not validated at run time.
- **Applied in**: FOR-154 (console); `apps/console/src` (`api.ts` and the screens' loaders and writers), `packages/platform-contract/src` (`addresses.ts`, `client.ts`, `database.ts`).

## D-075 — The routes of the CLI and CI are in the contract and mounted on the Hono app; the CLI calls them through the typed client

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Step 7, CLI part** (FOR-154): `/whoami`, `/deploy-tokens`, `/deploy` (`POST` and `DELETE`), `/promote`, `/rollback`, `/auth/token`, `/auth/token/revoke` and `/auth/cli/token` are route objects of `@forgeron/platform-contract` (`bearer.ts`), with their request and answers, and have an entry in `addresses`. The hand-written pages of `/auth/` (sign-in, `GET` and `POST /auth/cli`) are not routes of the API and stay as they are.
  2. **Mounted on the same app**: `createPlatform` mounts them (`apps/gateway/src/platform/bearer.ts`) and `createGateway` reaches them for these paths instead of calling the handlers itself. The app is told which path a request came on (`surface`: `console` for `/console-api/…`, `root` for these): a route answers on its own surface only, so `/console-api/whoami` is 404 as it was. Without `AUTH_SECRET` they are not reached, as before.
  3. **The handlers stay**: `handleDeploy`, `handlePromote`, `handleRollback`, `handleCreateDeployToken`, `handleIssueToken`, `handleRevokeToken` and `handleCliToken` keep their checks, because the order is part of what they promise (a deployment token is tried before a session; a method a path does not take is answered by the handler; 501 for a gateway with no Cloudflare account comes before the method check). These routes answer `{ error: { code, message } }`, the shape the CLI reads, not the console's `{ error, message }`: the contract says so (`PlatformErrorSchema`). Like the database explorer (D-070), they are not `app.openapi` routes and not in `/api/openapi.json`, which is the console's document served under `/console-api`.
  4. **The CLI** (`deploy`, `preview close`, `promote`, `rollback`, `token create`, `login`, `whoami`) calls them with `platformClient(endpoint, token)` (`apps/cli/src/platform-client.ts`), the contract's client with the Bearer token. It imports `@forgeron/platform-contract/client` only: no schema, no zod. The console's promote, rollback and close preview go through the same routes with `typedPlatform` over its `sendPlatform`. The client answers a body that is not JSON with a status and no body (it used to throw), which is what the CLI did before.
  5. **A bug the types found**: `forgeron preview close` read `closed` in the answer while the gateway says `removed`, so it always printed "had no preview". It reads `removed` now; the test's simulated gateway said `closed` and was corrected.
- **Measured** (one build each, 04/11/2026, origin/main against this change): gateway bundle 1,182,974 → 1,191,773 bytes raw, 256,902 → 258,451 gzip (no `node:*`, nothing external); console production bundle (`vite build`, JS and CSS, Node's default gzip level): 585,826 → 586,556 raw, 181,439 → 181,579 gzip (the address entries and `typedPlatform`; no schema). The CLI is not bundled.
- **Not changed on purpose**: no Origin check on these routes (D-073 covers `/console-api`): the console's session calls to `/promote`, `/rollback` and `/deploy` are as exposed as before. `forgeron secrets` still calls `/api/tools/…` at the root of the workspace (the gateway answers 404 there: the routes are under `/console-api`); it is not in this change.
- **Not verified**: no real gateway or Cloudflare account; the CLI tests run against HTTP servers that simulate the gateway.
- **Applied in**: FOR-154 (CLI); `packages/platform-contract/src/bearer.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/platform/bearer.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/gateway.ts`, `apps/cli/src`.

## D-076 — The consent routes are in the contract and mounted on the Hono app; the legacy chain and its fall-through tag are removed

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Last step of the spike's migration order** (FOR-154): the four consent routes of a delegated connector (start, callback, list, withdraw) are route objects (`consent.ts`) with `addresses` entries, registered in the document and mounted by `mountConsent`, which hands the request to `handleConsent`: a start is a `302`, the callback and the errors a person who follows the redirect sees are HTML pages, so the handler keeps its answers. Without `SECRETS_KEY` there is no consent and the path is a 404, as before.
  2. **The chain is gone**: after the Hono app the gateway had nothing left but `handleConsent` and the 404. The fall-through tag (`x-forgeron-unmatched`, D-064) is deleted: the app answers an unknown path with the gateway's own 404 (`notFound` binding: a page for a browser, JSON for any other client), so the answer is the same as before. The tests that needed to tell "not a route" from a 404 use a marker of their own (`NOT_A_ROUTE`, `test-app.ts`).
  3. **Kept**: the builders the routes use (`listTools`, `tool-pages`, the database explorer), the hand-written `/auth/*` pages and the personal secrets page.
  4. **Docs**: ARCHITECTURE.md, "The platform API": where the contract lives and how to add a route.
- **Measured** (one build each, 04/11/2026, origin/main against this change): gateway bundle 1,191,773 → 1,194,070 bytes raw, 258,451 → 259,021 gzip (no `node:*`, nothing external). The console and the CLI are not touched.
- **Not verified**: no real gateway or provider; the consent exchange is tested against simulated providers.
- **Applied in**: FOR-154 (end); `apps/gateway/src/platform`, `apps/gateway/src/gateway.ts`, `packages/platform-contract/src/consent.ts`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## D-077 — The gateway is composed in `compose.ts`: modules built once, a request context per request with a memoised person, and each area takes a narrow port

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **`apps/gateway/src/compose.ts`**: `createModules(env, options)` builds what lives as long as the Worker (the registry, the identity configuration, the one-time codes, the secret box, the resolver of the caller, the dispatcher, the sign-in switches); `createRequestModules(modules, parts, request, workspace)` builds what lives as long as one request: `who()` (the person, looked up at most once and only when a route asks) and the context of each area (`platformBindings`, `userSecrets`, `cliLogin`, `emailAuth`, `auth`). Explicit factories, no container (D-063).
  2. **`gateway.ts` is the table of routes**: it reads the path and picks the context; it builds nothing. `GatewayEnv` and `gatewayBindingsOf` moved to `env.ts` and are re-exported from `gateway.ts`, so the importers and the tests did not change.
  3. **Narrow ports**: the context of each area asks for `Pick<Registry, …>` of the methods it calls (identity: tokens, CLI login, caller, email sign-in, invitations; dispatch: personal secrets, connector tokens; consent; the console API builders). The compiler lists what is missing when a method is added.
  4. **Rule `gateway-compose-is-the-root`** (with a case in `scripts/deps-selftest.ts`): only `gateway.ts`, `compose.ts` and the tests import `compose.ts`.
- **Why**: `createGateway` built the same pieces in five places and every area took the whole registry. A reader could not see what an area depends on, and a test of one area needed the whole registry.
- **Rejected**: Awilix (D-063); a port type per area exported from the registry package (the registry's own modules already narrow what they ask of each other; a second layer of names there would be a change to the package for no gain).
- **Know**: `deploy/` and `promote/` still take the whole `Registry`: they pass it to `deployBuild`, `promoteVersion`, `rollbackEnvironment` and `closePreview`, which are in another package and ask for it. `PlatformBindings.registry` stays whole too: the Hono routes are many and each reads it through `c.env`. Narrowing them is a change to those packages, not to the gateway. The person is now shared by the two `platformBindings` calls of one request (only one is ever made), which changes nothing observable.
- **Not verified**: no deployed gateway; the behaviour is held by the gateway's own tests (unchanged) and the type check.
- **Applied in**: FOR-157; `apps/gateway/src/{compose,env,gateway}.ts`, the contexts of `identity/`, `dispatch/`, `consent/`, `console-api/`, `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`, `scripts/deps-selftest.ts`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## D-078 — `identity/` is split into `authn`, `sign-in` and `cli-login`; the tests that drive the whole gateway move to `integration/`

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **`identity/authn/`**: who is calling (`session`, `tokens`, `audience`, `authenticate`, `caller`, `issue`) and the primitives they share (`sealed`, `origin`, and the `Identity` type, moved out of `oidc.ts`). **`identity/sign-in/`**: `auth` (the OIDC and SAML routes), `email-auth`, `invitation`, `oidc`, `saml*`, `presets`. **`identity/cli-login/`**: `cli.ts`, the browser step of `forgeron login`. Tests moved with their subject.
  2. **Rules, each with a case in `scripts/deps-selftest.ts`**: `identity-authn-is-a-leaf` (authn imports neither of the other two) and `identity-sign-in-and-cli-login-are-apart`. The two rules of D-063 (`identity-is-below-dispatch-and-pages`, `identity-does-not-import-dispatch`) already cover the outside and still apply to every subfolder.
  3. **`integration/`**: the 15 `*.test.ts` that sat at the root of `apps/gateway/src` (the ticket counted 14) are moved there; the paths they build from (`../scripts/build`, `wrangler.jsonc`, the console folder) gain one level.
  4. **`gateway.ts` is not split further**: re-measured after FOR-154 and FOR-157 it is 96 lines (the table of routes), with `compose.ts` 481 and `env.ts` 128.
- **Why**: `identity/` held 18 files for three different jobs, and the one "who is calling" piece was imported by deploy, platform, views and playground next to OIDC and SAML code. The folders say which is which and the rules keep the arrows one way.
- **Rejected**: the rule name of the ticket, `identity-does-not-import-dispatch-or-pages`: identity renders pages (`pages/sign-in`, `pages/cli`, `pages/errors`) on purpose, so the rule would be false; the direction that holds (dispatch and pages never import identity) is `identity-is-below-dispatch-and-pages`. Splitting `compose.ts` by area now: it is one function per lifetime and nothing else changes it, so a split would add files without separating changes.
- **Know**: the `Identity` type is in `authn/identity.ts` and `oidc.ts` imports it, so `authn` needs nothing from `sign-in`. Nothing was renamed or rewritten: the files moved and their relative imports were rewritten by a script, then checked by the type check, the dependency check and the tests.
- **Not verified**: no deployed gateway; the behaviour is held by the existing tests, which are unchanged except for their import paths.
- **Applied in**: FOR-158; `apps/gateway/src/{identity,integration}`, `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`, `scripts/deps-selftest.ts`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## D-079 — The admin MCP is served at `/mcp` with a CLI token as Bearer; its tools call the platform's routes as the caller, and the audit lines say `via` the MCP

- **Date**: 04/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision** (FOR-107, `docs/sdk.md` §17):
  1. **Reach**: `POST /mcp` on a workspace origin, MCP over Streamable HTTP in its simplest form (one JSON answer per POST, no session, no stream; `initialize`, `ping`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`). Any other method keeps the JSON 404 the workspace gives its API paths. Without `AUTH_SECRET` it does not exist.
  2. **Credential**: a CLI token (`scp: cli`) sent as `Authorization: Bearer`, audience the workspace, not revoked, of a person still in the workspace (`caller.cliBearer`). A session cookie, a token issued for a tool and a deployment token are all refused: a cookie is what a page could make a browser send, and a tool's token is for that tool. OAuth for MCP clients such as a Claude connector is FOR-164.
  3. **One path with the console**: a tool is a function of a port, and the port calls the platform's Hono app in the process with the caller's identity (`identity()` and `bearer.person()` answer with the CLI token's person), so the role guard, the validation and the registry's own rules and audit lines are the console's. The folder `admin-mcp/` imports neither the registry nor another area of the gateway (rule `admin-mcp-goes-through-the-platform`, with two cases in the self-test): a shortcut would not compile through `bun run deps`.
  4. **The role is the person's, read at each call**: `tools/list` offers a person only the tools their role allows, `tools/call` refuses another with `403` before the call, and the platform's guard checks again. No tool takes a role, a person to act as or a caller; the schemas are strict, so an extra field is `400`.
  5. **Audit**: `PlatformBindings.via` is set to `VIA_FORGERON_MCP` for these calls and written by the registry in `via` for approvals, access-matrix changes, member roles, promotions and rollbacks (new optional `via` parameter of `setRoleAccessMany`, `setMemberRoles`, and `via` of `PromoteInput` and `RollbackInput`). Reads write nothing.
  6. **No secret**: no tool takes or returns a secret value (`secrets_status` is the route that gives the status); every answer goes through `redacted`, which blanks a field named `value`, `secret`, `password`, `token`, `clientSecret`, `apiKey` or `authorization`, as a second lock; a failure that is not a refusal is logged by tool name and answered as `500 FAILED` with nothing of it.
  7. **Tools**: `catalog_search`, `catalog_get`, `tools_list`, `tool_get` (member); `tool_versions`, `tool_changelog`, `secrets_status` (developer); `resources_pending`, `resources_approve`, `access_list`, `access_grant`, `access_revoke`, `tool_promote`, `tool_rollback` (admin). `destructiveHint` is on the five that change something; the others are `readOnlyHint`.
  8. **§17 amended**: `tool_changelog` is for developers (the route that carries it is); install, usage report, secret request and group grants are marked not yet available with their tickets (FOR-160 to FOR-165).
- **Choices inside the tools**: `access_grant` and `access_revoke` take a member (`person`, id or email, and `role`) or a cell (`tool`, `role`, `action`); a revoked cell is an explicit refusal (`allowed: false`), not a return to the manifest's default, which the console still does. `tool_promote` never sets `allowDestructive`; a migration that drops data stays in the CLI. `resources_approve` only approves; rejecting stays in the console.
- **Why**: the MCP is where an instruction slipped into a document could turn into an action, so it gets no path of its own to the registry and no more rights than the person has.
- **Rejected**: the stdio `forgeron mcp` (out of scope); accepting the session cookie (a browser can send it); an elevated service identity for the MCP; moving `/promote` and `/rollback` behind the platform guard (they keep their own checks and answer shape, D-075, and an admin's identity reaches them as the CLI's does).
- **Known, not changed**: `/promote` and `/rollback` name the actor by email in the audit line while the access lines name the person's id (as for the CLI). The token of a CLI login lives 30 days and cannot be revoked before except through the person's revocation (D-035). A person who is both a CLI user and an MCP client has one token for both.
- **Not verified**: no real MCP client (Claude) was connected, only JSON-RPC over the gateway in tests; no deployed gateway; hosting is simulated for the promotions.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/admin-mcp/`, `compose.ts`, `gateway.ts`, `identity/authn/caller.ts`, `platform/{app,access-matrix,approvals,members,bearer}.ts`, `deploy/promote.ts`, `packages/registry/src/{deploy.ts,modules/access,modules/members}`, `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`, `docs/sdk.md` §17.

## D-080 — A self-host preview is reached by a service binding the gateway adds to itself when the preview first serves; no per-version URL, Cloudflare Preview or HTTP token

- **Date**: 06/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: the redeploy of the gateway for each new branch is the design, not a problem to remove: a preview is a Worker of its own, bound as `TOOL_<WORKER>` on its first `activate` and unbound on `teardown` (D-017, D-029, D-032); later pushes to the branch redeploy nothing. The leads of FOR-20 are rejected: per-version URLs (public, production resources, no isolation, no Durable Object Workers), Cloudflare Previews (a service binding from a Preview reaches the production deployment only, so the gateway cannot select a Preview; public URL), an HTTP token (removes the premise of D-036 that only the gateway reaches a tool). Closes the "Still open" line of D-009 and the "Open decisions" row of `ARCHITECTURE.md`.
- **Proof**: a preview already runs on a real install: `/t/crm@fabriks-test-2` on demo.fabriks.cc, MCP `tools/list` answered 200 (checked by the parent session on 04/11/2026; not re-run for this decision). It proves that a preview Worker is bound to the gateway and served at `/t/<tool>@<branch>`. It does not prove: two previews deployed at the same moment, teardown, the time and size of a bind, behaviour near 500 Workers, or a gateway rollback after many binds.
- **Why**: see `docs/spikes/self-host-previews.md`: bounded cost (two redeploys per branch and tool), no binding limit documented for service bindings, the 500-Workers cap is hit first (`workers/platform/limits`, 2026-10-29).
- **Not done**: serializing concurrent binds, sweeping stale previews, showing the Worker budget, keeping a known-good gateway version (tickets proposed in the report).
- **Applied in**: `docs/spikes/self-host-previews.md`.

## D-081 — The template catalogue is the tools of the reserved workspace `@templates`; installing one copies its latest version into the workspace, audited, with its approvals pending

- **Date**: 06/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision** (FOR-160, `docs/sdk.md` §17; the launch list of templates stays FOR-30):
  1. **Where the catalogue lives**: in the platform D1, as the tools of a workspace named `@templates` (`TEMPLATE_WORKSPACE`; `@` cannot be part of an address, so no real workspace has that name and no workspace's `listTools` returns them). A template is published with the calls a deployment already uses: `createTool`, `recordBuild` (manifest, Worker, migrations, changelog), `storeDocs`, `publishVersion`. Its latest version is the one an installation copies. No new table for the catalogue.
  2. **What "install" creates**: `registry.installTemplate({ workspace, template, actor, via })` (new `templates` module, behind its `public.ts`) writes, in **one `d1.batch`**, the tool (same name as the template: its manifest names it and a deployment token is bound to that name), a copy of the build, its docs, the version under the same number, one pending approval per resource the template asks for (`diffResourceChanges` against nothing, as a first production deployment does), and the audit line. **No environment and no deployment**: nothing runs until the tool is promoted, and production waits for the approvals as it does for any version.
  3. **Why one batch, across tables**: a tool that exists without its approval requests would let a promotion skip them (`assertApproved` only looks at what is recorded). The docs content is copied by SQL (`INSERT … SELECT`) and never read into the Worker.
  4. **Audit**: a new kind `install` (migration `0022_audit_install`, the table rebuilt as in `0014`), `subject` `crm:1.2.0`, `detail` the template and the workspace, `via` set for the MCP.
  5. **Routes** (contract `templates.ts`, handler `platform/templates.ts`, typed-client addresses `listTemplates` and `installTemplate`), both **admin only**: `GET /api/templates` (name, latest version, the resources it asks for: the plan an admin reads before installing) and `POST /api/templates/{name}/install` (`201`; `404` no such template; `409` the workspace has a tool of that name). The actor is the caller; no argument names a person, a role or another workspace, so the install has the caller's rights and nothing more.
- **Rejected**:
  - **Templates read from `templates/` at install time**: the gateway is a Worker with no repository and no build step; a template is source, an install needs a built Worker.
  - **A `templates` table apart from tools and builds**: the same columns again, and a second path to publish, read and migrate a build.
  - **A flag on a tool in each workspace** (a "published as template" bit): it would make every tenant's tool a candidate for every other workspace.
  - **Deploying at install** (an environment, a URL): the install would then need hosting, secrets and the approvals at once. The tool is created in the state a first `forgeron deploy` leaves it, minus the environment; `tool_promote` is the next step.
  - **Calling `createTool`, `recordBuild`, `publishVersion` and `requestApproval` one after the other from the gateway**: a failure in the middle leaves a tool without its approvals (see 3).
  - **A name other than the template's**: the manifest and the deployment token name the tool; renaming would need a rewritten manifest. `forgeron install --name` of §17 is not possible yet.
  - **A cost estimate in the plan**: the plan gives the resources; a cost needs usage data that the platform does not hold (FOR-162).
- **Know**: nothing publishes a template into `@templates` yet: the catalogue is empty on a real install until a script or a route does it with the calls above (the tests do). The templates of `templates/` (FOR-30) are the content, built and published by that step. A template with a newer version than the installed tool's is not an update: updating an installed tool is not in this ticket.
- **Not verified**: no real install; tested on the in-memory D1 and the Hono app, not through the whole gateway or the admin MCP (FOR-161).
- **Applied in**: FOR-160; `packages/registry/src/modules/{templates,audit}`, `packages/platform-contract/src/templates.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/platform/templates.ts`.

## D-082 — The views keep the CSP sandbox; no dedicated views origin

- **Date**: 06/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: views stay under `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox allow-scripts allow-forms allow-popups allow-downloads` (D-016), on the workspace origin. The fallback of `sdk.md` §7 (`acme.forgeron-views.app`) is not taken. A view keeps no state in the browser (`localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, `indexedDB`, cookies and service workers throw under the sandbox): it asks an action. `/_f/call/<action>` answers CORS (`OPTIONS`, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, never `Allow-Credentials`) and stays authenticated by the audience Bearer only.
- **Why**: `docs/spikes/react-views-sandbox.md` (FOR-49): a real build of the dashboard template (React 19, `@forgeron/ui`, `@forgeron/sdk/react`) rendered under the real `VIEW_CSP`, called its actions with the view token, kept its URL parameters, downloaded a file, and still rendered under `script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'`. No cookie reached any request of the page. `@forgeron/ui` and `@forgeron/sdk` use no storage.
- **Rejected**: a dedicated views origin (a second certificate and cookie scope, and it would still need CORS for the calls); `allow-same-origin` (it removes the isolation of §7).
- **Known, not changed**: the pipeline around the sandbox is not finished: CORS on `/_f/call`, a mount entry in the build, a caller of `serveView`, CSS bundling, content-hashed entry names, renewal of the 15-minute token (tickets proposed in the report).
- **Not verified**: Chrome 156 headless only (no Firefox or Safari); popups to other sites, the clipboard and the other `@forgeron/ui` components were not run; no real gateway or Worker.
- **Applied in**: `docs/spikes/react-views-sandbox.md`, `sdk.md` §20.

## D-083 — The admin MCP asks for a secret with a single-use link to a console page; it never takes or sees the value

- **Date**: 06/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision** (FOR-163, `docs/sdk.md` §17):
  1. **Tool**: `secret_request` (developer) takes a tool, an environment (`preview` or `production`) and a secret name, and returns `{ url, expiresAt, note }`. Its input is strict (a `value` is refused) and it has no value in its output: the link only.
  2. **Link**: `https://<workspace>.<base>/secret-request#<token>`, 32 random bytes in base64url. The token is in the **fragment**, which a browser never sends to a server, so no gateway, proxy or CDN log holds it; the page reads it, removes it from the address bar and the history, and sends it in a body.
  3. **Record** (`secret_requests`, migration `0023`, secrets module): the SHA-256 hash of the token (never the token), the tool, the environment, the name, the person it was issued to, `expires_at`, `used_at` and a `claim`. It lives **15 minutes** and works **once**.
  4. **Routes** (`platform-contract/secret-requests.ts`): `POST /api/tools/{tool}/environments/{env}/secrets/{name}/request` (developer or admin; takes no body, answers `201 { url, expiresAt }`), `POST /api/secret-requests/lookup` (what a link is for, spends nothing) and `POST /api/secret-requests/fulfil` (sets the value). The last two are for any signed-in member: the link, not the role, is the right, and it is bound to one person.
  5. **Bound to the person**: lookup and fulfil answer `404` (the same body) for a link that is unknown, another person's, another workspace's or already used, and `410` for an expired one, so a link does not say that it exists to someone else and a wrong person does not spend it. They also need the **session** of that person: a CLI token of the same person (the admin MCP's credential) is refused with `401`, so that a program holding it cannot type a value in the person's place.
  6. **One batch**: fulfilling is one `d1.batch` of three statements: the claim (a conditional `UPDATE`: not used, the right person, not expired, with a fresh `claim`), the sealed secret and the audit line, the last two written **only if this batch holds the claim** (`INSERT … SELECT … WHERE EXISTS`, and `auditStatement` takes an optional `onlyIf`). Two parallel uses cannot both set a value, and a use never leaves a secret without its line, or a line without its secret. The value is sealed before the batch, so a registry without a key refuses before it spends the link.
  7. **Audit** (kind `access`, subject `secret:<NAME>`, detail `{ event: "secret-requested" | "secret-set", environment }`): one line when the link is asked for (with `via` "Forgeron MCP" when it came through the MCP) and one when the secret is set. Neither holds a value or a token. The kind `access` is reused: a new kind would rebuild the audit table.
  8. **Console**: `/secret-request` (`features/secrets`), reached by the link only (no sidebar item): it says which tool, environment and secret, takes the value in a masked field, sends it in a body and, on success, empties the field and shows no value.
- **Why**: the admin MCP is where an instruction slipped into a document could turn into an action, and a value typed in a conversation goes through Claude, its logs and its context. A link keeps the value between the person's browser and the gateway; the person is the one who types it.
- **Rejected**: the token in the path or the query (it reaches access logs and referrers); a link not bound to a person (anyone who sees it could set the value); a link that survives its first use, even a failed one that was the right person's (a use that fails after the claim, such as a database error, burns the link: a new one is cheap); a `value` argument on the MCP tool, even redacted; a new audit kind.
- **Known, not changed**: the registry takes `now` from its clock, so the expiry is the gateway's clock; a link that was spent by a batch that failed after the claim cannot happen (one batch), but a registry error before it leaves the link unspent. The existing `PUT …/secrets/{name}` (admin, value in the body) is unchanged and is not used by the MCP.
- **Not verified**: no browser was driven: the page is tested in jsdom, and the sign-in detour (a signed-out person opens the link, signs in, and must open it again, because sign-in does not bring them back to the fragment) was not tried; no deployed gateway; no real MCP client.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/secrets/{store,migrations,types}.ts`, `kernel/kernel.ts`, `migrations.golden.json`; `packages/platform-contract/src/secret-requests.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/platform/secret-requests.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/admin-mcp/tools.ts`; `apps/console/src/features/secrets/`; `docs/sdk.md` §17.

## D-084 — Keep the hand-written authentication; Better Auth is not adopted (FOR-149)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: authentication stays as written in `apps/gateway/src/identity/`. Better Auth is not adopted, in whole or in part, for now. The one partial lead of the spike (`@better-auth/oauth-provider` for FOR-107) is not pursued: D-013 already answers it and D-079 serves the admin MCP.
- **Why**: `docs/spikes/better-auth.md` (4 November 2026): the session and the code are bound to the workspace and to the asking browser (D-019, D-049), the SAML verifier has a structural wrapping defence with its own tests (D-050), and domain admission writes its audit line in the same batch (D-027, D-049). Better Auth does not cover these, adds a static `node:crypto` import and needs `nodejs_compat`, and multiplies the bundle by 4 to 5.
- **Rejected**: full adoption; adopting only its OAuth provider now.
- **Revisit when**: the number of tools makes the D-013 cache cost visible (about 100 tools), or an identity feature is needed that the spike lists as covered and we lack.
- **Applied in**: nothing changes in code; `docs/spikes/better-auth.md` stays the evidence.

## D-085 — Licence: Apache-2.0 for what a tool imports and runs, AGPL-3.0 for the gateway and the console

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active, to be confirmed by the copyright holder before the first public release
- **Decision** (FOR-32): the root `LICENSE` is Apache-2.0 and covers the SDK, the runtime, the compiler, the manifest, the hosting, the registry, the platform contract, the playground, the UI, the connectors, the CLI, the site and the templates. `apps/gateway` and `apps/console` carry their own `LICENSE`, AGPL-3.0-only. Each `package.json` has a `license` field.
- **Why**: the white paper (Business model) earns revenue on hosting and governance and names the two risks: a licence too strict stops adoption, a licence too permissive lets a third party resell the cloud. Tool code and templates import the SDK, the UI and the connectors, so those cannot be copyleft: nobody would write a company tool under a licence that reaches it. The gateway and the console are the platform itself, the part that is sold as Forgeron Cloud: AGPL obliges anyone who modifies it and offers it as a service to publish their changes, and does not touch a company that installs it unmodified on its own account (self-host).
- **Rejected**: MIT or Apache-2.0 for everything (leaves the cloud open to resale); AGPL for everything (reaches tool code through the SDK); a source-available licence (not open source, against the white paper's promise).
- **Not done**: no copyright holder is named in the files (the standard texts are unchanged); a contributor licence agreement is not decided, so contributions are accepted under the licence of the folder they touch. A legal review was not made.
- **Applied in**: `LICENSE`, `apps/gateway/LICENSE`, `apps/console/LICENSE`, every `package.json`, `README.md`.

## D-086 — Public npm names: `forgeron` for the CLI, `@forgeron/*` for the packages

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision** (FOR-33): the CLI is published as `forgeron`, so that `npx forgeron` works; the SDK and the other packages as `@forgeron/sdk`, `@forgeron/runtime`, and so on, as the repository already names them. Trusted publishing from GitHub Actions is the only way to publish (D-005's release flow).
- **Why**: `npm view` on 09/11/2026 answered 404 for `forgeron`, `@forgeron/sdk` and `@forgeron/cli`, so the names looked free then. A 404 for a scope name does not prove that the `@forgeron` organisation is free to create: that is checked when it is created.
- **Not done**: no name is reserved and nothing is published (FOR-96, FOR-93). Both need the owner's npm account. See D-122 for the build and the release.
- **Applied in**: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (open decisions).

## D-087 — Staging lives on its own registrable domain, not on a second-level subdomain of production

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision** (FOR-21): staging uses a separate domain, for example `forgeron-staging.app`, with a wildcard `*.forgeron-staging.app`. The gateway's route is `*.<domain>/*`, as in production.
- **Why**: Cloudflare's universal certificate covers one level of subdomain, so `acme.staging.forgeron.app` is not covered. A separate registrable domain keeps the same one-label workspace rule in staging as in production and needs no paid advanced certificate.
- **Rejected**: `*.staging.forgeron.app` with Advanced Certificate Manager (a recurring cost and a staging that differs from production).
- **Not done**: the domain is not bought and not on the Cloudflare account; `FOR-76` (deploy to staging and production) cannot start until it is. The name `forgeron-staging.app` is a placeholder the owner may change.
- **Applied in**: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` ("Platform environments", open decisions).

## D-088 — The launch catalogue is the four templates of `templates/`, maintained by the core team

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Replaced by D-114
- **Decision** (FOR-30): the launch catalogue holds `crm`, `odoo-gateway`, `inventory` (the data hub) and `dashboard`, the four that CI builds and previews (D-045). The core team maintains them; a template is added only with a test and a place in `templates-preview.yml`.
- **Why**: they are the SDK's integration tests, so they are kept working anyway; the white paper lists exactly these (CRM, contact records, data hub, API gateway, dashboard). A catalogue of tools nobody tests would break silently.
- **Not done**: no individual owner is named (that is for the team); no template is published into `@templates` on a real install yet (D-081).
- **Applied in**: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## D-089 — Connectors in order: Microsoft 365, Google Workspace, then Odoo

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision** (FOR-31): Microsoft 365 first, Google Workspace second (both exist, against simulated providers), then Odoo. Slack and GitHub come after the pilot.
- **Why**: the first internal user of Forgeron (gate 1) signs in with Microsoft and needs the Odoo gateway, so Microsoft sign-in and the per-person Odoo key come first; the white paper names Microsoft 365 and Google first.
- **Not done**: no connector ran against a real tenant (FOR-173).

## D-090 — Texts about self-host previews that D-080 made stale are corrected in place (FOR-166)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: D-009's and D-045's "Still open" and "Not verified" lines about previews are left as written (this log is never rewritten) and are superseded by D-080 and by `docs/spikes/self-host-previews.md`. The living documents are corrected: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the open-decisions row about previews, and the Hosting modes sentence) and `docs/self-host.md` (the `/mcp` line, now served by D-079, and the wording of "Binding limits").
- **Why**: the spike lists these inconsistencies; a reader who trusts them looks for a problem that was settled.

## D-091 — A group is not a grantable subject: `access_grant` gives roles to members and cells of the matrix (FOR-165)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**: `access_grant` and `access_revoke` act on a member's workspace roles (`setMemberRoles`) and on the cells of a tool's role × action matrix. There is no `group:<name>` subject, and `docs/sdk.md` §17 no longer promises one.
- **Why**: groups exist only as the identity provider's `groupRoles`, applied once, at a person's first sign-in (D-027). Making them a subject would mean a groups table, memberships kept in step with the provider, and a second path to a role that the audit log and the access matrix must both explain. Nothing asks for it: the matrix already grants an action to a role, and a role is given to people by the provider's mapping or by an administrator.
- **Rejected**: a `groups` registry module with its own route (a second source of truth for who holds a role); resolving the provider's groups at each call (a network call on the path of every request, and the token does not carry them).
- **Revisit when**: a customer needs a group to stay in step with its provider after the first sign-in. That is a sign-in feature (refresh the roles at each sign-in), not a new subject.
- **Applied in**: `docs/sdk.md` §17.

## D-092 — The admin MCP installs a template in two steps: a plan bound to the person, then only the plan's identifier (FOR-161)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision** (`docs/sdk.md` §17):
  1. **Tools**: `template_list` (admin, reads), `tool_install_plan` (admin; creates no tool) and `tool_install` (admin, `destructiveHint`). `tool_install` takes the plan's identifier and nothing else: no template name, no workspace, no person.
  2. **Plan** (`install_plans`, migration `0024`, templates module): the workspace, the template, the **version it showed**, the person, an expiry of **15 minutes** and a `used_at`. The identifier is a random UUID; the binding to the person and the workspace is what protects it, as for a secret request (D-083).
  3. **Refusals**: a plan that does not exist, is another person's or another workspace's gets the same `404`; one already spent gets `409 PLAN_USED`; an expired one `410 PLAN_EXPIRED`; one whose template has a **newer version** than the plan showed gets `409 PLAN_STALE`, so what an admin confirmed is what is installed. The conditional `UPDATE … WHERE used_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now` spends the plan before anything is created, so two parallel uses cannot both install.
  4. **Routes** (contract `templates.ts`): `POST /api/templates/{name}/install-plan` and `POST /api/install-plans/{plan}/install`, admin only, on the same guard as every route of the platform. The direct `POST /api/templates/{name}/install` (D-081) stays for the console: a person who clicks has already seen the screen.
  5. **Audit**: the install keeps its `install` line (D-081); making a plan writes one `access` line, subject `install-plan:<template>`, `via` set for the MCP. No line holds a secret: an install carries none.
- **Why**: the admin MCP is where an instruction slipped into a document could become an action (D-083). Two steps with an identifier that only the same administrator can spend means a hidden instruction cannot install a tool in one call, and cannot install a different thing than the one shown.
- **Rejected**: a signed, stateless plan (cannot be spent once); a `confirm: true` argument on one tool (a model can fill it); a plan identifier that is a secret token (the binding to the person already makes it useless to anyone else); giving the plan back after a failure (a new plan is cheap, and a retry cannot install twice).
- **Known, not changed**: a plan spent and then refused because the workspace gained a tool of that name in the meantime is not given back. Plans that expired stay in the table (no sweep: the table is small, and a sweep is one `DELETE` when it matters).
- **Not verified**: no real MCP client and no deployed gateway; tested through the whole gateway with a real registry and CLI tokens.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/templates/`, `packages/platform-contract/src/templates.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/platform/templates.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/admin-mcp/tools.ts`, `docs/sdk.md` §17.

## D-093 — Usage is one counter per workspace, tool, person and UTC day; the admin MCP reads it with `usage_report` (FOR-162)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **What is counted**: an authenticated call that reached a tool, in the place where a seat is counted (D-024): the dispatcher, once identity says who called and the tool is found. One row per workspace, tool name, person id and UTC day, with a number of calls (`usage_daily`, migration `0025`, new `usage` module). A call a tool makes on its own behalf (`tool:<name>`) and an anonymous one are not counted.
  2. **What is not kept**: no path, no action name, no payload, no address, no user agent. Tool names, not ids: a deleted tool keeps its past counts.
  3. **How long**: rows are kept as they are; there is no sweep. A row is one per person, tool and day, so a workspace of 100 people on 10 tools writes at most 1,000 rows a day. A sweep (`DELETE … WHERE day < ?`) is a later ticket if the table ever matters.
  4. **Read**: `GET /api/usage?from&to` (contract `workspace.ts`), admin only, a period of at most a year of UTC days (30 days to today by default), answered by tool (calls, distinct people) and by person (calls, with the member's name and email, empty if the person left). The MCP tool `usage_report` calls that route and is read only.
  5. **Failure**: counting never blocks a call (as for seats): an error is swallowed in the dispatcher.
- **Why**: the white paper sells governance; an administrator needs to know which tools are used and by whom, and per-seat billing (D-024) already counts people. A counter per day is the smallest thing that answers "which tool, how much, who" without becoming a second audit log: the audit log holds what changed, this holds how much was used.
- **Rejected**: logging each call (a row per request, a payload question, a growth question); counting inside the tool's Worker (a tool could lie, and the gateway already knows); reusing `seats` (a month and a person, no tool).
- **Cost, stated**: one more D1 write per authenticated call to a tool, next to the seat write. Not measured on a real D1. If it shows in latency, the two writes can share one batch or the counter can move to a Durable Object with a periodic flush.
- **Not done / not verified**: the console has no usage screen; the numbers were not compared with a real workspace's traffic; the unique-people count over a period is per tool, so a person who used two tools counts once in each.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/usage/`, `packages/platform-contract/src/workspace.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/dispatch.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/compose.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/platform/workspace.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/admin-mcp/tools.ts`, `docs/sdk.md` §17.

## D-094 — An MCP client signs in to `/mcp` with OAuth 2.1 written in the gateway: signed clients, codes and refresh tokens, no new table (FOR-164)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision** (`docs/sdk.md` §17):
  1. **Where**: the workspace origin serves `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` (RFC 9728, also at `/mcp`), `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` (RFC 8414), `POST /oauth/register` (RFC 7591), `/oauth/authorize`, `POST /oauth/token` and `POST /oauth/revoke` (RFC 7009). A `401` from `/mcp` carries `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer resource_metadata=…`, which is how a client finds them. The code is `identity/mcp-oauth/`, a sibling of `sign-in` and `cli-login` under the same dependency rules.
  2. **Clients**: public only (`token_endpoint_auth_method: none`), registered dynamically. A `client_id` is a value **signed by the gateway** (`fgo.`) holding the workspace, the registered redirect addresses (1 to 5; `https`, or `http` on `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` or `[::1]`; no credentials, no fragment) and the name the client gave. Nothing is stored. An address that is not one of the registered ones gets an error page and no redirect.
  3. **The person says yes**: `/oauth/authorize` needs the session cookie (a signed-out person is sent to `/auth/login?next=`, as for `forgeron login`) and shows a page with the client's name, the address the answer goes to and what the access is; **nothing is handed over without a click** (D-053), and the POST must come from the page (D-049). The client's name is a claim anyone can make, and the page says so by showing the address beside it. PKCE `S256` is required; `resource`, if sent, must be this workspace's `/mcp`; the only scope is `mcp`.
  4. **Tokens**: the code is signed, lasts 60 seconds, names the client, the address and the challenge, and is spent once (`cli_codes`). The access token is a platform token (`fgt.`, audience `<workspace>/platform`) with the scope **`mcp`**, **one hour**. The refresh token is signed (`fgr.`), lasts **30 days**, **rotates** (each use spends it and returns a new pair) and carries a family id.
