Every route other than `/mcp` asks for no scope or for `cli`, so an `mcp` token is refused there; `/mcp` accepts `mcp` and `cli` (`caller.mcpBearer`).
  5. **Revocation**: the access tokens of a connection share its family id as their `jti`, so revoking the family (`token_revocations`, D-035) ends the access tokens and the refresh token at once. `/oauth/revoke` does it for a refresh token or an access token; a refresh token used twice ends the connection (the app or a thief holds a copy); revoking all of a person's tokens, or removing them from the workspace, ends it too. The role is read at each call (D-079), so a change of role applies at once.
  6. **No elevation**: the person is the one who clicked; the admin MCP's tools are the platform's routes called as that person. A member who connects sees the member tools and nothing more.
- **Why**: the CLI token works for a developer in a terminal but a Claude connector cannot ask for it: it needs an authorization server it can discover. Writing it in the gateway keeps D-084 (hand-written authentication, a code and a verifier bound to the workspace and the browser) and needs **no migration**: the pieces that must be remembered (a code spent, a connection revoked) already have their tables.
- **Rejected**: `@cloudflare/workers-oauth-provider` (D-013's proof stores its state in KV and wraps the Worker's handler; the gateway is a Hono app over D1, and the library fixes its resources at construction); `@better-auth/oauth-provider` (D-084); a client table (a signed client is the same thing without a write); confidential clients and client secrets (a connector is a public client, and a secret in it is not a secret); long-lived access tokens without refresh (a stolen one would last); accepting the session cookie on `/mcp` (a browser can be made to send it, D-079); an `mcp` token that works on the CLI's routes.
- **Known, not changed**: anyone can register a client at a workspace origin (the page shows the name as a claim, with the address; the consent click is the control); registration has no rate limit of its own; the metadata and token endpoints answer no CORS preflight (a server-side client does not need one; a browser-only client would); one connection per refresh family: authorizing the same client again makes a second one, it does not replace the first; no screen lists or ends a person's connections (the revoke endpoint and the person-wide revocation exist); the signed `client_id` has no expiry.
- **Not verified**: **no real MCP client (Claude) was connected**; the flow is played step by step through the whole gateway in `integration/gateway-mcp-oauth.test.ts` with a simulated session. What Claude sends exactly (the order of its requests, whether it uses `resource`, the redirect address it registers) comes from the MCP specification, not from a trace. The consent page was not seen in a browser.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/identity/mcp-oauth/`, `apps/gateway/src/pages/mcp-oauth.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/identity/authn/caller.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/gateway.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/compose.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/admin-mcp/server.ts`, `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`, `docs/sdk.md` §17, `docs/self-host.md`.

## D-095 — OAuth also signs an MCP client in to a tool's MCP: `resource` names `/t/<tool>/mcp`, the token is for that tool with the `mcp` scope (FOR-171, ticket 1)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · extends D-094
- **Decision**:
  1. **Resources**: `/oauth/authorize` takes `resource` (RFC 8707) as the workspace's admin MCP (`…/mcp`, the default) or the MCP of one tool that exists in the workspace, `…/t/<tool>/mcp`, with or without a preview branch (`…/t/<tool>@<branch>/mcp`). Any other address, a tool that does not exist, another workspace and a tool named `platform` (the audience of the admin MCP) are refused with an error page and no redirect. The resource is in the code and in the refresh token; a `resource` sent to the token endpoint must be the same (`invalid_target`).
  2. **Discovery**: `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/t/<tool>/mcp` (and with `@<branch>`) describes that tool's MCP and points at the same authorization server; a tool that does not exist has none (`404`). A `401` on a tool's `/mcp` carries `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer resource_metadata=…` for that document. **The gateway sets it and deletes any `WWW-Authenticate` the tool sent**: a tool says nothing about where a person signs in.
  3. **Token**: audience `<workspace>/<tool>`, scope `mcp`, one hour, renewed by the rotating refresh token, family id as `jti` (D-094). The consent page names the tool.
  4. **Where it works**: `createCaller.identify` accepts an `mcp`-scoped token **only on `/t/<tool>/mcp` and `/t/<tool>@<branch>/mcp`, and only if its audience is that tool**. On the tool's API, CLI, secrets page, on another tool, on `/mcp` of the workspace and on every platform route it is nobody. A preview is still limited to administrators and developers by the dispatcher.
  5. **The admin MCP wants the platform's audience**: `mcpBearer` now requires `<workspace>/platform` for both the CLI and the `mcp` scope. Before, it checked only the workspace, so a token for a tool would have been accepted as the person at `/mcp`; it is closed by this change and a test covers both directions.
  6. **A tool that is deleted** gets no new token: a refresh for it answers `invalid_target`; an access token of one hour already issued dies at its expiry or with the connection's revocation.
- **Why**: the Odoo gateway's use is a person connecting Claude to `/t/odoo/mcp`, not to the admin MCP; and the setup asked at connection (`docs/spikes/tool-setup-oauth.md`) needs the OAuth step to know which tool it is for. The tool's own Worker is unchanged: it still receives the person in `x-forgeron-user` from the gateway.
- **Rejected**: one token for several tools (D-013: a token is for one resource); an `mcp` token valid on every path of its tool (a client that only needs MCP should not hold an API token); a challenge written by the tool; keeping `POST /auth/token` as the only way to a tool's MCP (it stays for the CLI and API, but Claude cannot use it).
- **Known, not changed**: a tool named `platform` can still be created and its CLI-scoped traffic shares the audience name (D-028); only the OAuth resource refuses it. The `x-forgeron-user` roles are the workspace's (D-036). One connection per client and resource.
- **Not verified**: no real MCP client; the tool in the tests is a stub Worker that answers who the gateway says the caller is, not the real runtime; the consent page for a tool was not seen in a browser.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/identity/mcp-oauth/mcp-oauth.ts`, `identity/authn/caller.ts`, `dispatch/dispatch.ts`, `pages/mcp-oauth.ts`, `gateway.ts`, `docs/sdk.md` §7 and §17, `docs/self-host.md`.

## D-096 — A tool declares a `setup`: plain fields in the manifest, secret fields as `resources.userSecrets` (FOR-171, ticket 2)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · first step of `docs/spikes/tool-setup-oauth.md`
- **Decision**: `forgeron.config.ts` takes `setup: { fields, check? }`, with the helpers `field.text`, `field.choice` and `field.secret` from `@forgeron/sdk`. At build, a **secret field moves into `resources.userSecrets`** (same name, label, help, why); the **plain fields stay in the manifest's `setup.fields`**, each with an explicit `required`. A secret declared both in `setup` and in `resources.userSecrets` is a build error; `resources.userSecrets` alone is unchanged. The manifest schema refuses a `check` that is not an action of the tool, a field name that is not `camelCase`, a choice with no option and a `secret` in `setup.fields`. The manifest diff has `setup.field-added` (minor), `setup.field-removed` (patch), `setup.field-changed` (minor when made required or of another type, else patch) and `setup.check-changed` (patch), none of which asks the admin to approve anything.
- **Why**: a secret already has everything the spike wants for it (approval of a new request, sealed storage, a header to the call, revocation: D-036), and the diff already treats it as a resource to approve. Keeping it in `resources.userSecrets` means a tool cannot dodge that approval by moving a secret to `setup`, and nothing downstream changes. Only what is new, the plain values and the check, needs a place in the manifest.
- **Decisions of the spike taken here**, on its recommendations, as the owner asked for autonomy on 2026-11-09 and to be confirmed: setup is per tool and per person, never per environment; plain values are kept in clear (a later ticket); `check` is optional and internal. The web as the only place (decision 1) and the removal of the CLI prompt are not applied by this change.
- **Rejected**: the secret fields in the manifest's `setup` (a second place to approve, store and diff them); an `order` key (plain fields come first, then the secrets, in declaration order); a `required: false` secret (a secret a tool asks for is required, as today).
- **Not done**: everything that makes use of the declaration: `user_setup` storage, `x-forgeron-user-setup`, `ctx.user.setup`, the page, the OAuth step, the `check` call, the CLI change (tickets 3 to 7 of the spike). The Odoo template now declares its two per-person secrets through `setup`; its manifest is the same as before.
- **Applied in**: `packages/manifest/src/schema.ts`, `diff.ts`; `packages/sdk/src/config.ts`; `packages/compiler/src/manifest.ts`; `templates/odoo-gateway/forgeron.config.ts`; `docs/sdk.md` §3 and §10.

## D-097 — A tool's plain setup values are kept in clear per tool and person, handed in `ctx.user.setup`, asked at `/t/<tool>/setup` (FOR-171, tickets 3 and 4)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · second step of `docs/spikes/tool-setup-oauth.md`, after D-096
- **Decision**:
  1. **Storage**: `user_setup` (migration `0026`, secrets module): one row per tool, person and name, the value **in clear**. Not per environment (FOR-174). Removing a member deletes their `user_setup` **and `user_secrets`** rows for every tool of the workspace in the same batch as the member (`removeMember`): until now a removed person's sealed secrets stayed in the table.
  2. **Audit**: setting values writes one `access` line, subject `setup:set`, detail the **names** only, never a value, in the same batch.
  3. **Header**: the gateway adds `x-forgeron-user-setup` (JSON of the caller's own values, `{}` when none) to every call to a tool it knows; the dispatcher drops it from what a caller sends (a test forges one). The built Worker reads it into `platform.userSetup` only when the tool declares a setup **and** the header is there; without it (a tool run on its own, in a test) the plain fields are not asked.
  4. **In the action**: `ctx.user.setup` (`ContextUser` in the SDK). A scheduled task is the tool and has `{}`. `createTestTool({ as: { setup } })` gives a person's values.
  5. **First use**: a required plain field with no value fails the call with `428 SECRET_REQUIRED`, **together with any missing secret**, one `inputUrl`: `/setup` when the tool has plain fields, `/secrets` otherwise (so a tool without a setup is unchanged). `missing` lists the plain fields (name, label, help) then the secrets.
  6. **The page**: `/t/<tool>/setup` and `/t/<tool>/secrets` are one handler (`handleSecretsInput`), also at a preview address. It shows the plain fields (text, or a select for a choice, prefilled with the person's value) then the secrets (as before: password fields, "Set", never the value). A form post checks **every** value first (a required field with none stored, a choice outside its options, a length) and keeps nothing if one is wrong (400, the form again with the message at the field); a JSON post (the CLI) checks only what it sends. An empty field means "no change".
  7. **Admin routes**: `GET /api/tools/{tool}/user-setup` (people with their name, email and values), `DELETE …/user-setup/{person}` and `…/{person}/{name}`, admin only, on the guard of the revocation routes.
- **Why**: the values are not secrets, and an admin who supports a person needs their first name or team; sealing them would hide them for nothing and make the page unable to prefill. Putting the plain fields and the secrets behind one `428` and one link means a person goes to one place once.
- **Rejected**: sealing the plain values (spike decision 2); a header only when the person has values (a tool could not tell "no values yet" from "no gateway"); a separate page for the plain fields; refusing the whole form when a secret is empty (an empty secret means keep).
- **Personal data**: first and last names are personal data of the workspace's people. They are deleted with the member and clearable by an admin, never in the audit log or any other log. How long a customer keeps a person's data and what it tells its people is the customer's and the owner's to confirm.
- **Not done**: the page at the OAuth connection and the connectors as steps (tickets 5), the `check` call (6), the CLI prompt removal (7), a console screen; `check` is declared and compiled, nothing calls it. Proven in tests against the in-memory D1 and the real runtime behind the gateway; no browser, no real Worker.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/secrets/` (`0026_user_setup`, `readUserSetup`, `writeUserSetup`, `listUserSetup`, `clearUserSetup`), `modules/members/store.ts`; `packages/runtime/src/{engine,secrets,types}.ts`; `packages/sdk/src/{context,testing/tool}.ts`; `packages/compiler/src/build.ts`; `packages/platform-contract/src/revocations.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/{dispatch,user-secrets}.ts`, `gateway.ts`, `platform/approvals.ts`; `docs/sdk.md` §10.

## D-098 — The OAuth step sends a person through the tool's setup page before the click, and issues no token for a tool whose setup is incomplete (FOR-171, ticket 5)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · third step of `docs/spikes/tool-setup-oauth.md`
- **Decision**:
  1. **At `/oauth/authorize`**, for a tool resource (D-095), once the person has a session: if the tool's latest version declares a setup and the person still has a required plain field with no value or a declared secret that is not set (`missingSetup` of `@forgeron/runtime`, the rule the `428` uses), a `GET` answers `302` to `/t/<tool>/setup?next=<the authorization request>`. The admin MCP, a tool with no setup and a tool with no published version are not affected.
  2. **The setup page keeps the way back**: it accepts `next` only if it is a path of this origin that starts with `/oauth/authorize?` (a short whitelist of characters, 2,000 at most); any other value is ignored, so the page is not an open redirect. The form keeps `next` in its action, and once the values are saved the page offers a "Continue" link to it. Back at the authorization step the setup is complete, so the person gets the consent page and its click (D-094).
  3. **No token without the setup**: a `POST` that allows the app while the setup is incomplete is refused with `400` and no code. The check is not a security control (the tool's own `428` still asks at the first call); it keeps the promise that a person is onboarded as they connect.
  4. **Why a redirect and not the form on the consent page**: the setup page already validates, stores, audits and handles secrets (D-036, D-097); a second form on the consent page would repeat all of it.
- **Rejected**: the setup fields on the consent page; a `next` that may be any same-origin path; a setup that is optional at the OAuth step; refusing at the token endpoint (the person is then past the browser step).
- **Not done**: the connectors as steps of the page (a delegated connector's consent still comes at the first call, as a `428`), the `check` call, the CLI prompt removal, a console screen. A person is sent through the setup page by a redirect: a client that does not follow the browser's navigation, or a person who closes the tab, ends the dance; the app starts it again.
- **Not verified**: no real MCP client, no browser; played in `integration/gateway-mcp-oauth.test.ts` with a session cookie and a stub tool Worker.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/identity/mcp-oauth/mcp-oauth.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/user-secrets.ts`; `packages/runtime/src/secrets.ts` (`missingSetup`, `next`); `docs/sdk.md` §10.

## D-099 — Per-person secrets may be carried sealed in the OAuth token, as an opt-in `keep: "token"` of a secret field; not started (FOR-172)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Replaced by D-103 (the owner kept the stored model on 2026-11-09). Was: Proposed: the direction was the owner's (oral, 2026-11-07), the design is `docs/spikes/secrets-in-token.md`, and five choices in it are the owner's to confirm before any code
- **Decision**: a secret field may declare where its value rests, `keep: "platform"` (today, D-036, the default) or `keep: "token"`. A token-borne secret is **sealed** (AES-GCM under a key derived from `SECRETS_KEY`, bound to the token's `jti`, audience and field name) into the claims of the access and refresh tokens of the person's OAuth connection, typed on the **consent page** and carried by the authorization code, re-sealed at each refresh and opened by the gateway at each call to fill the same header as today. The tool's code is unchanged (`ctx.userSecrets`). Nothing is written to the registry.
- **Why**: the platform's database then holds none of those secrets, so a leak of it, even with `SECRETS_KEY`, reveals none, and a person who leaves leaves nothing to delete.
- **What it costs, stated**: an admin cannot see or clear such a secret; changing one means connecting again in every client; `SECRETS_KEY` needs a previous key to be rotated without ending every connection; it does not work for the CLI and API, nor for event handlers and tasks; a token becomes a larger bearer credential (see the spike).
- **To confirm by the owner** (recommendations in the spike): MCP only for those fields (the Odoo template's `cli: true` would change); the secret typed on the consent page, not the setup page; opt-in per field with the platform as the default; a `SECRETS_KEY_PREVIOUS`; `ODOO_API_KEY` as the first and only token-borne field.
- **Rejected**: a short-lived stored record keyed by the token (it is the stored model with a shorter life); carrying it in the authorization code only (a code lives 60 seconds); making it the default (it would break the CLI, the admin's list and the rotation for every tool).
- **Not done**: everything. No code, no ticket created. The seven tickets are listed in the spike; the first, `SECRETS_KEY_PREVIOUS`, is useful on its own.
- **Applied in**: `docs/spikes/secrets-in-token.md`.

## D-100 — `SECRETS_KEY_PREVIOUS`: a value sealed under a replaced key still opens (FOR-172, ticket 1)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · useful on its own, and the first ticket of `docs/spikes/secrets-in-token.md`
- **Decision**: `createSecretBox(key, previous?)` opens a value with the current key, then with each previous one, and seals with the current key only. The gateway reads the previous keys from `SECRETS_KEY_PREVIOUS` (comma-separated, as `AUTH_SECRET_PREVIOUS`, D-035) and passes them to the registry's box, which also serves the identity configuration. A previous key that is not 32 bytes fails at startup. A value that no listed key opens fails as before; the slot is still the additional data, so a value moved to another slot does not open.
- **Why**: until now replacing `SECRETS_KEY` made every stored secret unreadable (`docs/self-host.md`), so the key could not be rotated, which is the first thing a security review asks. The tokens' own key has had its overlap since D-035.
- **Not done**: **re-sealing**. A value is sealed under the new key only when it is saved again, so the previous key cannot be dropped until every value has been typed or saved again (the sealed values are in `secrets`, `user_secrets`, the identity provider's client secret, the connectors' client secrets and the sealed refresh tokens of connections: five places in three modules). A re-sealing command is a later ticket. The install does not set `*_PREVIOUS` for you: set it with the dashboard or the API, as for `AUTH_SECRET_PREVIOUS`, which `self-host.md` now lists too.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/secret-box.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/compose.ts`, `env.ts`; `docs/self-host.md`; tests in `secret-box.test.ts` and `integration/gateway-tokens-secrets.test.ts`.

## D-101 — The gateway asks a tool's setup `check` before it keeps what a person gave; the check is on no interface (FOR-171, ticket 6)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · fourth step of `docs/spikes/tool-setup-oauth.md`
- **Decision**:
  1. **The action**: `setup.check` of a tool that declares one. At build, its `interfaces` is `[]` whatever the tool wrote: MCP, API and CLI never list or call it (a test calls it as an API action and gets 404).
  2. **The call**: when a person submits values at `/t/<tool>/setup` and the tool has a check, the gateway sends `POST /_f/setup-check` to the tool's **production** Worker with the person's identity headers (the gateway's own, from the person it identified), the marker header `x-forgeron-setup-check: 1` and a body `{ setup, secrets }`: the values given now over the ones already kept. The marker is **dropped by the dispatcher from what callers send**, and the runtime answers the path only with it, so reaching `/t/<tool>/_f/setup-check` from outside is a 404 (tested).
  3. **In the action**: `ctx.user.setup` and `ctx.userSecrets` are those values; **no access level is asked** (the platform is the caller, so a person with no role yet can still be onboarded); first-use checks and delegated connector tokens are skipped. It returns `{ errors: { field: "why" } }` to refuse, or nothing. A `ToolError` of `INVALID` or `FORBIDDEN` refuses with its message; any other failure, a tool that is down or an answer that is not 2xx all give "The check could not be completed. Try again." and **nothing is kept** (fail closed).
  4. **On the page**: a refusal shows "Not saved" and each message at its field (secret fields too), keeps the plain values the person typed, **never sends a secret back to the browser**, and answers `400` with `{ error: { message, fields } }` to a JSON post. Nothing is written, not the good fields either. The check is not run when nothing was given.
  5. **No Worker to ask** (the tool has no production deployment): the values are kept without a check. Said here, since a tool that is not deployed cannot be used either.
  6. **The Odoo template** declares `check: "setup.check"`: it asks Odoo (`res.users` count) with the login and key about to be kept, and says "Odoo refused this login and API key." on the key's field when Odoo answers an access denial; any other Odoo failure is not a wrong key and fails the check as "could not be completed".
- **Why**: without it nothing proves a key works until the first call fails, in a conversation with Claude. A check at the page answers where the person can act on it.
- **Cost, stated**: the check sees the plaintext values for one call, as `ctx.userSecrets` does in every call (D-036 "Know"). A tool's check can send them anywhere its network allow-list lets it: the same trust.
- **Not done**: the check is run by the web page only (the CLI's JSON route goes through the same handler, so it is checked too, but the CLI prompt is still there); no timeout of its own beyond the platform's; a preview address checks against the production Worker. A person who gives a secret cannot tell Odoo is down from a wrong key beyond the message.
- **Applied in**: `packages/runtime/src/{engine,runtime,secrets}.ts` (`checkSetup`, `SetupCandidate`, the route), `packages/compiler/src/manifest.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/{dispatch,user-secrets}.ts` (`createToolCaller`), `compose.ts`; `templates/odoo-gateway/`; `docs/sdk.md` §10.

## D-102 — A person's own secrets are not typed in the terminal: the CLI prints the link to the setup page (FOR-171, ticket 7)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · amends the CLI bullet of D-036 · last step of `docs/spikes/tool-setup-oauth.md`
- **Decision**: the web is the one place a person gives what a tool asks of them (the owner's direction of 2026-11-07, and decision 1 of the spike, confirmed on 2026-11-09). `promptUserSecrets`, `missingSecretsOf` and the hidden prompt of `forgeron secrets set <NAME> --user` are removed. `--user` is kept as a flag **so that it answers with the link** instead of an unknown-option error: it prints `<endpoint>/t/<tool>/setup`, exits `2`, reads nothing from the keyboard and sends nothing. A tool secret of the admin (`forgeron secrets set <NAME> --env`) is unchanged: it is not a person's own.
- **Why**: one place means one set of checks (D-097, D-101), one page to harden, and no secret going through a shell, a pipe or the CLI process. The page also covers what a terminal prompt could not: plain fields, the tool's check, the OAuth step.
- **Cost, stated**: a headless CI or a script cannot set a person's own secret any more. The JSON route of `/t/<tool>/setup` still takes `{ "NAME": "value" }` with a bearer token of the person (it is the page's own handler), but nothing in the CLI uses it; a person who needs it from a script writes the call. If this proves to be needed, a command that opens the browser to the page is the answer, not a prompt.
- **Not done**: the command `forgeron <tool> <group> <verb>` that would have used the prompt on a `428` never existed, so a failing call from the CLI shows the platform's error and its link as before.
- **Applied in**: `apps/cli/src/commands/secrets.ts`, `apps/cli/src/user-secrets.test.ts` (rewritten); `docs/sdk.md` §5 and §10, `docs/examples/02-odoo-gateway.md`.

## D-103 — Per-person secrets stay stored at the platform, sealed by a key the platform holds; the token-borne and user-held-key variants are not adopted (FOR-172)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · replaces D-099 · closes `docs/spikes/secrets-in-token.md`
- **Decision** (the owner, 2026-11-09): the model of D-036 stays. A per-person secret is **stored** in the platform's database, sealed with `SECRETS_KEY` (with `SECRETS_KEY_PREVIOUS` for rotation, D-100), and opened by the gateway for a call. Neither of these is adopted: (1) the secret **carried sealed in the OAuth token** (D-099); (2) the secret **stored encrypted under a key the person's client holds** and sent with each call. `keep: "token"` is not built and the seven tickets of the spike are dropped, except the first, which was done (D-100).
- **Why** (the owner's, and the spike's): a stored secret survives a reconnection and a change of client without the person typing it again; an administrator can set one, see that it exists and clear it; the same value can serve a person across the tools that ask for it. The token-borne variant loses all of this (the spike's "what is lost"). The user-held-key variant keeps the ciphertext at the platform but needs a key per connection that only the OAuth token can carry, which the gateway would create and then see on every call, and a lost token loses the secret: its gain (a leak of the database and of `SECRETS_KEY` reveals nothing; a compromised gateway exposes only the people who call during the intrusion) was judged not worth that cost.
- **What is kept from the spike**: the threat-model table and the observation that nothing improves against a stolen client token or a hostile tool. The protections that remain are those of D-036 and D-100: AES-GCM bound to the slot, only the gateway opens a value, a leak of the database alone reveals none, and the key can now be rotated.
- **Open, not decided** (asked by the owner on 2026-11-09, to be designed before any code):
  1. **An administrator sets a person's secret.** **Decided on 2026-11-09 (the owner): yes, with an audit line** (D-104). Setting one on someone's behalf means Odoo then attributes the actions to that person, who did not give the key, so the line is visible to the person.
  2. **An administrator reads a value, for debugging.** D-036 and the white paper promise that no route returns a value and that the developer never sees it. Reading would break the first for the administrator; it needs a deliberate exception: a recorded reason, an audit line the person can see, and probably the person's notice. The spike's recommendation is not to: a debug need is better served by "set / updated at / cleared" and by the tool's own check (D-101), which says whether a key works without showing it.
  3. **The same secret across a person's tools.** Today a secret belongs to one tool and one person. A secret shared by several tools needs a name above the tool (`ODOO_API_KEY` of the person), a table of its own, and a rule that a tool newly asking for it does not receive it silently: the person confirms it once for that tool on the setup page, and the administrator still approves the request (D-096).
- **Not done**: nothing is coded. The three points above are choices with security and legal weight (a person's key lets whoever holds it act as them in Odoo): they are for the owner to settle.
- **Applied in**: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (D-099 marked), `docs/spikes/secrets-in-token.md` (addendum).

## D-104 — A registry of a person's secrets: secret, versions and bindings; an admin may set one with an audit the person sees (FOR-172)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Proposed: the direction is the owner's (2026-11-09), the design is `docs/spikes/secret-registry.md`, five choices in it are the owner's to confirm; extends D-103
- **Decision**:
  1. **Admin sets a person's secret: yes, with audit** (the owner). The version records the administrator and `via: "admin"`; the audit line (`secret:set-by-admin`: admin, person, secret id, field names, never a value) is written in the same batch; the person sees it in their page as "set by an administrator on <date>". An administrator still reads no value (D-103 point 2 stays open).
  2. **Direction**: a person's secrets move to a registry of three parts: a **secret** (stable id, label, optional `kind`), immutable **versions** (a rotation adds one; each stores the additional data it was sealed with, so existing rows convert in SQL without re-sealing) and **bindings** (tool field, scope `*` / `production` / `preview` / `preview:<branch>`, secret, a pinned version or the latest). The tool's code and the header are unchanged.
  3. **A secret is reused only by the person's click**: a field with a `kind` is offered the person's saved secrets of that kind at setup; no binding is made by name or in silence; the admin's approval of the request (D-096) stays.
  4. **"My secrets"**: one page where the person sees their secrets, which tools and scopes use them, who last changed them, and changes a key once for every binding on "latest". No value is shown, even to its owner (to confirm).
  5. **Secrets only**: a person's plain values stay per tool (D-097).
  6. This **amends the setup spike's decision 5** (setup never per environment): the default stays tool-wide, a per-branch or per-environment binding is an override.
- **Why**: the owner wants one place to change a key, the same key across several tools and reconnections, a visible history of who set what, and an admin who can help without reading.
- **Rejected**: matching secrets across tools by field name (a name is not identity); binding silently (a tool could harvest keys); one row per tool with a copy of the value (changing a key would mean editing every copy); showing a value to its owner (it would have to be retrievable).
- **Done (ticket 1, the model)**: migration `0027_secret_registry` creates `person_secrets`, `person_secret_versions` (each with the `aad` it was sealed with) and `secret_bindings` (scope `*`, `production`, `preview` or `preview:<branch>`, with an optional pin) and converts every `user_secrets` row in SQL to a secret, its version 1 (`via: legacy`) and a tool-wide binding, without opening the value; `user_secrets` is kept, unused, until a later migration drops it. The four methods keep their names: `setUserSecret` adds a **version** of the tool-wide binding's secret (or makes the secret and the binding) and takes `by` and `via` (`setup`, `admin`) for the history; `readUserSecrets(tool, person, served?)` resolves the most specific binding for where the call is served (`preview:<branch>`, `preview`, `production`, `*`) and its pinned or latest version; `userSecretHolders` and `revokeUserSecrets` work on bindings, and a revocation deletes a secret only if it unbound it and nothing else uses it. Removing a member deletes their registry. The gateway passes no `served` yet, so every call resolves `production` then `*`, as before. Nothing the tool or the gateway does changed.
- **Not done**: the offer at setup and `kind` (ticket 2), the "My secrets" page (3), the admin's route and audit line (4), branch scopes in the gateway and on the page (5), re-sealing (6), dropping `user_secrets` (7). Two concurrent sets of the same field can collide on the version number: the second fails and is retried by the person. Seven tickets are in the spike, not created.
- **Applied in**: `docs/spikes/secret-registry.md`; `packages/registry/src/modules/secrets/{migrations,store}.ts`, `modules/members/store.ts`.

## D-105 — A secret field declares a `kind`; the setup page offers the person's saved secrets of that kind and binds one by a click (FOR-172, ticket 2 of the registry)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · second step of D-104 (`docs/spikes/secret-registry.md`)
- **Decision**:
  1. **The declaration**: `field.secret({ label, why, help?, kind? })`; `kind` goes to `resources.userSecrets.<NAME>.kind` in the manifest (`^[a-z][a-z0-9]*(\.[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*)*$`, 60 characters at most). Changing it is a `patch` of the manifest's diff (like a label), because it only changes which saved secrets are _offered_, and an admin's approval of the request is not touched.
  2. **A secret made on the page** is saved with the tool's label and kind (`setUserSecret` takes `label` and `kind` for a secret it creates; a rotation keeps them). Secrets that existed before the registry keep their field name as label and have no kind.
  3. **The offer**: for each secret field, if the person has saved secrets in this workspace that this field is not already using, the page shows a select: "Enter a new one", then **"Same kind"** (only if the field has a kind and the saved secret has the same one), then **"Your other saved secrets"**, each with its label and the tools that use it. Nothing is offered by field name: a name does not say that two values are the same thing. The page never shows a value.
  4. **The choice** (`use:NAME`, in the form or in JSON): it must be the person's own secret in the tool's workspace (`not available` otherwise, the same answer as for one that does not exist); choosing a saved secret **and** typing a value is refused; the tool's check (D-101) runs on the chosen value; only then is the tool-wide binding made (`bindUserSecret`), in one batch with the audit line `secret:bound` (actor the person, detail the field and the secret's id, **never a value**). The binding follows the latest version.
  5. **Effect**: one secret, several bindings. A new version of the secret (a rotation typed on any of the tools) changes what all of them get. Clearing one tool's field (the admin's revocation, D-036) unbinds it and keeps the secret for the others; a secret that no binding uses is deleted only by the revocation that unbound it.
  6. **The Odoo template** declares `kind: "odoo.api-key"` for the key and `"odoo.login"` for the login.
- **Why**: the owner wants a person to give an Odoo key once and reuse it in other tools without retyping, while one tool cannot harvest what the person gave another.
- **Rejected**: offering by field name; binding in silence when only one secret matches; a binding made before the check; showing which value is which (the label and the tools that use it say enough).
- **Not done**: the "My secrets" page (a person still cannot see or change a secret outside a tool's setup page), the admin's route and audit line, branch scopes, re-sealing, dropping `user_secrets` (tickets 3 to 7 of the spike). A saved secret that the person typed into a _plain_ field of another tool is not offered (plain values are not in the registry).
- **Not verified**: no browser; the select's markup and wording were seen only as HTML in tests.
- **Applied in**: `packages/manifest/src/schema.ts`, `packages/sdk/src/config.ts`, `packages/registry/src/modules/secrets/store.ts` (`listPersonSecrets`, `readPersonSecret`, `bindUserSecret`), `packages/runtime/src/secrets.ts` (`SavedSecrets`), `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/user-secrets.ts`, `templates/odoo-gateway/forgeron.config.ts`, `docs/sdk.md` §10.

## D-106 — A person's page of secrets: `/my/secrets` lists, changes and deletes them in one place; no value is ever shown (FOR-172, ticket 3 of the registry)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · third step of D-104
- **Decision**:
  1. **Where and who**: `GET`/`POST` `https://<workspace>.<domain>/my/secrets`, a gateway page in the shell of the other pages (same CSP, no script). The **session cookie only**: a token or nobody is sent to `/auth/login?next=/my/secrets`; a POST must come from the page (D-049) and from a session (401 otherwise). It shows only the signed-in person's secrets in this workspace.
  2. **What it shows**: for each secret its name, kind, number of versions, who last changed it in words (**by you**, **by an administrator**, **before versions were kept**), and for each use the tool, the field, the scope in words and whether it follows the latest version or is kept on one. **No value, no prefix, no suffix**: to change a key the person gives a new one (decision b of the spike, confirmed here by default; changing it is the owner's call).
  3. **What a person can do**: a **new version** (the secret's bindings on "latest" use it); **rename**; **delete** (a checkbox is required when tools use it, and they ask again); **change a binding** (another of their secrets, or an earlier version of it, which is read for the check) and **stop a tool using it**; **save a secret no tool uses yet**, with an optional kind. A binding can only be changed if the person has it (tool, field and scope name it); a secret or a version that is not theirs is refused with the same answer as one that does not exist.
  4. **A tool's own check protects the change**: before a new version is kept, or before a tool is bound to another value, the gateway asks **each tool that would get it from now on** (D-101) with the person's setup and the other secrets they already gave it. One that refuses stops the whole change with its message ("hr: Odoo refused this key. Nothing was changed."), and a tool that cannot be reached fails closed. A tool kept on an older version is not asked about a value it will not get.
  5. **Messages**: after a change the page is reloaded with `?done=<key>`; only a fixed list of keys is turned into a message, nothing else from the address is written into the page.
  6. **Audit**: each change writes a line (`secret:created`, `secret:version`, `secret:bound`, `secret:unbound`, `secret:deleted`): the secret's id, the field, the scope, the version, **never a value**, in the same batch as the change. They carry no tool for a secret no tool uses, so they are not in a tool's audit list.
  7. **Registry**: `createPersonSecret`, `addSecretVersion`, `renamePersonSecret`, `deletePersonSecret`, `unbindUserSecret`, and `bindUserSecret` with a scope and a pin (validated: `*`, `production`, `preview`, `preview:<branch>`; a version that exists); `readPersonSecret` can read an earlier version; `listPersonSecrets` also returns the history and each binding's pin. A value typed at a tool's own setup page is the latest again for that binding (its pin is cleared).
- **Why**: the owner wants one place to see and change a key and to see which tool uses which.
- **Rejected**: showing a value to its owner (it would have to stay retrievable); a change that skips the tools' checks (a key that works for one tool and breaks another is the failure this page exists to avoid); letting an administrator open the page for someone else (ticket 4 is the admin's way, with its own audit and the person's view of it).
- **Not done**: a link to the page from the console and from the setup page (the address is documented, nothing points to it yet); the administrator's route (ticket 4); creating a binding from the page for a tool that does not use the secret yet (the setup page of the tool is where a tool is bound); the branch scopes are shown and editable but nothing creates them yet (ticket 5); re-sealing (6); dropping `user_secrets` (7).
- **Not verified**: no browser (forms and `<details>` were read as HTML); no real tool behind the checks.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/my-secrets/my-secrets.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/pages/my-secrets.ts`, `gateway.ts`, `compose.ts` (`checkSetup` shared); `packages/registry/src/modules/secrets/store.ts`; `docs/sdk.md` §10.

## D-107 — An administrator may type a person's secret for a tool: audited, visible to the person, never read back (FOR-172, ticket 4 of the registry)

- **Date**: 09/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · fourth step of D-104; follows the owner's "yes, with an audit" of D-103
- **Decision**:
  1. **Route**: `PUT /api/tools/{tool}/user-secrets/{person}/{name}` with `{ "value": "..." }` (16 KB max), **admin only** like the other revocation routes. 204 on success; 404 for an unknown tool, a person who is not a member, or a field the tool does not declare in `resources.userSecrets`; 400 for a bad body. The value goes in and never comes out: no route returns it, the holders list still gives names only.
  2. **Effect**: the same as the person typing it at the tool's setup page: a new version of the secret behind the tool-wide binding, or a new secret (label and kind taken from the tool's declaration) with its binding. The binding follows the latest version, so the person's next call carries it. The version records **who typed it** (`created_by` = the admin) and `via: "admin"`.
  3. **Audit**: in the same batch, a line `secret:set-by-admin` on the tool (kind `access`): the admin, the tool, the field, the secret's id, the version, the person; **never the value**.
  4. **What the person sees**: "My secrets" (D-106) already says **by an administrator** for the last change and in the history; nothing else is needed on their side.
  5. **No MCP tool and no CLI command**: a secret typed into a chat would pass through a model's context and a terminal history. The route is for the console, which is where a person's keys are entered by hand.
  6. **The admin's own check**: none. The tool's setup `check` (D-101) is not run for an administrator's value; the person's next call tells whether it works. Running it would need the person's other values, which the administrator must not see.
- **Why**: a person blocked by a missing key (leave, wrong key) is unblocked by an administrator without sending the key through the person, and the person can see that it happened.
- **Rejected**: letting an administrator read a value for debugging (a stored secret is then retrievable by more people; unchanged from D-103); an e-mail to the person (decision d of the spike, left to the owner: no mail sender is configured for the gateway yet, so the line in "My secrets" is the notice for now).
- **Not done**: a console screen for the route; the mail; branch scopes (ticket 5).
- **Not verified**: no console, no browser.
- **Applied in**: `packages/platform-contract/src/revocations.ts` (`setUserSecretRoute`), `apps/gateway/src/platform/approvals.ts`, `packages/registry/src/modules/secrets/store.ts` (`setUserSecret`); tests `gateway-tokens-secrets.test.ts`, `approvals.test.ts`.

## D-108 — Re-sealing: `POST /api/secrets/reseal` seals a workspace's stored values again under the current `SECRETS_KEY` (FOR-172, ticket 6 of the registry)

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · closes the gap D-100 left ("there is no re-sealing yet")
- **Decision**:
  1. **What is sealed again**, for one workspace: the secrets of its tools, **every version** of its people's secrets, its connections, its sign-in provider's secret and its connectors' OAuth clients' secrets. Each value is opened with the current key or a listed previous one and sealed under the current key; one already under the current key is left alone. A version's own `aad` is its slot, a tool secret's slot is `<tool>/<environment>/<name>`, as when it was sealed.
  2. **Route**: `POST /api/secrets/reseal`, **admin only**, no body. It answers counts: `resealed`, `current`, `unreadable`. No value. 409 `NO_SECRET_KEY` where the gateway has no `SECRETS_KEY`. It writes an audit line `secrets:resealed` with the counts.
  3. **One workspace at a time**: the platform D1 is shared by workspaces, and an admin only touches the rows of theirs. The previous key can be dropped when **every** workspace of the install has answered `resealed: 0` and `unreadable: 0`.
  4. **Safe to repeat and to interrupt**: each update applies only to the value that was read (it matches its `iv` and ciphertext), so a value written meanwhile, already under the current key, is not overwritten. A second run answers `resealed: 0`.
  5. **A value no key opens** is counted as `unreadable` and left as it is; the run goes on. It must be typed again.
  6. **Not covered**: the legacy `user_secrets` table (ticket 7 drops it), and one-time data that is not kept (codes, states).
- **Why**: without it a replaced `SECRETS_KEY` could never leave `SECRETS_KEY_PREVIOUS`, so a leaked old key stayed useful.
- **Rejected**: a command that runs on every gateway start (a write on a read path, and nobody sees it happen); re-sealing all workspaces from one admin's call (one workspace's admin acting on others' rows).
- **Not done**: a console button and a CLI command for the route; the install does not set `SECRETS_KEY_PREVIOUS`.
- **Not verified**: no real D1 with a real rotation; tested on the in-memory database with two generated keys.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/reseal.ts`, `secret-box.ts` (`reseal`), `packages/platform-contract/src/secrets-status.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/platform/secrets.ts`, `compose.ts`; tests `reseal.test.ts`, `secret-box.test.ts`, `approvals.test.ts`; `docs/self-host.md`.

## D-109 — Scopes in use: a call carries the secret of the most specific scope for where it is served (FOR-172, ticket 5 of the registry)

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · fifth step of D-104; decision c of the spike (one binding per tool by default, an override per environment or branch optional)
- **Decision**:
  1. **Resolution at the call**: the dispatcher now gives the preview's **branch** with the environment, and `forwardIdentity` asks `readUserSecrets` for that place. On a preview address `/t/<tool>@<branch>`: `preview:<branch>`, then `preview`, then the tool-wide `*`. On production: `production`, then `*`. A person who set nothing else gets the same secret everywhere, as before.
  2. **Setting an override**: on "Your secrets" (D-106), a tool use bound tool-wide offers "use another secret for a test environment or a branch": **production**, **every preview** or **the preview of one branch** (lower case letters, digits, dots, dashes, underscores, as in a tool's address), and one of the person's own secrets. The binding follows that secret's latest version.
  3. **Only for a tool field the person already gave a secret to**; the secret must be theirs; a scope the registry does not accept is refused with a message. An override can be changed or removed with the same "Change" and "Stop using it here" as any binding.
  4. **The tool's own check** (D-101) runs on the value the tool would get there before the override is kept; a refusal changes nothing.
  5. **Unchanged**: the tool's setup page and the OAuth step read the production resolution; a value typed at the setup page is still the tool-wide secret; the audit lines are D-106's (`secret:bound` with the scope).
- **Why**: a developer tests a branch against a test instance of Odoo without replacing the key everyone else's production calls use.
- **Rejected**: choosing a scope by field name or guessing it from the address (a scope is chosen by the person); creating a binding for a tool the person has no secret for from this page (the tool's setup page is where a tool is first bound).
- **Not done**: a scope per branch on the tool's setup page; removing the branch's overrides when the preview is closed (they stay until the person removes them: a binding for a closed branch is harmless).
- **Not verified**: no browser; previews were exercised through the gateway with test values, not a real deployed preview.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/dispatch.ts` (`Caller.branch`), `dispatch/user-secrets.ts`, `compose.ts`, `my-secrets/my-secrets.ts` (`override`), `pages/my-secrets.ts`; test `gateway-user-setup.test.ts`.

## D-110 — The legacy `user_secrets` table is dropped: the registry is the only place a person's secrets live (FOR-172, ticket 7 of the registry)

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · last step of D-104
- **Decision**: migration `0028_drop_user_secrets` drops the table. Migration 0027 had copied each row into the registry (secret, version 1 with its own additional data, tool-wide binding), so nothing is read from it any more. Removing a member no longer deletes from it. A platform that is behind 0027 applies 0027 then 0028 in one run: the rows are converted first, then the table goes (a test does exactly this, and the converted value still opens).
- **Why**: a table nobody reads keeps sealed values under a key that D-108's re-sealing does not cover (it would have stayed the one place a previous key was still needed).
- **Departure from the spike**: the spike said "after one release". Done now on the owner's instruction to finish the tickets, on this deduction: the platform has no installed user yet (`migrations.ts`), so no database exists where a release would have shown a conversion problem. **If an install exists that I do not know of, check that its `person_secrets` holds what its `user_secrets` held before it runs 0028.** The migration is not flagged destructive (D-014 guards a tool's data); a rollback recreates the table **empty**.
- **Rejected**: keeping it "just in case" (the spike's point 7); flagging the migration destructive (the platform's own migrations are not applied with that flag, and the data was copied).
- **Not verified**: no real D1 with data from before 0027.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/secrets/migrations.ts`, `modules/members/store.ts`, `migrations.golden.json`; test `secret-registry.test.ts`.

## D-111 — The leftovers of the secret registry: links, console screens, a mail to the person, connectors as steps of the setup page (FOR-172)

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · closes what D-106 to D-108 listed as "not done"
- **Decision**:
  1. **Links to "Your secrets"** (`/my/secrets`, D-106): the console's _Personal secrets_ page now leads to it (it was an empty state), and a tool's setup page ends with a link to it.
  2. **Console, tool page, _Secrets_ tab (admins)**: a section _People's secrets_ lists who holds which secret **by name** (with the member's name and email), revokes one, and sets one for a person (person, name, value in a masked field) through D-107's route. The value goes in a body only and is shown nowhere; after a refusal it stays in the masked field so the name can be corrected.
  3. **Console, Settings, Administration, _Sealing key_** (admins): one button calling D-108's route, answering counts only, with a warning when a value is unreadable.
  4. **A mail to the person** when an administrator sets one of their secrets: "an administrator of <workspace> set your secret <FIELD> for the tool <tool>", with the link to `/my/secrets`; **never a value**, and not the administrator's name. Best effort: a mail that cannot be sent does not undo the secret and does not change the 204. It goes out through the gateway's mail sender (`SEND_EMAIL` and `MAIL_FROM`, or the console sender of a test install, which logs one line without a body). Where the gateway has no sender, nothing is sent and the line in "Your secrets" stays the notice. This answers decision d of the spike (yes, in "Your secrets" always, and by mail when mail is on).
  5. **Connectors as steps of the setup page**: for each _delegated_ connector the tool declares, the page says **Connected** or gives a **Connect** link to the existing consent route (D-046), and shows the connector's reason. A tool that asks nothing else shows these steps instead of "asks for nothing from you". Production only: the consent of a preview environment is not offered from this page.
- **Correction**: D-107 said no mail sender is configured for the gateway. That was wrong as a general statement: the gateway has a sender when `SEND_EMAIL` and `MAIL_FROM` are bound (it is the one that mails sign-in codes and invitations). What is true is that an install without them sends nothing.
- **Not done**: the OAuth step (D-098) does not wait for connectors, only for setup and secrets; a CLI command for re-sealing.
- **Not verified**: no browser (the console screens are tested in jsdom and the gateway page as HTML); no real mail.
- **Applied in**: `packages/runtime/src/secrets.ts` (`ConnectorStep`, `connectors`, `mySecrets`), `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/user-secrets.ts`, `compose.ts`, `mail/mail.ts`, `platform/approvals.ts`, `platform/app.ts`; `apps/console/src/features/tools/tabs/people-secrets.tsx`, `features/settings/pages/{personal-secrets,sealing}.tsx`; tests next to each.

## D-112 — One CI workflow; the per-template preview workflow is removed

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · supersedes the workflow of D-045 (the log of D-045 is left as written)
- **Decision**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is the only CI: one job `check` on one machine, as in the Linear repository: the PR title first (on a pull request), then lint, format, typecheck, knip, deps, neutral, templates, tokens and the tests. No shard, nothing in parallel. `.github/workflows/templates-preview.yml` is deleted, so no template is deployed as a preview by CI any more; templates are still built and tested by `bun run test`. `bun run templates:check` now only checks that each template has a config and a test.
- **Why**: one workflow and one machine to read and maintain; the four-template deploy matrix needed a workspace variable and one secret per template.
- **Not done**: the repository variable `FORGERON_WORKSPACE` and the `FORGERON_TOKEN_*` secrets are not deleted (a settings change); a branch protection that required a job other than `check` must be checked.
- **Not verified**: the workflow has not run; a single job is slower than the parallel ones it replaces, and its 30-minute timeout is a guess.
- **Applied in**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `scripts/check-templates.ts`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## D-113 — Promote a branch to production when nothing stops it, guide the person when something does; admin operations and remote SQL from the CLI (FOR-183)

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Points 1 to 4 replaced by D-125 (the plan stays, as the plan of an update; `POST /promote/plan`, `forgeron promote`, `tool_promote` and `tool_promote_plan` are removed); points 5 to 8 active
- **Decision**:
  1. **A promotion plan** (`POST /promote/plan`, same callers as `/promote`: a deployment token or an administrator). Read only. For a version, or for the version a branch's preview serves (`fromBranch`), and a target: the version currently served there, `alreadyServed`, the migrations (applied, pending, destructive), the manifest changes compared with what the target serves, and the **conflicts**. A conflict is what makes the promotion fail today, found before anything moves, **all of them at once**: `MIGRATION_MISSING` (the target's database applied one this version lacks, usually another branch's that went first), `MIGRATION_EDITED`, `MIGRATION_OUT_OF_ORDER` (the codes `applyMigrations` refuses with, compared against the target's own database without writing anything, not even the tracking table), `CHANGELOG_NO_ENTRY`, `CHANGELOG_IMPACT_TOO_LOW`, `PENDING_APPROVAL`, `NO_ARTIFACT`. A pending **destructive** migration is not a conflict: it sets `requiresAllowDestructive` and the promotion asks for the flag.
  2. **No conflict: it goes.** `POST /promote` takes `fromBranch` instead of `version`. The CLI (`forgeron promote --from-branch <b> --to production`), the console (the branch's _Promote to production_ dialog) and the admin MCP (`tool_promote`) ask for the plan first and promote only when `ok`. `forgeron promote <version>` is unchanged (no plan), and the promotion still refuses on its own what it refused before.
  3. **Conflicts: details, the way out, a prompt.** The plan carries a `prompt` written for an agent: the tool, the versions, each conflict with its details, how to fix each kind (an applied migration is never edited, deleted or renumbered; regenerate this branch's migration on top of the applied ones; the changelog; an approval is a human decision), the commands to inspect (`forgeron db status --env production`, `forgeron db shell`) and the rules (no write or destructive statement on production without showing it and getting a yes; no guessing at the schema). The CLI prints the conflicts, says to fix them with the CLI or an agent, prints the prompt between markers (and writes it with `--prompt-file`) and **exits 3** (neither a failure nor a wrong use); the console shows the conflicts, a _Copy prompt for your agent_ button, and the two commands; `tool_promote` answers `{ promoted: false, plan }`. A new tool, `tool_promote_plan`, only asks.
  4. **A destructive migration stays in the terminal**: the console and the MCP show it and do not offer to run it (`--allow-destructive` is the CLI's, as before).
  5. **Remote database from the CLI**, in the manner of `wrangler d1 execute`: `GET /api/tools/{tool}/db/migrations` (what the environment's database has applied against the version it serves, with the conflicts) and `POST /api/tools/{tool}/db/query` (one statement), **admins only**, behind `forgeron db status|shell --env preview|production [--branch]`. A read runs as it is; a statement that writes needs `write: true` (`--write`), and on production `confirm` set to the tool's name (`--confirm <tool>`); a `WITH` that contains a write is a write; more than one statement is refused. The environment must be one the registry knows. **The statement is recorded before it runs** and one that cannot be recorded is not run: a write keeps its text (1,000 characters), a read keeps a hash and a length (its text may hold anyone's data). 1,000 rows at most come back. It is admins only, not developers, because arbitrary SQL cannot honour the explorer's masked columns.
  6. **Admin operations from the CLI**: `forgeron admin` (tools, approvals, members, audit, people's secrets, re-sealing, revoking a person's tokens) over the typed client, and `forgeron api <METHOD> <path>` for any `/api/` route, with the token of `forgeron login`: the same guards, validation and audit lines as the console. No route returns a secret's value, so no command can show one; a value to set is typed, never an argument. They work outside a tool's folder with `--endpoint` or `FORGERON_ENDPOINT`.
  7. **`forgeron platform status`**: what is installed on the Cloudflare account (platform migrations, gateway bindings by name, route), read only, exit 1 when something is missing.
  8. **A re-run of the install keeps `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`** (FOR-184, #175): found while updating the staging install.
- **Why**: a promotion that fails halfway on a migration, with a bare message, leaves the person to work out what happened in production's database; saying it beforehand, with a way to fix it that an agent can follow, is the point. And an operator should not need the dashboard to look at or fix a database.
- **Rejected**: promoting on a conflict with a warning (a migration conflict is a production incident); running the SQL of a conflict for the person (a decision about production data); an MCP tool that runs SQL (a model deciding on production data, and the statement in a transcript).
- **Not done**: `db tables|schema|export` on a remote environment (a `SELECT` on `sqlite_master` does it); a clipboard copy from the CLI (the prompt is printed, or written with `--prompt-file`); the plan on a promotion by version number from the CLI (it asks for it with `--dry-run`); a preview as the target of `--from-branch` (production only from the CLI; the plan itself takes any target).
- **Not verified**: no real D1 behind `db status|shell` and the plan (tested on in-memory databases through a fake host); no browser for the dialog; `forgeron platform status` against a simulated account only.
- **Applied in**: `packages/hosting/src/migrate.ts` (`checkMigrations`), `packages/registry/src/promote-plan.ts`, `packages/platform-contract/src/{bearer,database-admin}.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/deploy/promote.ts`, `platform/database-admin.ts`, `admin-mcp/tools.ts`, `apps/cli/src/commands/{promote,db,admin,platform-status}.ts`, `remote.ts`, `apps/console/src/features/tools/release-dialog.tsx`; docs/sdk.md, docs/self-host.md.

## D-114 — The Odoo gateway leaves `templates/`: the launch catalogue is three templates, business tools live in tools repositories of their own

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · replaces D-088
- **Decision**:
  1. `templates/odoo-gateway` (tool `odoo`) is removed from the repository, with `docs/examples/02-odoo-gateway.md` and `docs/odoo-gateway-in-claude.md`. It moves to Edicia's tools repository, a repository of Forgeron tools like any customer's (`docs/sdk.md` §2: one folder per `forgeron.config.ts`).
  2. The launch catalogue is `crm`, `inventory` (the data hub) and `dashboard`, maintained by the core team. A template is a generic tool any workspace can use; a tool written for one company's systems is not one.
  3. The Odoo manifest stays in `packages/ui/src/architecture/fixtures.ts` as a static fixture of a tool without a database; tests that use `odoo` as a tool name are unchanged.
- **Why**: Odoo is one company's back office, not a standard tool; a business tool belongs to the repository of the company that runs it. Such repositories are meant to become marketplaces a workspace can add (to come).
- **Rejected**: keeping it as a fourth template (the catalogue would carry a tool nobody outside its company installs); keeping it as an untested example in `docs/examples/` (an example nobody builds breaks silently, D-088's reason).
- **Lost**: the SDK's integration tests no longer cover a tool without a database that uses per-person secret fields with a `kind`, a setup `check` and `network.allow` together; the unit tests of `manifest`, `runtime` and `sdk` still cover each.
- **Applied in**: `templates/`, `apps/site/src/pages/index.astro`, `packages/ui/src/architecture/fixtures.ts`, `packages/sdk/src/config.test.ts`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/README.md`, `docs/sdk.md`.

## D-115 — A tool has a summary and a guide for the people who use it; `forgeron bundle` writes a build as one file for a marketplace

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **`summary`** (config and manifest, optional): one sentence for the people who use the tool, with no technical term. The catalog's `summary` is the `summary`, else the `description` as before. A change is a `tool.text-changed` of impact patch, as for `description`.
  2. **`GUIDE.md`** (optional, next to `README.md`): the tool explained to the people who use it. It is collected at build like the README (D-061): its relative images too, the same limits (1 MB a file, 5 MB in all), `manifest.docs.guide` keeps its size and hash, `docs.json` its content, the platform checks it again (`docsProblem`) and stores it among the build's files under `GUIDE.md`. The tool card gains `guide` (text or `null`). The README stays the technical text, for administrators.
  3. **`forgeron bundle [--out <dir>]`** builds the tool of the current folder and writes `<name>-<version>.forgeron.json` (default `.forgeron/bundle/`): `{ format: "forgeron.bundle/1", name, version, build }`, where `build` is the body `forgeron deploy` sends (`ToolBundle` in `@forgeron/hosting`). It prints the file and its SHA-256. One JSON file, so a Worker reads it without an archive library. The CLI's deploy and bundle read `.forgeron/build/` through the same `readBuild`.
- **Why**: a member's library must speak to the person, not to the developer; and a marketplace needs a built tool the platform can take without a build step (D-004).
- **Rejected**: a guide inside the README behind a marker (one file for two readers, and the console would have to cut markdown); a tarball of `.forgeron/build/` (an archive parser in the gateway); the `build.json` file hashes in the bundle (the index will carry the hash of the whole file).
- **Not done**: nothing reads a bundle yet (marketplaces come next); the console does not show the guide yet.
- **Applied in**: `packages/manifest/src/{schema,catalog,diff}.ts`, `packages/sdk/src/config.ts`, `packages/compiler/src/{docs,manifest}.ts`, `packages/hosting/src/core.ts`, `packages/registry/src/{docs-check,catalog}.ts`, `packages/registry/src/modules/catalog/store.ts`, `apps/cli/src/{built,cli}.ts`, `apps/cli/src/commands/{bundle,deploy}.ts`, `docs/sdk.md` §3, §5 and §14.

## D-116 — Marketplaces: a GitHub repository of tool bundles a workspace adds, synced into a hidden catalogue and installed like a template

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **What a marketplace is**: a GitHub repository with `forgeron-marketplace.json` at its root (`MarketplaceIndexSchema` in `@forgeron/manifest`): a name, a title, and per tool its version, the tag of the release that holds its bundle (`forgeron bundle`, D-115), the file's name and its SHA-256. The repository's own CI builds and publishes (D-004); the platform builds nothing.
  2. **Where it lives**: a table `marketplaces` (migration `0029`, new registry module): workspace, `owner/name`, the read token **sealed** under `marketplace/<workspace>/<id>` (added to the re-sealing, D-108), the index's title and the last sync's state. What it offers is recorded as the tools, builds, docs and versions of the hidden workspace `@mp:<id>`, with the calls a deployment uses, exactly as `@templates` holds the templates (D-081). Removing a marketplace deletes that workspace's tools and the token; the tools already installed stay the workspace's.
  3. **Sync** (`apps/gateway/src/marketplace/`): reads the index through GitHub's contents API, and for each tool whose version is not recorded yet, downloads the release asset through the releases API (25 MB at most), then treats it as untrusted code: the SHA-256 the index gives, the bundle's format, name and version, `ManifestSchema`, the manifest's name and version against the index, `docsProblem`. A refused tool is reported with its reason and does not stop the others. The token goes to `api.github.com` only: an asset address outside the API is refused, and the download's redirect to GitHub's storage drops the header. A sync is by hand (_Sync_ in the console); there is no schedule.
  4. **The official marketplace** is the template catalogue `@templates` itself, with the id `official`: when `OFFICIAL_MARKETPLACE` names a public repository, an admin's sync of it fills `@templates` with the same code (no token). This is the step D-081 lacked to fill the catalogue on a real install.
  5. **Install** is the template install, given a source: `installTemplate`, `createInstallPlan` and `installFromPlan` take `@templates` (default) or `@mp:<id>`; a plan keeps its source (migration `0030`). Same batch, same pending approvals, same audit line (with the source in its detail).
  6. **Routes** (contract `marketplaces.ts`, handler `platform/marketplaces.ts`), all **admin only**: list, add (`400` not `owner/name`, `409` already added), remove, sync (`502` with GitHub's refusal in words, kept on the marketplace), list the tools, a tool's technical card (README, guide, actions, roles, hosts, secrets, connectors, database, changelog), install, install plan. **Admin MCP**: `marketplace_list`, `marketplace_tools`, and `tool_install_plan` takes an optional `marketplace`. Adding, syncing and removing are not on the MCP.
  7. **Console**: a _Marketplace_ screen (admin, `g k`) and a technical sheet per tool (`docs/console-ux.md`, "Marketplace").
- **Why**: a company's own tools (D-114) need a way into its workspace without a deployment token per tool, and an admin must read what a tool asks for before it exists.
- **Rejected**: the platform building from source (no build machines, D-004); a tarball per tool (an archive parser in a Worker, D-115); pushing bundles from the marketplace's CI into each workspace (one deployment token per workspace in that CI, and no review before install); adding or syncing a marketplace from the admin MCP (an instruction slipped into a document could point the workspace at a repository and pull its code in); a schedule (a sync records third-party code: an admin starts it).
- **Not done**: images of a marketplace tool's README or guide are not served on the technical sheet (only the text); an installed tool is not told about a newer version in its marketplace (updating an installed tool is not done, as in D-081); the token cannot be changed in place (remove and add again).
- **Not verified**: no real GitHub (a simulated API in the tests: index, release, asset, wrong hash, a bundle naming another tool, a private repository without token); the console checked in the preview harness only.
- **Applied in**: `packages/manifest/src/marketplace.ts`, `packages/registry/src/modules/{marketplaces,templates}/`, `reseal.ts`, `migrations.golden.json`, `packages/platform-contract/src/marketplaces.ts`, `addresses.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/marketplace/`, `platform/marketplaces.ts`, `admin-mcp/tools.ts`, `env.ts`, `apps/console/src/features/marketplace/`, `mock/marketplaces.ts`; `docs/sdk.md` §17, `docs/console-ux.md`, `docs/self-host.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## D-117 — A person's library: an admin says who may add a tool, a person adds it, gives its setup and removes it; a tool with a rule is reached only from a library

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **Availability** (`tool_availability`, migration `0031`, new registry module `library`): per tool, `nobody`, `everyone`, `roles` (workspace roles) or `people` (member ids). An admin always may add a tool, to try it. A tool installed from a catalogue or a marketplace starts at `nobody`, written in the install's batch (D-081, D-116).
  2. **A library** (`person_tools`): the tools a person added. Adding one is audited (`access`, `library-added`); removing one deletes, in one batch, the person's setup values for it and the bindings of their saved secrets to it, and is audited (`library-removed`). The secrets themselves stay on the person's page of secrets (D-106): another tool may use them, and adding the tool again offers them back (D-105).
  3. **The rule is enforced where a tool is reached**: the dispatcher refuses a production call to a tool with a rule from a person who may not add it (`403 NOT_AVAILABLE`) or has not added it (`403 NOT_IN_LIBRARY`, with the library's address); the MCP sign-in sends such a person to the tool's page of their library before the consent, as it sends them to the setup (D-098). Previews keep their own rule (`canPreview`). The roles × actions matrix still decides each action (D-039): availability is who may have the tool, not what they may do with it.
  4. **A tool without a rule is open as before**: every member who may call it reaches it with no install, and it shows as already in their library, not removable. A first `forgeron deploy` creates such a tool. Saving a rule on it is the admin's switch to libraries; the Availability tab warns that people then add it to keep using it.
  5. **Routes** (contract `library.ts`): for any member, `GET /api/library` (added and available tools, with whether a setup is unfinished), `GET /api/library/{tool}` (the tool for the person: summary, guide, where to finish the setup, how to use it on the web, in Claude and in a terminal, only once added), `POST` (adds it; answers the setup's address when something is missing) and `DELETE` (`409` for an open tool); for admins, `GET` and `PUT /api/tools/{tool}/availability` (a person named must be a member). The setup page may now send a person back to `/library/<tool>` as well as to the OAuth step.
  6. **Console**: Home is the library (_My tools_, _Available to you_, _Add_, _Use_, _Finish setup_); the tool page gets an admin tab _Availability_. The tool's page in the library comes with the member's sheet (next decision).
- **Why**: a tool is something a person chooses to use, gives their own key to, and stops using; an admin decides who may, without a role per tool.
- **Rejected**: enforcing libraries on every existing tool at once (every person would lose their tools until they add them again); availability as one more role of the matrix (a role is about actions; who may have a tool is about people, and roles are per tool); revoking the person's tokens on removal (the dispatcher refuses them already, and a session is not per tool); deleting the person's secrets on removal (another tool may use them, and the guide promises they stay).
- **Not verified**: the MCP sign-in's redirect to the library is not driven by a test (the refusal at the dispatcher is, through the whole gateway); the console checked in the preview harness only.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/library/`, `modules/templates/store.ts`, `migrations.golden.json`; `packages/platform-contract/src/library.ts`, `addresses.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/library/library.ts`, `platform/library.ts`, `dispatch/user-secrets.ts`, `identity/mcp-oauth/mcp-oauth.ts`; `apps/console/src/features/portal/{home,library,mock}.ts(x)`, `features/tools/tabs/availability-tab.tsx`, `mock/install.ts`; `docs/console-ux.md`.

## D-118 — Two sheets per tool: the member's in their library, with nothing technical; the admin's in Tools, which becomes the technical screens

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active
- **Decision**:
  1. **The member's sheet** (`/library/<tool>`, console `features/portal/library-page.tsx`, data from `GET /api/library/{tool}`, D-117): the summary, the guide, adding and removing the tool, finishing its setup, and how to use it in the browser, in Claude and in a terminal, shown only once it is added and set up. Adding a tool that asks for something takes the person to its setup page, which brings them back. No version, role, action, interface name, resource or README reaches it.
  2. **The admin's sheet** is the tool page in Tools: the Overview (README, details, how to install, and now _What its users read_), branches, versions, availability, access, database, secrets, activity. The Tools screens are for admins and developers (a new console access level `developer`); a member's way to a tool is their library.
  3. **The Secrets tab is for admins**: it lists who holds which secret. A person's own are given through the setup page and listed on their page of secrets (D-106).
- **Why**: the person who uses a tool needs to know what it does for them and how to start, not how it is built; the admin needs the opposite.
- **Rejected**: one card with technical parts hidden by role (the same screen would keep growing both ways); keeping Tools in a member's menu (it shows branches, versions and roles).
- **Not done**: the images of a guide on the member's sheet come from the catalog's assets, so they need the person to be a member of the workspace, as before; a developer without the admin role still sees only the Overview tab of a tool, as before.
- **Applied in**: `apps/console/src/features/portal/{library-page,library,routes,tool-card,mock}.ts(x)`, `portal.css`, `apps/console/src/shell/access.ts`, `features/tools/{routes,register}.ts`; `docs/console-ux.md`.

## D-119 — Pilots: a branch preview opened to some people, as a tool of its own with its own data; production reaches everyone who added the tool

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Replaced by D-125 (a pilot rule is the access of a channel; the tables are renamed, the rows kept)
- **Found, not changed**:
  1. A person's library points at the tool, and the dispatcher serves what production serves: when a version is promoted, everyone who added the tool gets it at their next call, with nothing to do. A preview never reaches them unless it is promoted.
  2. A preview already runs as its own Worker with its own database per branch (D-055, D-080), filled by its seed or left empty (`preview.data`; `snapshot` is not implemented): no data goes from production to a preview or back.
- **Decision**:
  1. **A pilot** is a branch preview an admin opens to people: `preview_availability` (migration `0032`, library module), per tool and branch, with the modes of D-117 (`nobody` reads _admins and developers only_). Without a rule a preview stays for admins and developers, as before.
  2. **A person adds a pilot to their library apart from the tool** (`person_previews`): it is listed as a tool of its own (`pilot: <branch>` in `GET /api/library`), with its own page (`/library/<tool>/pilots/<branch>`) that says it is a version being tried with its own data. Adding the pilot does not add the tool, nor the reverse. Removing it removes the person's secret bindings for that branch (`preview:<branch>`, D-109); the setup values belong to the tool and stay.
  3. **Reaching it**: `canPreview` lets in admins and developers as before, and anyone else who may pilot the branch and added it. The MCP sign-in on `/t/<tool>@<branch>/mcp` checks the pilot, not the tool. The pilot's setup page comes back to the pilot's page.
  4. **Routes**: `GET`, `POST`, `DELETE /api/library/{tool}/pilots/{branch}` for any member; `GET`, `PUT /api/tools/{tool}/previews/{branch}/availability` for admins (`404` for a branch with no preview).
  5. **Console**: Home shows a pilot as its own card, marked _Pilot · branch_; the admin's Availability tab lists the branches that have a preview, each with who may pilot it.
- **Why**: a person can try a version that is being prepared, on data that is not production's, without confusing it with the tool everyone uses; an admin decides who, as for a tool.
- **Rejected**: a pilot as a new tool in the registry (a second name for the same code, and its versions would not follow the branch); copying production's data into a pilot (personal data leaving production; asked not to, for now).
- **Not done**: a pilot's CLI command (a terminal reaches a preview with `--branch` already, for developers); the images of a pilot's guide; removing rules and library entries of a branch whose preview was torn down (they stay unused, and the library only lists branches that still have a preview).
- **Not verified**: the MCP sign-in on a pilot's address is not driven by a test; the console checked in the preview harness only.
