- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/library/`, `migrations.golden.json`; `packages/platform-contract/src/library.ts`, `addresses.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/library/library.ts`, `platform/library.ts`, `compose.ts`, `dispatch/user-secrets.ts`, `identity/mcp-oauth/mcp-oauth.ts`; `apps/console/src/features/portal/`, `features/tools/tabs/availability-tab.tsx`, `mock/install.ts`; `docs/sdk.md` §15, `docs/console-ux.md`.

## D-120 — The tool page shows production or one branch, chosen in its header

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active; "branch" reads "channel" since D-125 (`/tools/<tool>/tree/<ref>`, `<ref>` a branch or `~<name>`)
- **Decision**:
  1. **One address per branch**: `/tools/<tool>` is production, `/tools/<tool>/tree/<branch>` a branch's preview (the branch encoded, a slash included). A picker at the right of the header moves between them and keeps the open tab.
  2. **Each tab says its scope** (`scope` in `registerToolTab`): Overview, Architecture, Availability, Database and Secrets follow the branch; Branches, Versions, Access and Activity are the same for every branch and say so on a branch's page. They stay visible, so the tabs do not move.
  3. **A branch is hard to miss**: a band under the header names it, says that changes there are for that branch only, and offers to go back to production. A branch whose preview is gone shows production, with a word, and the address goes back to production's.
  4. **API**: `GET /api/tools/{tool}/manifest?branch=`, `GET /api/catalog/{tool}?branch=` and its images take a branch and answer what its preview serves (`404` without a preview). A branch's card and images are for admins and developers (`403` for anyone else); pilots read theirs from their library (D-119). A branch's card gives the preview's addresses (`/t/<tool>@<branch>/`).
  5. **Replaced**: the Database tab's environment picker and the list of pilots in the Availability tab (D-119): both follow the page's branch.
- **Why**: one choice of branch for the whole page instead of one per tab, and the Overview and Architecture of a preview, which the console could not show.
- **Rejected**: a picker per tab (what replaced); hiding the tabs that do not follow the branch (the tabs would move, and Versions and Branches are what one looks at to choose a branch); `?branch=` in the query (a branch is a place, as on a code host).
- **Not done**: Activity is not filtered by branch; Access stays the tool's matrix even when a branch declares other roles or actions (the matrix is read from production's manifest, as before); a person's secrets on a branch are not listed (they are given by each pilot from their library).
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/catalog.ts`; `packages/platform-contract/src/{tools,catalog}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/platform/{tools,catalog}.ts`; `apps/console/src/router.ts`, `features/tools/{routes,tabs,register}.ts`, `features/tools/pages/tool-page.tsx`, `tools.css`, `features/tools/tabs/{availability,secrets}-tab.tsx`, `secrets-status.tsx`, `features/{portal,architecture,database}/`; `docs/console-ux.md`.

## D-121 — Two ways in: a marketplace's releases, or a repository's branches; approvals come with the update

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Replaces D-055 in part (production is reached through an update), completes D-116 and D-120 · Points 2 (repository updates), 4 (`withoutRelease`) and 5 (by itself) replaced by D-125
- **Decision** ([`lifecycle.md`](lifecycle.md)):
  1. **Two sources.** A tool comes from a **marketplace** (a monorepo of finished tools: Forgeron takes the latest release of each) or from a **GitHub repository that holds only it** (each pushed branch is a preview, a deleted branch's preview goes). One source per tool in a workspace. `tool_sources` (migration `0033`, templates module) records it: the marketplace and the tool's name there, or the repository, the **production branch** (`main` by default) and **update by itself**. A tool deployed before has no row: a repository on `main`, updated by hand.
  2. **Updates.** A marketplace tool has an update when the marketplace publishes a newer release; a repository tool when the preview of its production branch serves another version than production. `GET /api/tools/{tool}/source` says what it brings (changelog, release or not, what it asks to approve); `POST /api/tools/{tool}/update` puts it in production, refused when the update changed since it was read (`STALE`). Admin MCP: `tool_source`, `tool_update`.
  3. **Approvals come with the update.** No screen of approvals: the install, update and promotion dialogs list what the version asks for, and the admin approves there (`approve`, an admin's, never a deployment token's). Installing from a marketplace approves what the sheet showed and puts the tool in production where the gateway can deploy. The approvals API and `resources_approve` stay, for the admin MCP.
  4. **A release is advised, not required.** Production takes a build with no changelog entry when an admin says so (`withoutRelease`, written in the audit line); a release that declares too little is still refused. `forgeron release` runs on the production branch only, so that two branches never number the same version. A marketplace's publication is its release.
  5. **By itself.** With _update by itself_, a deployment of the production branch goes to production when it is a release and asks for nothing new; a marketplace sync updates the tools that update by themselves. Otherwise the update waits on the tool's page, with the reason.
  6. **No cron, no webhook, no build on the platform.** The repository's CI builds and pushes (`forgeron deploy`, the deploy action, D-004); the generated workflow deploys every pushed branch and closes a deleted one's preview (`FORGERON_BRANCH`). Opening the marketplaces syncs, after the answer, those read more than an hour ago. A Worker cannot run the build (no file system, no process); a container could, later.
  7. **Add from GitHub.** `POST /api/tools/from-repository` creates the tool (no environment, nobody may add it yet) and gives, once, the deployment token for the repository's `FORGERON_TOKEN`.
  8. **A marketplace entry may point to another repository** (`repo` in `forgeron-marketplace.json`): a tool developed in its own repository is published without being copied, read with the marketplace's token.
  9. **Data between branches.** A new branch starts with no data. `POST /api/tools/{tool}/branches/{branch}/data` replaces a branch's data by another branch's: both previews, the same migrations, at most 50,000 rows, the tool's name typed to confirm, audited. Production's data is never copied nor replaced.
  10. **Generators.** `forgeron init marketplace` (index, `tools/`, release workflow); `forgeron init <name>` at a marketplace's root puts the tool in `tools/<name>/` and lists it; alone, it writes the deploy workflow. Each repository gets `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (`@AGENTS.md`) and `docs/lifecycle.md`, a copy of this repository's, kept identical by a test.
- **Why**: a marketplace is for finished tools an admin installs and updates; a repository is where a tool is developed, branch by branch, as on Vercel or Cloudflare. Approving on a separate screen made an update two trips for one decision.
- **Rejected**: branches in a marketplace (the interface only needs the latest release); a monorepo of tools developed together (a branch would hold several tools); a GitHub App or webhooks (a secret to keep and an inbound route, when the CI already pushes); building in a Worker; numbering versions on secondary branches (two branches would release `1.3.0`); copying production's data into a branch (personal data at developers').
- **Not done**: a newer marketplace release is seen only once the marketplace is synced (by hand, or when the marketplaces are opened); a marketplace whose sync fails is tried again each time they are opened; the published name of the deploy action is still to be confirmed (D-004); the copy of data is row by row in one request of the gateway (hence the limit); the `edicia-forgeron` repository is not regenerated.
- **Not verified**: the deploy action on a real GitHub `delete` event (`github.event.ref`); auto-update after a real deployment (tested against a simulated host); the console checked in the preview harness only.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/templates/`, `deploy.ts`, `modules/catalog/store.ts`, `migrations.golden.json`; `packages/hosting/src/{core,deploy}.ts`; `packages/manifest/src/marketplace.ts`; `packages/platform-contract/src/{sources,bearer,marketplaces,templates}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/sources/`, `platform/{sources,marketplaces,templates}.ts`, `deploy/{deploy,promote}.ts`, `marketplace/`, `admin-mcp/tools.ts`; `apps/console/src/features/{tools,marketplace,database}/`, `app.tsx`, `shell/routes.ts`, `mock/install.ts`; `apps/cli/src/{scaffold,workflow}.ts`, `commands/{init,preview}.ts`; `.github/actions/deploy/action.yml`; `docs/lifecycle.md`, `sdk.md`, `console-ux.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md`.

## D-122 — The npm packages: `forgeron` (the CLI, one file) and `@forgeron/sdk`, `runtime`, `manifest`, built by tsdown, released together

- **Date**: 10/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Applies D-086; replaces `sdk.md` §18's single `forgeron` package with subpaths
- **Decision**:
  1. **Four published packages**: `forgeron` (the CLI), `@forgeron/sdk`, `@forgeron/runtime`, `@forgeron/manifest` (`scripts/packages.ts`). The others stay private: the compiler, hosting, registry, platform contract and playground are bundled into the CLI; the UI, connectors and templates later.
  2. **Built by tsdown** (Rolldown, the Vite team's library packager; `0.23.5`, pinned: 0.23.7 brings rolldown 1.3, whose WebAssembly binding pulls a beta of `@emnapi` that breaks `astro check`): the CLI as one ES module for Node (`dist/index.mjs`, 234 kB), the SDK, the runtime and the manifest as ES modules with their types. The workspace keeps importing `src/`; `scripts/pack.ts` writes `dist/package.json` with `exports` on the built files and `workspace:*` replaced by the version, and `dist/` is what npm receives.
  3. **The CLI keeps the SDK, the runtime and the manifest as dependencies**: the compiler bundles the runtime and the SDK into a tool's Worker, resolved from where the CLI is installed. Its own npm dependencies (rolldown, miniflare, zod, kysely) are declared, not bundled. A new tool (`forgeron init`) depends on `@forgeron/sdk` and, for its scripts, on `forgeron`, at the CLI's own version, written in at build (`__FORGERON_VERSION__`).
  4. **One version for all**, release-please in monorepo mode: a component per package plus the platform (`.`), linked versions, a changelog per package; the platform's tag stays `v<version>`. The first release is `0.1.0`.
  5. **Proved before published**: `bun run packages:smoke` packs the four, installs them with npm in an empty folder, runs `forgeron --version`, `forgeron init`, then the new tool's `npm run build`, `npm test` and `forgeron bundle`. CI runs it on every pull request; the release workflow runs it again, then `scripts/publish.ts` publishes in dependency order, skipping a version already on npm.
  6. **No token** (npm's rules of 2026: legacy tokens are gone, publishing with a granular token ends in January 2027): the release workflow publishes by **trusted publishing** (OIDC, `id-token: write`) on a runner hosted by GitHub, which trusted publishing requires. A trusted publisher can only be set on a package that exists, so each package's **first publication is made by hand** by a maintainer (`npm login`, then `bun run packages:build` and `bun scripts/publish.ts`); then `npm trust github <package> --file release.yml --repo forgeron-sh/monorepo --allow-publish` for each, and the next release publishes alone. Provenance comes with trusted publishing once the repository is public; it is private today, so none yet.
- **Why**: Node does not load TypeScript from `node_modules`, nor extensionless imports: the sources cannot be published as they are. One file for the CLI keeps the private packages private and the install small.
- **Rejected**: one `forgeron` package holding the SDK and the CLI (`sdk.md` §18 before): the code imports `@forgeron/sdk` everywhere, and D-086 had chosen the scoped name; publishing every workspace package; `bun build` (no types); `publishConfig` rewriting `exports` (npm does not honour it).
- **Not done**: nothing is published (the first publication is the owner's, by hand); trusted publishers are set per package once it exists, and the first trusted publication must follow within two days or the setting expires; `create-forgeron` and the empty `@forgeron/cli`, `@forgeron/core` are not reserved.
- **Applied in**: `scripts/{packages,pack,publish,smoke-packages}.ts`; `apps/cli/{tsdown.config.ts,package.json,README.md}`, `apps/cli/src/{version,template,cli}.ts`, `commands/init.ts`; `packages/{sdk,runtime,manifest}/{tsdown.config.ts,package.json,README.md}`; `release-please-config.json`, `.release-please-manifest.json`; `.github/workflows/{release,ci}.yml`; `docs/sdk.md` §5, §18.

## D-123 — A GitHub App per workspace, and CI that deploys with GitHub Actions' identity, no token

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Completes D-121 (its "Rejected: a GitHub App or webhooks" no longer holds) and D-004 (the deploy action has a name)
- **Decision**:
  1. **CI with no token.** `POST /deploy`, `DELETE /deploy`, `/promote` and `/rollback` take a GitHub Actions identity token (OIDC, issuer `https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com`, RS256, keys from its JWKS) whose audience is the workspace's address `https://<workspace>.<domain>`. The gateway reads the `repository` and `repository_id` claims and deploys the tool linked to that repository in this workspace; none is a 403. One `deployCaller` (`deploy/deploy.ts`) takes this or a `fgt.` deployment token, for every route that took the token. The RS256 check is shared with the OIDC sign-in (`identity/authn/jwt.ts`).
  2. **A repository by its number.** `tool_sources.repo_id` (migration `0034`) keeps GitHub's number when it is known: a renamed repository keeps its tool, and one deleted then made again under the same name is not the linked one. A link with no number matches the name, without case (`toolByRepository`).
  3. **A GitHub App per workspace** (every route is served on `<workspace>.<domain>`, so the app is the workspace's): made from the console with GitHub's manifest flow (`/github/app/new`, `/github/app/callback`, a state signed with `AUTH_SECRET`, an admin's session at both ends), installed on organisations or accounts (`/github/app/installed`, checked with the app's own JWT). Its private key, webhook secret and client secret are sealed under `github-app/<workspace>` (migration `0035`, registry module `github`, re-sealed with the rest, D-108). Its permissions: contents, workflows, administration and statuses in writing, metadata in reading; its event: `delete`.
  4. **The webhook** (`/github/webhook`): each delivery is checked against the sealed secret (`X-Hub-Signature-256`). A deleted branch of a linked repository closes its preview; an installation created or deleted on GitHub is recorded. Other events are acknowledged.
  5. **The console** (`/api/github…`, admin): Settings → GitHub makes, shows and forgets the app. "Add from GitHub" lists the repositories its installations see (with `forgeron.config.ts` and their workflow) and links one in a click, adding the workflow when asked; without the app the dialog with a token stays. "New tool" makes a private repository in an organisation the app is installed on, links it, then commits the files of `forgeron init`, so its first push deploys. GitHub lets an app make a repository in an organisation only: an account is refused, with the reason.
  6. **After a deployment** of a repository tool whose owner has the app, a commit status `forgeron/<environment>` carries the address. It never fails the deployment.
  7. **The deploy action** stays in this repository, which is public: `forgeron-sh/monorepo/.github/actions/deploy@v<version>`. It installs the tool's dependencies (bun with a `bun.lock`, npm otherwise) before `forgeron test`, deploys when a token or GitHub's identity is there, and runs by default the CLI of its own release (read from `apps/cli/package.json` at the tag). The generated workflow has `id-token: write` and no secret; `FORGERON_ENDPOINT` (the action's `endpoint`) names the workspace when `.forgeron/project.json` does not.
  8. **`packages/scaffold`**: the files of a new tool and of a marketplace, as pure functions, used by `forgeron init` and by the gateway. Bundled into the CLI, not published.
  9. **The install**: `forgeron platform install --official-marketplace <owner/name>` sets `OFFICIAL_MARKETPLACE`, kept from one install to the next. The gateway is built with its release and commit, shown in Install information; a tool it makes depends on the SDK of that release.
- **Why**: copying a token into each repository's secrets was the step people missed, and it expired after a year; GitHub already says which repository runs. An app lets the console see repositories, make one, and close a preview when its branch goes, without asking for anything else.
- **Rejected**: one Forgeron app for every install (the app's webhook and callbacks are a workspace's address); a personal access token kept by the platform (tied to a person, wider than needed); an action in its own repository (`forgeron-sh/deploy-action`), useless while this one is public; building on the platform (D-121).
- **Not done**: the app is not created anywhere, and no real GitHub was called: everything is tested against a simulated GitHub. The first hundred repositories of each installation only. A repository linked by name before its number was known keeps no number until it is linked again. Pull requests are not commented on.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/github/`, `identity/authn/jwt.ts`, `identity/sign-in/oidc.ts`, `deploy/{deploy,promote}.ts`, `platform/{github,bearer,workspace,app,test-app}.ts`, `gateway.ts`, `compose.ts`, `version.ts`, `scripts/build.ts`; `packages/registry/src/modules/{github,templates}/`, `reseal.ts`, `migrations.golden.json`; `packages/platform-contract/src/{github,workspace,addresses}.ts`; `packages/scaffold/`; `apps/cli/src/ci-credential.ts`, `commands/{init,deploy,preview,platform,gateway-identity}.ts`, `template.ts`, `tsdown.config.ts`; `.github/actions/deploy/`; `apps/console/src/features/{settings,tools}/`, `settings.tsx`, `workspace.ts`, `mock/install.ts`; `scripts/lifecycle.ts`; `docs/self-host.md`, `sdk.md` §15, `lifecycle.md`, `console-ux.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## D-124 — A workspace admin calls a tool's actions only through the tool's roles; the card tells them how to give one

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Replaced by D-127 (the admin's access comes from the tool's mapping, `admin` gets every permission by default; the card no longer tells an admin to give themselves a tool's role) · Completes D-039 (the matrix) and D-061 (the catalog)
- **Decision**:
  1. **No implicit access.** The workspace's `admin` role administers: it deploys, promotes, approves, edits the matrix and the members. It does not call a tool's actions by itself. At call time the runtime's one check (`mayAccess`) reads the person's roles and the matrix, and `admin` is a role like any other there: it opens an action only if the tool declares a role named `admin`, or the matrix allows it. The catalog card (`findCard`, `listCatalog`) uses the same check, so it says `requestable` to an admin with no role of the tool, and the runtime refuses that admin's call.
  2. **The card says what an admin can do about it.** On the tool's Overview, an admin whose roles open nothing is told that being an admin manages the tool and does not use it, and to give themselves one of the roles that open it in Members, or to change what each role may call in the Access tab. Only a person who is not an admin is told to ask an admin.
- **Why**: the card was right and its words were wrong: it told the only person who can grant the role to ask for it. Giving admins every action of every tool would open data and side effects (writes, deletions, a connector acting as the person) that a tool's author scoped to a role, on MCP, API, CLI and Web alike; D-023's rule (the workspace roles are enforced identically on every interface) would gain an exception no audit line shows.
- **Rejected**: an implicit grant to `admin` in `mayAccess` (an elevation the tool's author did not declare); a card that says `granted` while the runtime refuses (the two must agree).
- **Not done**: the library sheet (`/library/<tool>`) does not show roles or access, so it has nothing to change; an admin who adds a tool there without a role of it is refused at the first call, with the runtime's words.
- **Applied in**: `apps/console/src/features/portal/tool-card.tsx`; `packages/registry/src/catalog.test.ts`; `docs/console-ux.md` (Catalog API).

## D-125 — Channels: production and permanent channels take only releases of the branch they follow; one temporary channel per branch; no promotion

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Replaces D-055 in part, D-119, D-113 points 1 to 4, D-121 points 2, 4 and 5; D-120's "branch" becomes "channel"
- **Decision** ([`lifecycle.md`](lifecycle.md)):
  1. **Two words.** A **branch** (GitHub) is where people work; Git is the versioning and the history, and GitHub is the source of truth: Forgeron never moves code between branches. A **channel** (Forgeron) is where a tool is distributed; each has its own database and its own access rule.
  2. **Permanent channels.** `production` always exists, is unique and is the default. An admin may add others per tool (`staging`, `recette`): `POST /api/tools/{tool}/channels` `{ name, follows, autoUpdate }`, `PUT` and `DELETE /api/tools/{tool}/channels/{channel}` (the name typed to confirm; the Worker and the database go; production is never removed). A permanent channel **follows one branch** (production: the source's production branch, `main` by default) and takes **only releases** of it. A marketplace tool's permanent channels follow the marketplace's releases, as before.
  3. **A release** is a build whose `changelog.json` says `released: true`: no change pending in `changes/`, and the version is the one `forgeron release` wrote (the compiler writes it). A build of an older CLI, without the field, is a release when its changelog has entries. Each version keeps the branch whose push made it (`versions.branch`): a channel's update is the newest release of its branch that it does not serve. **Pre-releases are not supported**: `forgeron release` numbers `x.y.z` only, and a version's name already carries `-preview.<branch>`, so a `-rc.N` filter would need its own field; not done.
  4. **The update.** When a release lands on a branch, each permanent channel that follows it and updates by itself takes it when nothing stops it (no approval to give, migrations that apply); the others wait for an admin's _Update_, which approves what the release asks for in the same step (D-121). `POST /api/tools/{tool}/update` takes `channel` (production by default) and `allowDestructive`; `withoutRelease` is gone: a build that is not a release never reaches a permanent channel. `GET /api/tools/{tool}/source` answers every permanent channel with its update and the **plan** of D-113 (conflicts against the channel's own database, the prompt for an agent).
  5. **Temporary channels.** Each pushed branch is one (today's preview, unchanged rows): it takes every push with no version needed, starts empty (seed or empty per `preview.data`), shows the branch's commits as Forgeron recorded them (its deployments: sha, message, author, date), and goes when the branch is deleted. Copying data between temporary channels stays (D-121); never from or to a permanent channel.
  6. **No promotion.** `POST /promote`, `POST /promote/plan`, `forgeron promote` (with `--from-branch`), the console's _Promote to production_ and the admin MCP's `tool_promote` and `tool_promote_plan` are removed. `POST /deploy` takes a branch only; `environment: production` is refused (`NOT_A_BRANCH`), and `forgeron deploy` has no `--env`. **Rollback stays**: a permanent channel goes back to a release it served (`POST /rollback` `{ environment: "channel", channel }`, `forgeron rollback --channel`, `tool_rollback`), no new code.
  7. **Access.** The rules of D-117 apply to every channel: production's is the tool's availability; every other channel has its own (`channel_access`, from D-119's pilot rules) and its people add it to their library (`person_channels`). Admins and developers always reach a channel other than production; who can make a temporary channel is who can push to the repository.
  8. **Addresses and references.** Production `/t/<tool>/`, a temporary channel `/t/<tool>@<branch>/`, another permanent channel `/t/<tool>~<name>/`. Outside production a channel is named by its **reference**: the branch, or `~<name>`. Git refuses `~` in a branch name, so the two never clash. The same reference is the channel's access key, its library entry, the `{branch}` of `/api/tools/{tool}/previews/{branch}/availability` and `/api/library/{tool}/pilots/{branch}`, the `branch` query of the manifest, catalog and database routes, and the console's `/tools/<tool>/tree/<ref>`. A channel's name: lowercase letters, digits and `-`, starting with a letter, 30 at most, not `production`, `preview`, `local` or `channel`.
  9. **Hosting.** A new environment kind, `channel`: its Worker and database are `<workspace>--<tool>--channel-<name>` (no branch or other environment can take that name). It is guarded as production is (a release, approvals, no destructive migration without `allowDestructive`), and never seeded. Tool secrets and connections stay per kind: production has its own, every other channel uses the `preview` ones, so production's credentials never leave production. A person's secrets for a channel bind to `preview:<ref>`.
  10. **Migrations** (one way): `0036_channels` rebuilds `environments` with the kind `channel` and three columns (`channel`, `follows`, `auto_update`), copying `deployments` aside as `0019_remove_pilot` did, and adds `versions.branch`, filled from the previews that first served each version. `0037_channel_access` renames `preview_availability` to `channel_access` and `person_previews` to `person_channels` (`branch` becomes `channel`). Every existing row is kept: production stays production, a preview becomes a temporary channel, a pilot rule becomes a channel's access.
  11. **Console.** The tool page's tabs _Branches_ and _Versions_ become _Channels_ (the permanent channels with their updates, add, remove, update by itself, roll back; then the branches with their commits and _Close channel_) and _Releases_ (releases only; builds on demand). The header picks a channel. Availability says who may use the channel. The library marks _Channel · staging_ or _Branch · feat_.
  12. **CLI.** `forgeron rollback [--channel <name>]`; `forgeron db status|shell --channel <name>`; the deploy action no longer passes `--env`.
- **Why**: promotion moved code from one branch's preview to production outside Git, so production could serve what no branch held, and a build with no release could reach the people who use the tool. With channels, Git decides what is in a branch, a release decides what is distributed, and Forgeron only distributes.
- **Rejected**: a parallel table of channels beside `environments` (two places for what a deployment targets); storing a permanent channel as a preview row named `~<name>` (every rule of a preview, seed, data copy, close on branch delete, would need an exception); a channel with its own tool secrets (a rebuild of `secrets` and `connections` for a need nobody stated; to revisit); keeping `withoutRelease` for an emergency (a fix is a release: `forgeron change`, `forgeron release`, push).
- **Not done**: pre-releases; a CLI command to update a channel (`forgeron api POST /api/tools/<tool>/update` does it); the GitHub API for a branch's commits (Forgeron shows the deployments it recorded); per-channel tool secrets; removing a branch's access rule and library entries when its channel goes (they stay unused, as in D-119).
- **Breaking for tool repositories**: `forgeron promote` is gone; `forgeron deploy --env production` is refused (release on the branch production follows instead); the deploy action's `environment` input is gone; a workflow or script that promoted, or deployed to production directly, must release instead. Builds made by an older CLI carry no `released` field: their entries decide.
- **Not verified**: the migrations ran against an in-memory D1 (`channels-migration.test.ts`), not a real one: D1's foreign keys during the rebuild are the part to watch, as for `0019`; a permanent channel's Worker on a real account; the console checked in the preview harness only.
- **Applied in**: `packages/hosting/src/{core,deploy}.ts`, `self-host/names.ts`; `packages/compiler/src/changelog.ts`; `packages/registry/src/{channels,deploy,update-plan,catalog}.ts`, `modules/{catalog,templates,library}/`, `kernel/errors.ts`, `migrations.golden.json`; `packages/platform-contract/src/{bearer,sources,tools,library,database,database-admin,catalog,addresses}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/deploy/{deploy,rollback}.ts`, `sources/update.ts`, `platform/{sources,library,database-admin,marketplaces,bearer}.ts`, `dispatch/`, `console-api/`, `library/library.ts`, `identity/mcp-oauth/mcp-oauth.ts`, `admin-mcp/tools.ts`, `tool-address.ts`; `apps/cli/src/commands/{deploy,rollback,db,platform}.ts`; `.github/actions/deploy/`; `apps/console/src/features/{tools,portal}/`, `mock/`; `packages/ui/src/badge.tsx`; `docs/lifecycle.md`, `sdk.md` §5, §7, §15, §16, §17, `console-ux.md`, `self-host.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## D-126 — A workspace on any host: mapped hosts and a default workspace beside the subdomain; one primary origin per workspace

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Completes `sdk.md` §7 (a workspace is one label under the base domain) and D-123 (the audience of CI tokens)
- **Decision** ([`self-host.md`](self-host.md), "Serving a workspace on any address"):
  1. **Which workspace a host serves.** Two optional gateway settings beside `BASE_DOMAIN`: `WORKSPACE_HOSTS` (`host=workspace,host=workspace`: exact hosts, checked first) and `DEFAULT_WORKSPACE` (single-workspace mode: any host that is neither mapped nor `<workspace>.<BASE_DOMAIN>`, checked last). With neither, nothing changes. `BASE_DOMAIN` becomes optional when one of them is set. One resolver (`workspaceHosts`, `kernel/workspace.ts`) answers for the gateway, the dispatcher and both sign-ins.
  2. **One primary origin per workspace**: its first mapped host, else `https://<workspace>.<BASE_DOMAIN>`; a default workspace with neither uses each request's origin. It is the address the gateway shows, mails and checks, whatever host the request came on: the console's address, tool, channel and MCP addresses in the console and the catalog, invitation and secret mails, the OIDC and SAML redirect URIs and the connectors' consent callback, the GitHub App's manifest and redirects, the deploy workflow's endpoint, and the deployment addresses.
  3. **The audience of a GitHub Actions token is the primary origin only**, on any host: a token that names another accepted host is refused. One audience, stated once, is easier to reason about than a list that grows with each host.
  4. **On another accepted host**: the console, `/t/<tool>`, sign-in by email code, CLI and deploy tokens work as on the primary origin. Cookies stay host cookies (`__Host-`, no `Domain`): a session belongs to the host that set it. Single sign-on started there (`GET /auth/login` that reaches OIDC or SAML) is redirected to the primary origin first, where the provider sends people back. MCP OAuth answers with the host the client connects to (its documents name that host), since its authorization page needs that host's session; its tokens name a tool, so they work on every host.
  5. **Install.** `forgeron platform install --host <host>=<workspace>` (repeatable; `workers.dev` stands for the gateway's own workers.dev host; `none` forgets them), `--default-workspace <workspace>` (`none` forgets it) and `--workers-dev` (reads the account's workers.dev subdomain, turns on `POST /workers/scripts/<worker>/subdomain` with previews off, prints the address). They are kept on later runs like the identity settings (`inherit`). `--domain` is optional with `--workers-dev` or `--host`: without it no zone, record or route is touched and the base domain of the previous version is kept. A first install needs `--domain`, `--host` or `--default-workspace`. `forgeron platform status` shows the hosts, the default and the workers.dev address, and checks the route only with `--domain`.
- **Why**: the gateway knew a workspace only by `<workspace>.<BASE_DOMAIN>`, so an install whose domain became unreachable (a registrar suspension) could not be served anywhere else, not even on the Worker's own workers.dev address. Addresses were built from `BASE_DOMAIN` in many places; one primary origin keeps what must stay stable (the GitHub App, CI tokens, identity provider registrations) in one place.
- **Rejected**: accepting every served host as a CI audience (a token minted for one host would be good on all, and the list would change with each mapping); the request's host as the address in mails and the console (a link would depend on where the sender happened to be); a cookie shared across hosts (`Domain` cannot span unrelated hosts, and workers.dev is a public suffix); JSON for `WORKSPACE_HOSTS` (a plain list is easier to type in the dashboard); a separate `PRIMARY_HOST` setting (the first mapped host says it without a second place to keep in step).
- **Not done**: the install does not route a mapped host other than workers.dev to the gateway (a route or a custom domain is the operator's); `--workers-dev` never turns the address off; the CLI's `--workspace <host>` still reads the workspace from the host's first label, so a workers.dev address is given to `forgeron login` and `forgeron link` with `--endpoint`.
- **Not verified**: the workers.dev calls (`GET /accounts/<id>/workers/subdomain`, `POST /accounts/<id>/workers/scripts/<worker>/subdomain`) are written from Cloudflare's API reference and tested against a simulated account only, not against Cloudflare; the gateway on a real workers.dev address.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/kernel/workspace.ts`, `compose.ts`, `gateway.ts`, `env.ts`, `dispatch/dispatch.ts`, `identity/sign-in/{auth,email-auth}.ts`, `platform/app.ts`, `integration/gateway-hosts.test.ts`; `packages/platform-contract/src/workspace.ts`; `apps/cli/src/commands/{platform,platform-status,gateway-identity}.ts`; `apps/console/src/workspace.ts`, `features/settings/pages/general.tsx`; `docs/self-host.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `console-ux.md`.

## D-127 — Tools declare permissions and suggest profiles; the workspace owns its roles and maps them to each tool

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Replaced in part by D-137 (no workspace roles: profiles are given to `everyone` and teams; the manifest key is `profiles`) · Replaces D-124 (the admin's access, the card's wording) · Completes D-039 (the matrix stays, as exceptions), D-117 and D-125 (availability and channel access are unchanged)
- **Decision** ([`sdk.md`](sdk.md) §3, "Access"; [`console-ux.md`](console-ux.md), the Access tab):
  1. **Three notions.** _Organisation roles_ belong to the workspace only: `admin`, `member`, and the ones an admin names (`support`, `sales`). A tool never creates or names one. _Tool permissions_ are what a tool declares: the levels its actions and views require (`access: "read"`). _Tool profiles_ are named sets of permissions the tool suggests (`user` = `read`, `manager` = `read` + `write`); one is the default.
  2. **Config.** `profiles: { user: { label, grants, default: true } }` in `forgeron.config.ts`. `roles` stays, deprecated, read as profiles, so a config for `@forgeron/sdk` 0.3 keeps building; `defineConfig` refuses both keys at once and two defaults. The manifest keeps the key `roles` for the profiles and gains `default` on one of them (the schema refuses two). With none marked, the default is the profile with the fewest permissions, the first declared on a tie.
  3. **Mapping per tool, set by an admin.** Table `role_profiles` (`tool_id`, `role`, `kind`: `all` | `profile` | `custom` | `none`, `profile`, `permissions`). A tool's first deployment or first version on a channel writes the default mapping, if the tool has none: `admin` → all permissions, `member` → the default profile. It is a written, visible default, edited in the Access tab (`PUT /api/tools/<tool>/access-matrix` with `profiles: [{ role, grant }]`, in one batch with the cells) and by `access_grant` / `access_revoke` with `tool` and `role` and no `action`. Every change is audited (`profile:<role>`). A tool with no row yet answers its default mapping.
  4. **One check.** `mayAccess` (`@forgeron/manifest`) reads, for each of the person's workspace roles, the exception of the matrix if any, else whether the action's permission is among those the role is mapped to. The gateway resolves it (`registry.accessFor`) and forwards `roles`, `permissions` per role and `access` resolved for every action and view of the current manifest: a runtime built before D-127 reads only `access` and applies the mapping with no new version. Without the gateway (`forgeron dev --as`, `createTestTool`), a role is read with the default mapping: a profile of that name, else `admin` all and `member` the default profile. The catalog card, the library sheet, views, the playground and the MCP listing use the same check.
  5. **Migration `0038_tool_profiles`** (one-way). For each tool with a version (production's manifest, else the latest): each role of the tool is mapped to the workspace role of the same name (people who held it keep their access); each role found in `role_access` too; then `admin` → all and `member` → the default profile where no row exists. The matrix rows stay, as exceptions. On an install where `echo` has the role `reader`, `admin`, `member` and `reader` are mapped and an admin calls `echo` at once.
  6. **Console.** Access tab: profiles, a row per workspace role with a select (no access, a profile, custom permissions, all), then the exceptions by action. Members and Roles: workspace roles only (`admin`, `member`, `developer` offered, others typed); the hint no longer speaks of tools' role names. Tool card: an admin whose roles open nothing is sent to the Access tab; anyone else asks an admin. The library sheet says the same (`allowed` in its answer). The marketplace sheet lists _Profiles_, the default marked.
- **Why**: tools imposed their role names on the organisation: to use Echo a person needed a workspace role literally named `reader`, and an admin could call nothing until given it. The decision moves the vocabulary where it belongs: the organisation names its people's roles, a tool says what it needs, and the admin connects the two once per tool.
- **Rejected**: keeping the name match as a default for new tools (tools would still name workspace roles); an implicit `admin` grant in `mayAccess` (D-124's objection stands: the access is a written default the admin can change, not a rule); renaming the manifest key to `profiles` (every stored build and every deployed runtime reads `roles`); making `member` a role every person holds implicitly (it would open every tool to everyone; an admin gives `member` like any other role).
- **Not done**: the exceptions are resolved against the current manifest (production's, else the latest); an action that exists only on another channel's version falls back to the mapping in a new runtime, and to the tool's own roles in a runtime built before D-127. `member` is not given to existing people by the migration. No `role.default-changed` entry in the changelog detection.
- **Breaking for**: tool code that reads `ctx.user.roles` to find a tool role name (they are workspace roles now); people who held a workspace role named like a tool role of a tool deployed after the migration (a new tool maps only `admin` and `member`); MCP clients calling `access_grant` with a `tool` and a role of the tool and no `action` (now a mapping, which needs `profile`, `permissions` or `all`). `PUT …/access-matrix` accepts a role that is not mapped yet.
- **Applied in**: `packages/manifest/src/{access,schema,catalog}.ts`; `packages/sdk/src/config.ts`; `packages/compiler/src/manifest.ts`; `packages/runtime/src/{engine,types}.ts`; `packages/registry/src/modules/access/*`, `catalog.ts`, `deploy.ts`, `migrations.golden.json`, `tool-profiles-migration.test.ts`; `packages/platform-contract/src/{access-matrix,library,marketplaces}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/{dispatch/user-secrets,views/serve,playground/serve,platform/access-matrix,platform/library,platform/marketplaces,admin-mcp/tools}.ts`; `apps/console/src/{access-matrix,members}.tsx`, `features/portal/{tool-card,library-page}.tsx`, `features/tools/tabs/access-tab.tsx`, `features/settings/pages/roles.tsx`; `apps/cli/src/dev.ts`; `packages/scaffold/src/tool.ts`; `templates/*/forgeron.config.ts`; `docs/sdk.md`, `console-ux.md`, `self-host.md`, `lifecycle.md`.

## D-128 — The console's navigation: the person's space, Administration, Settings, one place per page; everyone holds `member`

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Active, point 5 replaced by D-137 (no `member` role: `everyone` is a subject) · Completes D-056 and D-058 (the shell and the settings area), D-117 and D-118 (the library and the two sheets), D-127 (the `member` role)
- **Decision** ([`console-ux.md`](console-ux.md), "Shell", "Administration", "Member space"):
  1. **Three places.** The sidebar has the person's own space on top, for everyone and with no heading: **Home** (`/`), **My tools** (`/library`, the tools they added) and **Discover** (`/discover`, the tools the workspace makes available to them that they have not added). Then **Administration**, shown only to those who may open it: **Tools** (`/admin/tools`, every tool of the workspace, the admin's view: channels, releases, availability, access), **Marketplace** (`/admin/marketplace`), **Members** (`/admin/members`), **Roles and access** (`/admin/roles`). **Settings** stays at the bottom with its own area: Account for everyone; Workspace (General, Sign-in, Domains, GitHub) and Operations (Audit log, Usage and seats, Sealing key, Install information) for admins. The settings group once called _Administration_ is renamed _Operations_, so the word names one place.
  2. **One place per page.** Members was at `/members` and `/settings/members`, the audit log at `/audit` and `/settings/audit`: each is now in one place (Members in Administration, the audit log in Settings, Operations). Roles and access leaves Settings for Administration: who holds which role is managed beside the people, not configured. Home no longer repeats the lists: it shows the person's first six tools with _All my tools_, and how many tools they can still add with _Discover tools_; an admin also sees the workspace's numbers and the first-run checklist.
  3. **Old addresses redirect** in the console (`shell/redirects.ts`, a replace, no new history entry), the rest of the address kept: `/members` and `/settings/members` → `/admin/members` (`?invite` kept), `/settings/roles` → `/admin/roles`, `/audit` → `/settings/audit`, `/tools/...` → `/admin/tools/...` (a tool, a channel, `?tab=`), `/marketplace/...` → `/admin/marketplace/...`. An old address is never a route, so no menu or search shows it twice. The gateway, the CLI and the mails print only `/library/<tool>`, `/settings/github` and `/my/secrets`, which do not move; the gateway's API is unchanged.
  4. **Guards.** Every `/admin/...` route is for admins, except Tools, which stays open to developers (D-118): a developer who is not an admin sees Administration with Tools only. Anyone else typing an address there is told the page is for admins, as before. Shortcuts: `g` then `h` Home, `m` My tools, `d` Discover, `t` Tools, `k` Marketplace, `p` Members, `r` Roles and access, `s` Settings; the audit log loses its letter (the search finds it).
  5. **Everyone holds `member`.** The gateway's one resolver of who is calling (`identity/authn/caller.ts`) adds `member` to the roles a person was given (`workspaceRoles`, `@forgeron/manifest`). Every check reads those roles: a tool's call (`mayAccess` with the role mapping and the exceptions), views, the playground, the catalog card, the library, the console. A person given no role gets what each tool maps `member` to (by default its default profile, D-127), and only on a tool they may add: availability (D-117, D-125) is unchanged, so holding `member` opens no tool by itself, and an availability rule that names `member` opens the tool to everyone. An admin's exception on `member` still wins over the mapping. The stored roles are unchanged: `member` is added when the person is read, so there is no migration, an explicit `member` already stored is harmless, and taking it away changes nothing. `/whoami`, `/api/workspace` and `ctx.user.roles` show it. The console's Members page shows `member` beside the given roles and no longer warns _No role_; its role pickers do not offer it; Roles and access counts everyone for `member`; the invitation page says `member`.
- **Why**: the sidebar mixed what a person uses with what an admin manages (Tools, a technical screen, sat next to Home), and two screens existed twice, so a person had to guess which one was the real one. Three places with one name each, and one address per page, make the console readable for both. `member` was a role an admin had to give before a person could use anything, while D-127 already made it the role of "a person of the workspace": computing it removes a step that every admin had to remember, and a migration that would have to be kept in step with every new member.
- **Rejected**: keeping `/tools` and `/marketplace` and only regrouping the sidebar (the addresses would not say which place a page belongs to, and a guard by prefix would be impossible); Tools for admins only (it would take the technical screens away from developers, against D-118); Home as the two full lists, as before (My tools and Discover would repeat it); writing `member` into every member's stored roles with a migration (it would have to be written again for every new member, and an admin could take it away and wonder why nothing changed); adding `member` in the registry's `accessFor` only (the catalog, the library and the console would disagree with the runtime).
- **Not done**: `forgeron dev --as` and `createTestTool` read the roles they are given, without adding `member`; the admin MCP's `access_grant` still accepts `member` for a person (stored, harmless).
- **Breaking for**: links or bookmarks to the old addresses keep working through the redirects; tool code that treated an empty `ctx.user.roles` as "no role" now sees `["member"]`.
- **Applied in**: `packages/manifest/src/access.ts` (`workspaceRoles`); `apps/gateway/src/identity/authn/caller.ts`, `pages/sign-in.ts`, `integration/gateway-implicit-member.test.ts` and the integration tests that read `/whoami`; `apps/console/src/shell/{routes,redirects,app-layout,chrome}.ts(x)`, `router.ts`, `app.tsx`, `features/admin/`, `features/portal/{home,tool-lists,routes,library-page,connect,portal.css}`, `features/{tools,marketplace,settings}/routes.ts`, `members.tsx`, `overview.tsx`, `mock/install.ts`, `README.md`; `docs/console-ux.md`, `lifecycle.md`, `sdk.md`.

## D-129 — A channel is a draft or published, in the header of the tool page; the Availability tab goes

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Completes D-117 (availability), D-119 and D-125 point 7 (who reaches a channel other than production), D-128 (everyone holds `member`)
- **Decision** ([`console-ux.md`](console-ux.md), "Publication"; [`lifecycle.md`](lifecycle.md), "Channels"):
  1. **Two states, on the existing rule.** A channel is a **draft** while its rule is `nobody` (or, for a channel other than production, while it has none), and **published** when the rule is `everyone`, `roles` or `people`. The rule, its table (`tool_availability`, `channel_access`), the contract (`GET`/`PUT /api/tools/{tool}/availability`, `…/previews/{ref}/availability`) and the audit lines are unchanged: only the console's words change. Production with no rule (a tool deployed before libraries) stays open and reads _Published · everyone, without adding it_.
  2. **A draft is for admins.** On production nothing changes (`nobody` already let only admins add a tool). On a **permanent channel** (`~<name>`), a developer no longer reaches it by being a developer: in draft only admins do; once published to them (by a role such as `developer`, by name, or to everyone) a developer adds it like anyone (`reachesChannelAlways`, `@forgeron/registry`, used by `pilotAccess` and the dispatcher's channel check). A **branch's temporary channel keeps D-125's rule**: admins and developers always reach it, draft or not, since it is where developers work and who can make one is who can push to the repository; publishing it opens it to others.
  3. **In the header.** Right of the channel picker, for admins: the state (_Draft · Admins only_, _Draft · Admins and developers_ on a branch, _Published · everyone_, _Published · developer, support_, or the people) and a split button. Draft: _Publish_, with a chevron menu holding _Who has access_. Published: _Manage sharing_, with _Who has access_ and _Unpublish_ (confirmed; it says how many people lose access, and their library keeps the tool). The dialog of _Publish_ and _Manage sharing_ offers everyone, some roles (any workspace role but `admin`, who always has access, and `member`, which is everyone since D-128) or some people found by a search field, and says before saving how many people will have access and why. A person who is not an admin sees no state and no button (the rule's routes are for admins).
  4. **The Availability tab goes.** `?tab=availability` opens the tool page (the parameter is dropped); installing from a marketplace opens the tool page, a draft. The channel picker loses its _Channel_ label and is as wide as its value, its chevron right after the text.
- **Why**: who may use a tool was a tab among nine, read only by those who opened it, while it is the first thing to know about a channel. Two words, draft and published, say it at a glance and match what an admin does: prepare, then share. Keeping the four modes underneath keeps every existing rule and capability (people by name included) with no migration. A permanent channel is a release channel an admin prepares for some people (`staging`, `recette`): it should not reach every developer before the admin shares it; a branch's channel is a developer's own work, so it keeps the developers.
- **Rejected**: a new `published` flag beside the rule (two sources for one answer); renaming the modes in the contract (every client and stored row would change for the same meaning); draft for admins only on temporary channels too (a developer could not open the preview of the branch they pushed); keeping the tab beside the header (two places for one rule, against D-128).
- **Not done**: the contract and the audit lines keep the word availability and the rule's four modes; neither the CLI nor the admin MCP sets the rule (unchanged); a person outside the rule who added a channel keeps it in their library, refused at the call, as before.
- **Breaking for**: a developer who used a permanent channel without being given it: an admin publishes it to `developer` to keep that.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/library/{types,public}.ts`, `registry.ts`, `index.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/library/library.ts` (and `library.test.ts`), `compose.ts`; `apps/console/src/features/tools/{publish,register,tools.css}.ts(x)`, `pages/tool-page.tsx`, `tabs/update-panel.tsx`, `features/marketplace/pages/tool-sheet.tsx`, `mock/install.ts`; `docs/console-ux.md`, `lifecycle.md`, `sdk.md`.

## D-130 — `forgeron dev` serves a tool's views; the build generates one mount entry per view

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Completes D-016 (what it listed as not done: the mount script) and D-082 (CORS on `/_f/call`) for the local mode
- **Decision**:
  1. **The build mounts the view.** Each view is built from a generated entry that imports the view, reads the page's `#forgeron-bootstrap` JSON and renders `ViewHost` with `createHttpRuntime`. The view, the hooks and React are in one bundle, so there is one copy of React (a view built apart from its host crashed on `useState`). The default export of `views/<name>.js` is still the `defineView` object.
  2. **The local host plays the gateway's part.** In `forgeron dev`, `GET <view path>` answers the page with the sandbox CSP (D-016), the access level checked first, the bootstrap with the developer's identity, and `/_f/views/<file>` serves the built files. `/_f/call` answers `OPTIONS` and carries CORS headers: a sandboxed page has an opaque origin, so its calls are cross-origin. The dev command prints a `Web` line per view.
  3. **No token locally**: the Worker trusts its host, which sets the identity (as before for the API).
- **Why**: a view could be built but never seen: `forgeron dev` listed MCP, API and CLI only, and the platform's gateway does not serve views yet. A developer could not try a page.
- **Rejected**: a separate views origin (D-082); serving the files from the Worker (it holds no static assets); dropping the sandbox in dev (the page would work here and fail on the platform).
- **Not done**: the platform's gateway still does not serve views (`serveView` is not wired into the dispatcher); the 15-minute token is not renewed; CSS is not bundled (a view carries its own `<style>`); the built-in browser of the desktop app refuses a document served with `CSP: sandbox`, so a test there goes through a proxy that drops that header.
- **Applied in**: `packages/compiler/src/build.ts`; `packages/hosting/src/{local,local-views}.ts`; `apps/cli/src/{dev.ts,commands/dev.ts}`.

## D-131 — A tool reads the workspace's people through `ctx.directory`, only what it asked for

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Follows D-127 (roles are the workspace's) and the resource approval of D-096
- **Decision**:
  1. **A resource, declared and approved.** `resources.directory: { why, members?, roles?, avatars?, teams? }` (`directory()` in `forgeron.config.ts`). Nothing is open by default: `ctx.user` carries the caller's `id`, `email`, `name` (and `avatar` once a source exists), and the other people, their roles, their pictures and the teams are one flag each. An admin approves them as any resource (the install shows a _Directory_ line).
  2. **`ctx.directory`**: `me()`, `member(idOrEmail)`, `members({ query?, team?, limit? })`, `teams({ mine? })`, `team(id)`, `inTeam(id)`. A call that needs a flag the tool did not declare is refused (`FORBIDDEN`). The caller is always found by `member()`; nobody else without `members`. At most 500 members per call.
  3. **Two filters.** The gateway sends the Worker `x-forgeron-directory` only when the tool declared the resource, with the fields it named (members of this workspace only); the runtime filters again by the manifest, so a snapshot that carries too much leaks nothing.
  4. **The rule belongs to the action.** A tool that shares something with a person checks them with `ctx.directory.member(...)` in the action; a view's suggestions are only a help. A person outside the workspace cannot be invited, whatever a caller sends.
  5. **Local and tests.** `forgeron dev` reads `.forgeron/local/directory.json` (a small made-up directory without it); `--user <email>` chooses who the developer is, so a note shared with someone is seen from their side. `createTestTool({ directory })` does the same in tests.
- **Why**: a tool for companies cannot take an email from its caller and trust it: the platform is the one that knows who belongs to the workspace. The notes tool shared a note with any address because it had no way to ask.
- **Rejected**: giving every tool the whole directory (an enterprise rule is that a tool sees what it needs); a runtime call from the Worker to the gateway (a Worker cannot always reach its own gateway, and each call would need a token); looking people up through the admin MCP (it is the admin's, not the tool's).
- **Not done**: pictures have no source yet (a member has no avatar column; OIDC `picture` is not stored), so `avatar` is empty on the platform; the snapshot is a header, capped at 500 members, which a very large workspace will outgrow (a bound lookup is the next step); the matrix does not know the directory.
- **Applied in**: `packages/manifest/src/schema.ts`; `packages/runtime/src/{directory,engine,types}.ts`; `packages/sdk/src/{config,context,testing/tool}.ts`; `packages/compiler/src/{build,check}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/user-secrets.ts`; `packages/hosting/src/local.ts`; `apps/cli/src/{dev,dev-directory,resources}.ts`.

## D-132 — Teams: named groups of members, a subject of the access matrix, readable by tools

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Active, point 2 replaced by D-137 (a team is the subject `team:<id>`, not a role; teams carry platform permissions) · Builds on D-127 (the matrix is keyed by role) and D-131 (`ctx.directory`)
- **Decision**:
  1. **A team** is a named group of the workspace's members: an address (`product`, chosen once, 2 to 34 lower case letters, digits and `-`), a name, a description and its members. Tables `teams` and `team_members` (migration `0039_teams`); only members of the workspace can be in one, and a removed member leaves their teams. Console API `GET /api/teams` (anyone signed in), `POST`, `PATCH /api/teams/{id}` (name, description, replace the members) and `DELETE` (admins), each audited. Console page **Administration, Teams**.
  2. **A team is a role in the matrix.** In a tool's Access tab and in the mapping of D-127, a team is the subject `team-<id>`: the same profile, the same per-action exceptions, no second path. The gateway gives a call the person's workspace roles **and** the `team-<id>` of their teams, so access is the union. A workspace role cannot be named `team-…`, and deleting a team deletes its rows of the mapping and exceptions. `ctx.user.roles` therefore lists `team-<id>` entries.
  3. **Tools read teams with `resources.directory.teams`** (D-131): `ctx.directory.teams({ mine? })`, `team(id)`, `inTeam(id)`, `members({ team })`, `ctx.user.teams` and each member's `teams`. A tool that did not ask gets none, not even the caller's own team ids.
  4. **A tool's team space is the tool's rule**: the platform tells which teams the caller is in; a tool that scopes data to a team (`team_id` on its rows) checks `ctx.directory.inTeam(...)` in the action. It cannot read another team's rows by accident because the action filters, and an action that forgets to is a bug of the tool, as for any scoping.
  5. **Local and tests**: `forgeron dev` reads `teams` of `.forgeron/local/directory.json`; `createTestTool({ directory: { members, teams } })`.
- **Why**: companies organise by team, not only by role; giving access to "Product" is one decision, not a role to invent and keep in sync. Reusing the matrix key keeps one mechanism for access.
- **Rejected**: a second kind of subject beside roles (every reader of the matrix and the mapping would need two paths); making a team a role members hold (it would show in Members, and the identity provider's group mapping would collide); tools reading teams by default (an enterprise tool sees what it asked for).
- **Not done**: no nested teams, no team owner or lead; the identity provider's groups do not fill teams (D-127's group mapping gives roles); the Access tab shows `team-<id>` rather than the team's name; the admin MCP and the CLI do not manage teams.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/teams/`, `migrations.ts` and `migrations.golden.json`, `modules/members/store.ts`; `packages/platform-contract/src/{teams,addresses,index}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/platform/teams.ts`, `dispatch/user-secrets.ts`; `apps/console/src/features/admin/pages/teams.tsx`, `features/tools/tabs/access-tab.tsx`, `mock/install.ts`.

## D-133 — A member has a picture: their provider's, or one they chose

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Completes D-131 (`avatars` in `resources.directory` had no source)
- **Decision**:
  1. **Two sources, one wins.** The **provider's picture**: at each OIDC sign-in the gateway reads the ID token's standard `picture` claim and, when the address is new, copies the image (https, png, jpeg or webp, 64 KB at most, 3 seconds) into the member's row. The **chosen picture**: a member sets their own in Settings, Profile (the browser crops it to a 128 px square and sends a webp or jpeg data URL, `PUT /api/me/avatar`; `null` gives it back to the provider's). The chosen one wins; removing it falls back to the provider's.
  2. **Served by the gateway**: `GET /avatars/<member id>?v=<version>`, public and cacheable for good (the version changes with the picture), with a locked-down CSP and `nosniff`. Public because a tool's page is sandboxed and sends no cookie; the member id is random and nothing is listed. Copying the picture keeps the console's CSP (`img-src 'self'`) and does not tell the provider who looks at it.
  3. **Tools see an address, not bytes.** With `directory.avatars`, `ctx.user.avatar` and each member's `avatar` are absolute `…/avatars/<id>?v=…` addresses; without the flag nothing is sent. Columns `avatar_provider`, `avatar_provider_url`, `avatar_custom`, `avatar_version` on `members` (migration `0040_member_avatars`); setting or clearing the chosen one is audited, the provider's copy is not (it happens at each sign-in).
  4. **Console**: the `Avatar` component takes a `src`; the account menu and the Profile page show the picture.
- **Why**: a tool that shows people needs a face, and the platform is the one that knows it. Copying avoids a hot-link to a provider and a CSP hole.
- **Rejected**: storing the picture's bytes in the directory header (a workspace's pictures would exceed any header); serving it behind the session cookie (a sandboxed page has none); refetching the provider's picture at each request.
- **Not done**: only OIDC states `picture`: Entra ID gives none in its ID token (a picture needs a Graph call with its own scope), and SAML and email sign-in have none, so those members have initials until they choose one; the Profile page does not refresh the account menu before the next load; an admin cannot set or clear another member's picture; no size other than 128 px.
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/members/{migrations,store,types}.ts`; `packages/platform-contract/src/{workspace,addresses}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/identity/{avatar,authn/caller,authn/identity,sign-in/oidc}.ts`, `gateway.ts`, `platform/workspace.ts`, `dispatch/user-secrets.ts`; `packages/ui/src/avatar.tsx`; `apps/console/src/features/settings/`, `shell/chrome.tsx`.

## D-134 — The platform serves a tool's pages

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Completes D-016 (what it left: wiring `serveView`, reading the files from the registry), D-082 (CORS) and D-130 (the same page in `forgeron dev`)
- **Decision**:
  1. **A build carries its views.** `Build.views` is the files of `views/` (the generated mount entry of each view and the chunks they share), path to code. `forgeron deploy` and `forgeron bundle` send them, a marketplace sync takes them, the registry keeps them in `builds.views` (migration `0041_build_views`), and a build with none serves no page. They are checked as the docs are: `views/…js` names, 50 files, 2 MB each, 8 MB in all.
  2. **The gateway serves the pages** of the channel's build, before the request reaches the Worker: `GET /t/<tool>[@branch|~channel]/<view path>` answers the page (sandbox CSP, the access level checked first with the person's roles **and teams** and the matrix, the bootstrap with a 15-minute token bound to the tool), and `GET …/_f/views/<file>` the built file (public, with CORS: a sandboxed page sends no cookie). The tool's root opens its first view when none is at `/`. This makes the library's "Open" button (`/t/<tool>/`) lead somewhere.
  3. **Calls from the page** go to `POST …/_f/call/<action>` with `Authorization: Bearer <view token>`; the existing identity path accepts that token (audience `<workspace>/<tool>`, D-015), and the gateway answers the preflight and adds CORS to the answer. A token for another tool is refused as any other.
  4. **Pages are served from the registry**, not from the Worker: the Worker holds no static asset, and a tool Worker is replaced at each deploy while the build row stays.
- **Why**: a view could be built (D-016) and tried locally (D-130) but never opened on a platform: the library said "Open CRM" and the address answered 404.
- **Rejected**: serving the files from the tool's Worker (it would carry hundreds of KB it does not need and answer with the platform's CSP rules left to the tool); a views origin of its own (D-082); a cookie to authenticate the page's calls (a sandboxed page sends none).
- **Not done**: not exercised on Cloudflare itself (the tests run the whole gateway on a memory D1, not a deployed one); a tool's existing builds carry no views until deployed again; the 15-minute token is still not renewed; each view bundles React, so a tool with several views sends it once per view; no cache layer in front of the registry read (the files are immutable by fingerprint, so one is easy to add).
- **Applied in**: `packages/hosting/src/core.ts`; `packages/registry/src/modules/catalog/`, `deploy.ts`; `apps/cli/src/built.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/{compose.ts,deploy/deploy.ts,dispatch/dispatch.ts,marketplace/sync.ts,views/check.ts}`.
- **Amended 13/11/2026 (previews)**: a page's scripts were read **without asking who**, but the dispatcher checked `canPreview` first on a channel other than production, and a sandboxed page sends no cookie: every script of a preview or a draft channel answered `403`, shown by the browser as a CORS error. A page served to a person who may open it, on such a channel, now names a token in the **path** of its scripts (`/_f/a/<token>/views/…`; a query would be lost by the imports between files). The token (scope `view-asset`, the page's lifetime, tool-bound) is good for those files only, and the gateway reads it in place of `canPreview`. Production keeps its plain, cacheable addresses. A refused script now carries `access-control-allow-origin: *`, so that the page and the console report the `403` and not a CORS error. Rejected: letting the scripts of a draft be read by anyone who knows the address (the code of a tool not yet published).
- **Amended 13/11/2026 (preflight)**: the preflight (`OPTIONS`) of a page's call (`/_f/call/<action>`) carries no token, so on a channel other than production `canPreview` refused it before the answer meant for it, and the page's calls were blocked. It is now answered before that check (it says nothing, and is the same for everyone); the call that follows is still checked, and its refusal carries `access-control-allow-origin: *` so that the page reads the `403`. **Not changed**: the page's WebSocket (`/_f/realtime`) is still refused on such a channel, its token being in the address; letting it through on that token would let someone who may not open a draft join its room (the token is bound to the tool, not the channel), and needs a token bound to the channel first.

## D-135 — Realtime: channels, messages, presence, over a WebSocket

- **Date**: 12/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Builds on D-134 (a page and its token), D-131 and D-132 (the person, their teams)
- **Decision**:
  1. **A tool declares its channels** (`realtime.channels` in `forgeron.config.ts`, kept in the manifest): a name (`note`, or an instance `note:<id>`), the `access` level to listen, an optional `send` level for **pages** to send, `presence`, and an optional `authorize` action for an instance. Not a platform resource (nothing to approve: it concerns the tool's own people), so it sits beside `resources`.
  2. **Access is enforced where the tool and the platform agree**: the channel is declared, the person holds the level (matrix included, subject `channel:<name>`), and for an instance the tool's `authorize` action, run **as the person**, says yes. The same rule that reads a note decides who follows it. A refusal says why.
  3. **Actions publish, pages follow.** `ctx.realtime.publish(channel, event, payload?)` and `changed(channel)`; a message leaves only if the action succeeds, is 8 KB at most, 20 per call, and travels in the response as `x-forgeron-realtime` (base64 JSON, about 24 KB at most), which the gateway (or the local host) reads, removes and sends to the room: a tool Worker needs no way to reach the room. The recommended use is a **signal**: listeners read again through the action with their own access (`useAction({ live })`), so realtime shows nothing the action would not.
  4. **Presence and client messages** (`track`, `send`) are the hub's, with limits (50 channels per connection, 30 sends a second, 8 KB); sending needs the channel's `send` level.
  5. **One hub, three hosts.** The hub (`RealtimeHub` in `@forgeron/runtime`, neutral) holds connections, subscriptions and presence; `forgeron dev` runs it on Node's `ws` (people tried at once with `?as=`), the gateway in a Durable Object per workspace, tool and channel (`REALTIME`, `RealtimeRoom`). A page connects to `…/_f/realtime?token=<view token>` (a browser cannot set a header on a WebSocket) and the client reconnects and joins again by itself.
  6. **Hardening found on the way**: the gateway now strips `x-forgeron-directory` and `x-forgeron-realtime` from what a caller sends, as it does the identity headers.
- **Why**: a notes page that shows a colleague's change only after a reload is not a team tool; and the platform is the one that knows who may follow what.
- **Rejected**: Postgres-style change feeds (the tool's data is not the platform's, and a row-level feed would skip the action's access rule); a broadcast without access checks; tool Workers calling the room (a Worker cannot always reach its gateway, and each call would need a token); SSE with polling (not realtime, and a read per tick).
- **Not done**: **the Durable Object has not run on Cloudflare**: its logic is tested through a factory with fakes, the class binding is a thin layer not exercised, and `forgeron platform install` does not create it (a Durable Object class needs a migration, which the Versions upload the install uses cannot carry, so enabling it needs a one-time script upload that does not exist yet); a room keeps its WebSockets open (no hibernation), which costs duration on a busy tool; no message history or replay after a reconnect (a client reads again); presence is per room, not per channel across tools; the 15-minute view token is not renewed, so a long-lived socket outlives it (the connection was authorized once).
- **Applied in**: `packages/manifest/src/schema.ts`; `packages/runtime/src/{realtime,realtime-context,engine}.ts`; `packages/sdk/src/{config,context,react/realtime,react/hooks,react/http,testing/tool}.ts`; `packages/compiler/src/{build,check,manifest}.ts`; `packages/hosting/src/{local,local-realtime}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/{compose,env,worker}.ts`, `dispatch/dispatch.ts`, `realtime/room.ts`; `apps/cli/src/{dev,commands/dev}.ts`.

## D-136 — A build carries its contract; the platform refuses what it cannot run

- **Date**: 13/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Builds on D-115 (bundles), D-116 (marketplaces), D-123 (the gateway knows its release)
- **Decision**:
  1. **A contract number** is the shape of the manifest and of what the runtime offers a tool. `forgeron build` stamps `manifest.forgeron = { contract, sdk }` (the contract of the `@forgeron/sdk` it resolved, and its version). A manifest without it is contract 1.
  2. **A register** (`CONTRACTS` in `@forgeron/manifest`, `compat.ts`) has one line per contract: the SDK versions that stamp it, its status (`supported`, `deprecated` with a `sunset`, `removed` with `removedIn`) and what it brought. Lines are never edited away; a contract only goes supported, deprecated, removed, and a new one is appended whenever a platform of the previous contract could not read the new manifest. `docs/compatibility.md` says when.
  3. **One check, before anything else** (`checkContract`), on the raw manifest, so that a manifest this platform cannot parse still gets a reason. A tool **ahead** of the platform and a **removed** contract are refused; a **deprecated** one is accepted with a warning. The check is in `POST /deploy` (422 `INCOMPATIBLE`, so CI shows it), the marketplace sync (the tool shows its reason in the index instead of "the manifest is not valid") and `deployBuild` (a rollback, a channel update: the reason is the "waiting" message).
- **Why**: a tool installed from a repository built with an SDK newer than the gateway was rejected by the strict manifest schema with no explanation, and nobody could tell the tool was ahead.
- **Rejected**: comparing SDK versions (most releases change nothing a platform reads, so they would refuse working tools); a `requires` range written by the author (it can be wrong, the SDK knows); a lenient schema that ignores unknown fields (the tool would run without the feature it needs).
- **Not done**: the gateway does not yet publish its contract (`GET /compat`) for `forgeron deploy` and the console to warn before the push; the console does not show the warnings of a deprecated contract; a gateway released before this decision still rejects any manifest that has `forgeron` (it cannot be fixed afterwards: update it).
- **Applied in**: `packages/manifest/src/{compat,schema}.ts`; `packages/compiler/src/{build,manifest}.ts`; `packages/registry/src/{deploy,kernel/errors}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/{deploy/deploy,deploy/rollback,marketplace/sync}.ts`.

## D-137 — No roles: teams, `everyone`, and one permission engine

- **Date**: 13/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Replaces D-127's workspace roles, D-128 point 5 and D-132 point 2 · Keeps D-127's tool permissions, profiles and exceptions · Detailed shape: [`spikes/teams-permissions.md`](spikes/teams-permissions.md)
- **Decision**:
  1. **No roles.** A person is in **teams** (any number); every person is part of **everyone**. A grant names a **subject**: `everyone` or `team:<id>`. The words role, `admin`/`member` roles, `team-<id>` and `minRole` leave the code, the API, the console and the docs.
  2. **Two built-in teams** in every workspace: `admin`, which holds every platform permission now and to come (`*`, locked, never empty), and `developer` (build, deploy and publish tools; editable). Neither can be deleted. Admins create any other team.
  3. **Platform permissions** are atomic (`tools:read`, `tools:write`, `tools:publish`, `tools:approve`, `tools:data`, `tools:secrets`, `tools:delete`, `access:read/write`, `members:read/write`, `teams:write`, `marketplace:read/write`, `settings:read/write`, `audit:read`, `usage:read`); a write implies its read. A team is given some; a person holds the union of their teams'. Every platform route, the admin MCP's tools, the console's navigation and buttons read them.
  4. **One engine**, the new package `@forgeron/permissions` (a leaf, published with the others because the manifest reads it): subjects, the catalog, implication, the union, and the rule against escalation: **nobody gives what they do not hold** (changing a team's permissions or members needs every permission the team has, before and after). `@forgeron/manifest` applies the same subjects to a tool's own access.
  5. **A tool's access is given to subjects.** The Access tab has a row for **Everyone** and one per team, by name: no access, a profile, custom permissions, or all; exceptions by action as before. A new tool gets `team:admin` → all and `everyone` → its default profile. Availability and pilot channels name teams.
  6. **The manifest key is `profiles`.** A stored build with `roles` is read as profiles, and the header sent to a Worker carries the subjects under `roles` too, so tools deployed before D-137 keep working without a new version; each legacy reader is one function marked `legacy (D-137)`.
  7. **Migration** (one-way): `admin` and `developer` holders join the built-in teams, any other role becomes a team of the same name with its members, `member` becomes `everyone`, `team-<id>` becomes `team:<id>`, in grants, exceptions, availability, invitations, the join-by-domain setting and the identity provider's group mapping.
- **Why**: roles and teams were two ways to say "a group of people", with two screens and a computed `member` role; a person in several groups is the normal case. Teams are the one group; permissions say what a group may do on Forgeron, profiles what it may do in a tool.
- **Rejected**: keeping roles beside teams (two vocabularies for one thing); an `admin` flag on a person (it would be the role again, and could not be split); coarse permissions per page (a manager who invites people would also change the sign-in); letting a team manager give any permission (anyone with `teams:write` could make themselves admin). 8. **A new contract (3).** The manifest's key changes (`profiles`) and the wire carries subjects, which a platform of contract 2 cannot read: `CONTRACTS` gains contract 3 (D-136), stamped by the SDK that ships this decision. Builds of contracts 1 and 2 keep running (the migration rewrites stored builds; the legacy readers cover the rest).
- **Not done**: nested teams; the identity provider's groups fill teams only at the first sign-in, as roles did.

## D-138 — The platform updates itself from the console, with a restore point

- **Date**: 13/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Builds on D-032 (the gateway uploads new versions of itself), D-122 and D-123 (releases), D-137 (permissions) · Detailed shape: [`spikes/self-update.md`](spikes/self-update.md)
- **Decision**:
  1. **A release carries the built platform**: `forgeron-platform.json` (the gateway's code, the console, the platform's migrations, the version and commit) and its sha256, attached to the release `v<version>` by the release workflow.
  2. **The console shows the version and what is newer** (Settings › Operations › Install information): the latest release of `UPDATE_REPOSITORY` (default `forgeron-sh/monorepo`), its notes, the migrations it will apply.
  3. **One click updates**, for a holder of the new permission `platform:update`: the gateway downloads the artifact and checks its hash, takes a **Time Travel restore point** of its D1 (no restore point, no update), applies the new migrations, keeps the new code and console, uploads a new version of itself with every binding inherited and deploys it. Each step is recorded and audited; a failure says how to restore.
  4. **Never a downgrade, one update at a time, off on the cloud** (its operator updates it) and when `SELF_UPDATE=off`. The install records `PLATFORM_D1_ID`, so the first update of an install made before this decision is still `forgeron platform install`.
