- **Why**: updating meant a checkout and every install flag repeated; the person who administers a workspace could not even tell which version ran against which release.
- **Rejected**: automatic updates (a one-way migration is a human decision); the new gateway migrating on its first request (a failed migration would leave a running gateway on a half-migrated database); building the platform inside the Worker (it cannot run the build); a signature beyond the release's checksum (the release is as trusted as the npm packages the same workflow publishes).
- **Not done**: rolling back the code from the console (Cloudflare's deployments and the restore command cover it); updating tools.

## D-139 — The admin MCP offers the rest of the console

- **Date**: 13/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Builds on D-079 (the admin MCP), D-092 (installing in two steps), D-137 (permissions)
- **Decision**:
  1. **One tool per route** of the platform contract that an administrator uses and that carries no secret: 58 tools added to the 26 of D-079 (a preview closed, a channel added, a team made, a member removed, an invitation sent, a database read, an audit log read, the platform updated…). A tool's input **is the route's own schema** (path, query and body together), read from `@forgeron/platform-contract` when the gateway starts, so a tool cannot drift from the console; its answer is the route's. `apps/gateway/src/admin-mcp/tools-admin.ts`.
  2. **The same guard, twice**: each tool names the permission its route requires and is offered only to who holds it; the route checks it again with the person's identity and writes its audit line "via Forgeron MCP". A tool that changes something carries the `destructiveHint`.
  3. **Left out, on purpose**: everything that takes or gives a secret (D-079 stays true: no secret passes through Claude), what is not JSON or not an administrator's, and what has a safer way in (the two-step install, D-092). The database explorer's `platform` and `reveal` switches are not offered. A `204` answers `{ ok: true }`, a query count (`limit`, `page`) is a number.
  4. **Closing a preview** is `DELETE /deploy`, which already took a signed-in person who may build tools; the next push of the branch makes a new preview, seeded again.
- **Why**: debugging a deployed install meant clicking through the console or reading logs; an agent that may be given the same rights as an administrator should reach the same routes, no more, with the same trace.
- **Rejected**: a tool that returns a sign-in code (D-049 stores it as a hash: it cannot be read back, and keeping it in the clear would weaken the sign-in); one that reads the Worker's logs (the Workers API for it is not used by any part of the platform yet: to decide before building it); setting the sign-in provider or a secret (they carry a secret); a second, hand-written list of tools (it would drift).
- **Not done**: reading logs; per-tool actions of a person's own library; the CSV export of a table.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/admin-mcp/{kit,tools,tools-admin}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/integration/gateway-admin-mcp.test.ts`; `docs/sdk.md` §17.

## D-140 — The platform's release has no release-please component

- **Date**: 13/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Builds on D-122 (released together), D-138 (the platform artifact)
- **Decision**: in `release-please-config.json` the root path has **no `component`** (`"package-name": ""`, `include-component-in-tag: false`), and `platform` leaves the linked versions' list. Its release is tagged `v<version>`, whatever else is released. `scripts/release-config.test.ts` keeps it so.
- **Why**: a release PR whose body has **one section and no component** is read by release-please as a standalone release: it compares the component of the PR's branch (none) with the one configured for the path. With `"component": "platform"` on the root they differ, so release-please logs `PR component: undefined does not match configured component: platform`, tags nothing, and the PR stays `autorelease: pending`: no `v<version>`, no GitHub release, no `platform-artifact` job (the console then says the release cannot be installed from it), and **every later release PR is refused** (`untagged, merged release PRs outstanding`). A root-only change (a gateway fix) makes exactly that body: it happened with 0.6.1. Replayed against release-please 17.3.0 on the body of a real release PR: the old config gives no release, the new one `v0.7.0`.
- **Rejected**: giving the root a component in its tag (`platform-v0.7.0`): the deploy action of a tool's repository is referenced as `.../actions/deploy@v<version>`, and the root's tag is that; a step that creates the plain tag next to it would be one more thing to break; tagging by hand after each release (what 0.6.1 needed).
- **Not done**: the root is **not** in the linked group (release-please skips a path whose component is empty in a tag-less config: `LinkedVersions.preconfigure`). A release of the packages with no change at the root would give the CLI a version with no `v<version>` tag for the action it writes into new tools' workflows. Today almost every change touches the root (docs, decisions); the day one does not, the root has to be made to follow.
- **Applied in**: `release-please-config.json`; `scripts/release-config.test.ts`; `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## D-141 — A tool's database is created next to the platform's

- **Date**: 13/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Builds on D-029 (self-host resources), D-138 (the platform update)
- **Decision**: when the gateway creates a tool's D1 (a preview, a channel, production) it passes `primary_location_hint`, the region the **platform's own D1** runs in (`running_in_region` of `PLATFORM_D1_ID`, read once, lower-cased, and only if it is one of `wnam`, `enam`, `weur`, `eeur`, `apac`, `oc`). A region that cannot be read, or is not one of those, sends no hint: the creation goes on as before. `@forgeron/hosting`: `d1NearDatabase`, in both self-host and cloud.
- **Why**: without a hint Cloudflare places a new D1 where the API request came from. The tool `notes` was given a database in `ENAM` while the gateway answers from Paris and the platform's D1 is in `WEUR`: each query of the tool crossed the Atlantic (about 100 ms), so `items.add` (three queries) took ~380 ms inside the tool, and a call ~1 s in all. The measure: tail of the gateway and of the tool's Worker on the same calls (CPU 3 to 6 ms in the tool, 30 to 45 ms in the gateway: the time was spent waiting for databases).
- **Rejected**: a fixed region in the install (a self-host in Asia would be wrong, and the platform's D1 already says where the install lives); asking the person (nobody knows what `weur` is); moving a D1 afterwards (Cloudflare cannot).
- **Not done**: a database that already exists stays where it is (a D1 does not move): a preview is closed and deployed again from a new commit (the registry refuses a version it already has), production needs a copy of its data into a new database. **The gateway's own ~550 ms of waiting around each call is not changed**: it queries the platform's D1 several times in sequence (the tool, the person, their rights, the directory, the seat and usage counts, some of them writes it waits for); to measure per step before touching it.
- **Applied in**: `packages/hosting/src/{resources,self-host/{index,cloudflare,fake-cloudflare},cloud/index}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/compose.ts`.

## D-142 — The time of a call is told step by step

- **Date**: 13/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Builds on D-141 (where a tool's database is)
- **Decision**: every answer that came from a tool carries a `Server-Timing` header (`find;dur=12.3, identify;dur=…, seat;dur=…, views;dur=…, forward;dur=…, tool;dur=…, total;dur=…`), made by `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/timing.ts`. `forward` is told apart by what `forwardIdentity` reads (`f.identify`, `f.tool`, `f.library`, `f.access`, `f.secrets`, `f.setup`, `f.manifest`, `f.member`, `f.members`, `f.teams`, `f.connectors`); a step that runs again adds up and is counted (`desc="3 times"`). A call refused before it reaches a tool carries none. Read it in the browser: Network, the call, Timing, Server Timing.
- **Why**: a call of ~1 s with 30 to 60 ms of CPU waits, and nothing said on what. Measured with a tail on the gateway and on the tool's Worker: the tool took ~400 ms (its D1 was an ocean away, D-141, now ~90 ms) and the gateway still adds 600 to 800 ms around it. By reading the code, the gateway resolves the same person three times per call (`canPreview`, the seat count, `forwardIdentity`) and `forwardIdentity` makes about ten reads one after the other: a guess, until this says it.
- **Rejected**: a log line for each call (it would sit in the logs of every install, read by nobody); a sampling profiler (a Worker's clock moves only when it waits, which is what this measures anyway); changing the sequence of reads first (the gain would be a guess).
- **Not done**: what these numbers show is not acted on here: no read is moved, merged or put off. Browsers do not give a page the header of a cross-origin call unless it is exposed; it is read in the developer tools, not by the page.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/{timing,dispatch,user-secrets}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/compose.ts`.

## D-143 — The path of a call reads the registry once, together, and remembers it for seconds

- **Date**: 13/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Builds on D-142 (what the time is spent on); amends how often D-039, D-127 and D-137 read the matrix of access
- **Decision**: on the path of a call to a tool (the dispatcher, the pages, the forwarding):
  1. **A request is resolved once.** `caller.identify` keeps its answer for the request (`WeakMap`): the dispatcher, the preview check, the pages and `forwardIdentity` used to resolve the same person three times.
  2. **The reads that do not depend on each other run together**: the person and their permissions, the revocation of a token, the tool's manifest, access, library rule, secrets and setup, then the directory and the connectors in `forwardIdentity`; the identity is asked while the tool is looked up.
  3. **The registry's reads are remembered by the Worker for seconds** (`dispatch/hot-registry.ts`): 5 s for who the person is and what they may do (`findMember`, `platformPermissionsOf`, `isTokenRevoked`, the library rule, `readUserSetup`), 10 s for the tool, its environments, versions, builds, manifest, access matrix and the directory. A write made through this Worker empties the memory before and after it runs, so whoever makes a change sees it at once; another instance of the Worker shows it within the delay. A failure is not kept, a read that is already running is shared, a person's secrets are never kept, and the console and every route that changes something read through the registry that does not remember.
  4. **A build is read without what a call never uses.** `getBuild` takes `omit` (`worker`, `migrations`, `views`, `changelog`): the Worker's code is hundreds of kilobytes that came from D1 with every manifest. `servedBuild` reads an environment's build in one query instead of three, and `currentManifest` uses it.
  5. **Seats and usage are counted after the answer** (`waitUntil`), and a seat already counted by this Worker for the month is not written again.
  6. **What the database costs is told too**: `Server-Timing` carries `d1` (time waited, added up), `d1.sql` (the SQL's own duration, as D1 reports it) and `d1.where` (the region that served, primary or replica); the pages carry the header as well.
- **Why**: D-142 measured a call at 638 ms, of which the tool's Worker took 95 ms and the gateway 543 ms, with `forward` alone at 340 ms. A read of the platform's D1 costs about 10 ms of road, and a call made some twenty of them one after the other: the cost was the number of trips, not the SQL.
- **Rejected**: putting the person's teams and permissions in the signed token (it cuts the reads on a cold Worker too, but the permissions would then apply only when the token expires, a heavier change to D-039, D-127 and D-137 than seconds of memory, and with the memory it buys little); a Durable Object with SQLite for the hot data (reads without a trip, but a second copy of the registry to keep true: not before this is measured); Smart Placement and D1 read replicas (a switch of the account, not of the code: to be tried against the numbers of `d1.where`, not decided here); a longer memory.
- **Consequences**: a person removed from a team, a revoked token or a tool deployed in another instance of the Worker takes up to 5 s (identity) or 10 s (catalog) to be seen on the path of a call. What is cached is shared by reference: nothing on that path may modify what the registry returned.
- **Not verified**: the gain on a real install. It is read in `Server-Timing` before and after; no figure is announced here.
- **Applied in**: `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/{hot-registry,d1-probe,dispatch,user-secrets,timing}.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/identity/authn/caller.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/compose.ts`; `packages/registry/src/modules/{catalog,access,members,teams}/store.ts`.

## D-144 — A cold call makes fewer trips; what changes is not shared through KV

- **Date**: 13/11/2026 · **Status**: Active · Builds on D-143
- **Decision**: after D-143 the call measured 277 ms (tool 97 ms), with 22 reads of D1 on a cold Worker, 4.3 ms of SQL in 411 ms waited, served by `WEUR primary`: what is left is the road, so what is left is the number of trips on a cold Worker. Now:
  1. **The tool and its environments are read together by name** (`environmentsOfTool`, `dispatchRegistry.find`): one trip instead of two.
  2. **`accessFor` reads the manifest, the grants and the exceptions together** (the manifest only stands in when the tool has no grant of its own): one trip instead of two. `forwardIdentity` asks for it at once, without waiting for the manifest.
  3. **A read a method makes of its own is shared** (the hot registry calls its methods with itself as `this`): the manifest that `forwardIdentity` and `accessFor` both ask for is one read, not two.
  4. **`listTeams` asks the check of the built-in teams and its three reads together**, and reads again only when the check created something (the first time a workspace is read).
- **Why**: each trip is about 13 to 18 ms of road; the SQL is 1 % of the wait.
- **Rejected**: **a shared KV for the registry's reads.** KV is eventually consistent: a value written in one place is seen elsewhere after up to a minute, and its read cache holds at least 30 s. Who a person is, what their team may do, a revoked token, the version an environment serves and the access matrix would then change for a minute in some places and at once in others; a deployment made from the panel would not be seen by the person who tests it right after. What is immutable (a build by its id) is the only thing KV could hold without that cost, and it is a small part of a cold call: a read of D1 of the same size is of the order of the KV read it would replace, with an install to change (a namespace created and bound on `platform install` and on each update, D-138). Not built until a measure says it is worth that. The memory of the Worker (D-143) already shares what is read within seconds.
- **Not done**: Smart Placement and D1 read replicas (account switches, to try against `d1.where`); a timing from inside the tool's Worker (the tool's own reads are 97 ms of which nothing is told; the runtime shares one database handle across requests, so a per-call counter is a change of the runtime, not of the wrapper); a runtime built once instead of once per call for a tool that declares secrets, setup or a directory (CPU, not a trip, not measured).
- **Applied in**: `packages/registry/src/modules/{catalog,access,teams}/store.ts`; `apps/gateway/src/dispatch/{from-registry,hot-registry,user-secrets}.ts`.
